[`FcRefs`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/FcRefs.java) resolves BTW content by `FCBetterThanWolves` field name (null, -1 or EMPTY before FC init; one WARN per name that does not resolve; `item()` never answers AIR), builds the `compat_refs` manifests that datagen ships (`refsManifest` fails runData on an unresolved field) and reads them back (`readManifest`). [`BtwThermalTable`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/thermal/BtwThermalTable.java) holds BTW's heat sources with their lit metadata, clothing, textiles, hot meals and fuels, in tiers that belong to no mod. [`BridgeSelfAudit`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BridgeSelfAudit.java) adds `[SELF-AUDIT/compat-refs]` and `[SELF-AUDIT/thermal]`, each in its own `try/catch`. [`CompatMixinPlugin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/CompatMixinPlugin.java), the plugin of `betterthanwolves.mixins.json`, prepares a mixin on an optional mod's class (`btw.forge.mixin.compat.<mod>`, #028, #031) only when that mod is installed. `Forge/build.gradle`: `compileOnly` APIs (none for HarvestCraft, whose licence forbids redistribution), plain `content { includeGroup }` repositories, the opt-in `-PbtwDevMods=<tokens>` dev runtime (nine tokens, checked at configuration time), the jar bundling and mixin-config guards, and the refmap properties on `runs.data`. `META-INF/mods.toml`: nine optional dependency blocks, all `mandatory=false`. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Mod compatibility (BTWCompat) |
| **Effect** | Without optional mods nothing changes and no `[BTW-Compat]` line is logged. With a mod, BTW logs `[BTW-Compat] <mod> <version> present: integration #0NN enabled` once that integration's entry point has returned; if its setup throws, one ERROR names the mod and BTW continues without that integration. Cold Sweat together with LSO logs one WARN: both simulate body temperature from the same BTW data and their effects stack, and the recommended setup lets Cold Sweat own temperature (`"Temperature Enabled" = false` under `[core]` in LSO's common config). A BTW ID config that moves an id named in shipped compat data is reported at startup: a WARN when the data's mod is loaded, an INFO (`mod absent, data inert`) otherwise |
| **BTW adds** | One description of BTW's own climate content that every temperature integration maps to its own scale: the campfire stages (embers are the unlit campfire at meta 8-11), stoked fire, the hibachi (lit at meta 4-7 and 12-15), the burning brick oven, finite and nether-coal torches, candles, smouldering logs and burning ladders, and loose cobblestone and loose stone brick with lava in their cracks (every odd meta, FC's own warm-block rule, Server FCBlockLavaReceiver.java:99-109; rows added with #030 and #031); the wool, padded, tanned, gimp and plate armour (20 pieces), four textiles, three hot meals, and every BTW fuel with its FC burn time against coal's 1600 ticks. References to FC content survive ParseID edits wherever the other mod accepts runtime data |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — nothing is removed; every integration is optional and can switch only itself off |
| **Data or code?** | Code (no mixin of its own; the plugin only filters the optional mods' mixins), generated `compat_refs` manifests and build-script guards |
| **Alt rejected** | One `@Mod.EventBusSubscriber` class per integration: FML scans them by ASM, and a static handler naming a foreign type loads that type when the mod is absent. Hand-written `block_<id>` / `item_<id>` JSON with no audit, which breaks silently after a ParseID change |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | `exclusiveContent` repositories for the optional-mod mavens: ForgeGradle serves `fg.deobf`'s `<version>_mapped_official_1.20.1` from its own bundled repository, which `exclusiveContent` would shut out, so the Cold Sweat and LSO APIs could not resolve. A denylist bundling guard: it misses JEI's `mezz/`, dtphc2's `maxhyper/`, ss-hc2crops' `darkorg/` and Cold Sweat's default-package classes, so the guard is an allowlist (every class under `btw/` or `net/minecraft/src/`, no nested jar, no `META-INF/jarjar/`) |
| **Known limitation** | The loader's catch covers each entry point only: a handler that calls a foreign API must catch `RuntimeException` and `LinkageError` itself and switch its integration off. The `mods.toml` lower bounds refuse older installs at launch (checked: Dynamic Trees 1.20.1-1.4.9, Cold Sweat 2.3.11 and 2.4-b04a, LSO 1.20.1-2.4.6 and HarvestCraft Crops 1.0.2 are refused). The mixin-config guard checks that every listed entry has a class and every `@Mixin` class is listed, not that a listed class carries `@Mixin` (the first boot would fail instead). The compat-refs audit also runs on the client, where "this server's BTW ID config" means the client's own. `BtwThermalTable.fuels()` walks all 32000 item slots on every call, so its callers compute it once. BTW's older ad-hoc `FCBetterThanWolves` reflection sites (BTWLifecycle, BTWJeiPlugin, RemovedContent and others) are not yet moved onto FcRefs |
| **Verified by** | Build when the foundation landed: `Jar bundling OK (1759 classes, all under btw/ or net/minecraft/src/)` and `Mixin config OK (38 entries, all present, no unlisted @Mixin)`; javap of BTWCompat shows no `invokedynamic` and exactly six compat method references. Dev server, plain BTW: `[SELF-AUDIT/thermal] 13 heat-source rows (12 blocks), 20 clothing, 4 textiles, 3 meals, 39 fuels (nethercoal betterthanwolves:item_481 = 3200); 0 warning(s)` and 6/6 in-world checks of the heat-source metadata and items; with moved ids (a `BTWConfig.txt`) the large campfire resolves at `block_3000` and the wool helm at `item_29256` (2/2). Nine `-PbtwDevMods` token sets booted with 34 BTW mixins applied and no injection error. With the shipped compat data, moved ids are reported as `1 stale (1 warned, 0 inert)` when Serene Seasons is loaded and `2 stale (0 warned, 2 inert)` when the data's mods are absent. Production smoke (release jar on a clean Forge 1.20.1-47.3.0 dedicated server, 2026-10-18): plain BTW logs no `[BTW-Compat]` line, `[SELF-AUDIT/compat-refs] 3 manifest(s) read [sereneseasons, lso, harvestcraft], 43 entries, 0 stale (0 warned, 0 inert)` and `[SELF-AUDIT/thermal] 29 heat-source rows (14 blocks), 20 clothing, 4 textiles, 3 meals, 39 fuels (...); 0 warning(s)`; with every mod installed, five `integration #0NN enabled` lines (AppleSkin is client-only) and exactly one Cold Sweat + LSO WARN |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None by itself |

### #021 — FC hunger and health contract for other mods

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Hunger costs that other mods add (`FoodData.addExhaustion`, `Player.causeFoodExhaustion`) and 1.20.1's own exhaustion sites (jumping, moving, attacking, getting hurt); the `naturalRegeneration` gamerule; fractional player health; drinks (`UseAnim.DRINK`); `Player.canEat` |
| **Added** | MC 1.6 (float health, the `naturalRegeneration` gamerule); the exhaustion API every Forge survival mod uses |
| **Conflict** | `FoodDataMixin` cancels `FoodData.tick`, so exhaustion added through `FoodData.addExhaustion` landed in a field nothing drains: LSO's walking, regeneration and limb-healing costs were free. `PlayerBridge.addExhaustion` skipped FC's armour-weight multiplier and its creative exemption (vanilla/server EntityPlayer.java:1934-1946). 1.20.1's own costs differed from 1.5.2's: measured at 50b0f34, a jump cost 0.25 (FC's 0.2 plus vanilla's 0.05), a sprint jump 1.2, walking 100 m nothing, swimming 100 m 1.0, a melee hit and an armour-respecting hurt 0.1 each, where 1.5.2 charges 0.2, 1.0, 1.0, 1.5 and 0.3. FC's heal ignored `naturalRegeneration`, so a world configured for LSO ran two regeneration clocks. `PlayerBridge` truncated health to an integer every tick: 7.5 HP became 7, and 0.4 HP became 0, which FC then treated as dead. A food-poisoned player could still start a potion, a honey bottle or a modded drink. `Player.canEat` read the vanilla `FoodData` copy, refreshed once per tick (a fake player's copy stays at 20) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC `FoodStats` becomes the hunger backend for every mod), with the 4.B parity fixes of maintainer decision D-A. Nothing is subtracted by default: `naturalRegeneration` defaults to true, so an unconfigured world heals as in 4.B; setting it false stops BTW's heal, which is the rule's vanilla meaning and how exactly one regeneration clock runs beside LSO (#031) |
| **Implementation** | [`FoodDataMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/FoodDataMixin.java): `addExhaustion` (`m_38703_`) HEAD sends a known server owner's cost to `PlayerBridge.addExhaustion` (the owner is cached from `tick` and `eat`, because fake players are not in the player list). [`PlayerBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/PlayerBridge.java): `addExhaustion` runs FC's 1.5.2 rule (armour weight, `disableDamage` exemption, server only); `IsNaturalRegenerationEnabled` reads the gamerule; FC reads health between 0 and 1 as 1 in `syncFromReal`, `heal` and `attackEntityFrom`, and health is written back only when it changed. [`PlayerMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/PlayerMixin.java), for server players: `causeFoodExhaustion` routes through `PlayerBridge`; `@ModifyArg` makes vanilla's charges in `jumpFromGround` and `checkMovementStatistics` 0 (FC's own jump charge stays) and the melee charge in `attack` 0.3; a HEAD inject on `checkMovementStatistics` runs Modern-Common `EntityPlayer.AddMovementExhaustion` (a port of 1.5.2 `addMovementStat`'s exhaustion, vanilla/server EntityPlayer.java:1707-1780, without the statistics; it skips passengers, invulnerable players and moves that round to 0 cm); a `@Redirect` of `DamageSource.getFoodExhaustion` inside `actuallyHurt` gives a `minecraft` damage type 0 when it is tagged `bypasses_armor` and 0.3 otherwise, while other namespaces keep their own value; `canEat` reads FC's `needFood()` (1.5.2 `EntityPlayer.canEat`) unless the food is always edible or the player invulnerable. Modern-Common [`FoodStats`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/FoodStats.java): named thresholds and the heal gated on the gamerule. [`FCDrinkRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/food/FCDrinkRules.java): `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Start`. [`BTWNetwork`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BTWNetwork.java): `PenaltySync` carries FC exhaustion; protocol 5 |
| **Effect** | Any mod's food cost drains BTW's 60-point bar and fat, weighted by FC armour; creative and invulnerable players pay nothing. Moving costs 1.5.2's hunger per metre: walking 0.01, in water 0.015, sprinting 0.1. A jump costs FC's 0.2 (1.0 sprinting) once; a melee hit and an armour-respecting hurt cost 0.3 each, an armour-bypassing hurt nothing. BTW's heal runs only while `naturalRegeneration` is true. Fractional health survives, and FC reads 0 < health < 1 as 1, so a player at 0.4 HP stays alive to FC. No drink can be started while food-poisoned (splash and lingering potions are thrown and stay usable, as in 1.5.2), with FC's refusal feedback. Clients of an older BTW build (network protocol 4) are refused |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's fat-before-hunger and armour-weight rules apply to every modded hunger cost; 1.5.2's `CanDrink` rule (FCItemPotion, FCItemBucketDrinkable) reaches every drink; walking hunger, a core 4.B pressure, is back |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — other mods' costs, the gamerule and every drink item stay; BTW only routes and gates them |
| **Data or code?** | Code: injections in three existing mixin classes, one Forge event, Modern-Common ports |
| **Alt rejected** | Telling admins to switch off other mods' costs, or deactivating their systems (Protocol #8). Per-mod exhaustion hooks, which miss every mod nobody wrote compat for |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | `@Redirect` of the `causeFoodExhaustion` calls: two redirects of one call fail at load, while `@ModifyArg` handlers chain. In `actuallyHurt` a `@ModifyArg` handler cannot see the `DamageSource`, so the `getFoodExhaustion` call is redirected there instead |
| **Known limitation** | The `actuallyHurt` redirect runs under `defaultRequire` 1: another mod redirecting the same call would fail the load (no known mod does). FC's jump charge lacks 1.5.2's ladder exemption (vanilla/server NetServerHandler.java:298-300), so starting a climb costs 0.2. `PlayerBridge.isInsideOfMaterial(water)` tests the feet, not 1.5.2's eyes, so wading takes the water branch (0.015 per metre of 3D movement) and FC's underwater mining penalty. Two 1.20.1 costs reach FC at 1.20.1's amount, to be proved and fixed later: the hunger effect drains 0.005 x (amplifier + 1) per tick (MobEffect.java:65) where 1.5.2 drained 0.025 (vanilla/server Potion.java:149), so FC food poisoning costs a fifth of 4.B's hunger; breaking a block added after 1.5.2 costs 0.005 (Block.java:354) where 1.5.2's `harvestBlock` cost 0.025 (vanilla/server Block.java:1156); since 2026-10-20 that covers the wood and stripped forms of the eight woods (class F), stems, hyphae and modded logs (#034), while the four newer logs are FC logs and cost 0.025, and beds and doors, which 1.20.1 now harvests (#039), keep FC's 0.025 (BlockPlayerDestroyMixin); FU-W5 is the general fix |
| **Verified by** | Refmap emits `m_38703_`, `m_36378_`, `m_6475_` and `m_19377_` (with `m_6135_`, `m_5706_`, `m_36399_` and `m_36391_`). Dev-server proof with a fake player, 55/55 checks: `FoodData.addExhaustion` reaches FC (FC 4, vanilla 0); FC and modded costs carry the armour weight (ratio 1.5454545, the armour modifier); an invulnerable player pays nothing by either route; the cached owner is used after a tick; with the rule off there is no heal (HP 10), with it on HP 11; 7.5 HP stays 7.5, 0.4 HP stays 0.4 while FC reads 1 and the player alive; a potion, milk and honey are refused while poisoned and a potion drinks once cured; jump 0.2, sprint jump 1.0, 100 m walking 1.0, 100 m swimming 1.5, a melee hit 0.3, an armour-respecting hurt 0.3, an armour-bypassing hurt 0 (at 50b0f34: 0.25, 1.2, 0, 1.0, 0.1, 0.1, 0). Not runtime-tested: a real moving player (the proof calls `checkMovementStatistics` directly), the refusal of a protocol-4 client |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Walking costs hunger again, jumps are charged once, and FC's armour weight multiplies every cost, as in 1.5.2 |

### #022 — FC actions visible to other mods

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Forge's contracts for breaking, digging and growing that other mods listen to: `BlockEvent.BreakEvent` and `IForgeItem.onBlockStartBreak` (claims, protection, LSO thirst), `PlayerEvent.BreakSpeed` (mining-speed effects such as Cold Sweat's freezing), `BlockEvent.CropGrowEvent` (seasons), `ToolActions` and tier-correct drops (tools on modern and modded blocks); and modern or modded blocks in front of FC's saw, stump remover and bone meal |
| **Added** | Forge (the events, `ToolActions`, `TierSortingRegistry`) |
| **Conflict** | FC's harvest pipeline must read the block before it is removed, so `ServerPlayerGameModeMixin` cancels `destroyBlock` at HEAD for every block with an FC identity, which also skipped the BreakEvent: claim, protection and thirst handlers never saw an FC harvest, and direct `destroyBlock` callers (machines, fake players, vein-miners) bypassed the spectator and adventure rules. `FCMiningSpeed` never posted BreakSpeed. BTW wheat (block_1089 and its top block_1090) and hemp (block_241) are ProxyBlocks, which TickPolicy never sees, so they fired no CropGrowEvent (#016 covers vanilla-id and twin crops). FC-only tools were plain ProxyItems: no ToolActions, speed 1.0 on modern blocks, and no drops from modern blocks that need a tool (the steel pickaxe and the mattock got nothing from deepslate or its ores). BTW's mining penalty never slowed digging of blocks added after 1.5.2 (`STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (z) caveat). FC's saw, stump remover and bone meal were inert on modern blocks (`ModernBlockStandIn`). The FC pipeline also had no 1.5.2 creative branch and skipped `removeBlock`'s hooks: creative breaks chewed logs, dropped items and wore tools, broken vases did not explode, broken mining charges dropped nothing, and FC animals did not panic |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC behaviour exposed through Forge's own contracts and a hook registry), with the 4.B harvest parity fixes of maintainer decision D-A |
| **Implementation** | [`ServerPlayerGameModeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java) (`destroyBlock` `m_9280_` HEAD): for a block with an FC identity (since 2026-10-20 except the beds and doors 1.20.1 harvests, `VanillaPairDrops.isRuled`, #039: there vanilla `destroyBlock` runs, and FC's animal panic follows at the RETURN of Forge's `removeBlock`, ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java:216-226), `ForgeHooks.onBlockBreakEvent` and the held item's `onBlockStartBreak` in vanilla's order, inside one `try/catch` that refuses the break when another mod's code throws, then the player's game mode handed to FC. Modern-Common [`ItemInWorldManager`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/ItemInWorldManager.java): 1.5.2's creative branch and `removeBlock` (`onBlockHarvested`, removal, `onBlockDestroyedByPlayer`, `NotifyNearbyAnimalsOfPlayerBlockAddOrRemove`; vanilla/server ItemInWorldManager.java:258-280, :421-444). [`FCMiningSpeed`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FCMiningSpeed.java): `relativeHardness` posts BreakSpeed with FC's strength as the original speed, outside the client lock, and scales FC's progress by the result (0 when cancelled, never NaN; a listener that throws leaves FC's progress); `miningModifier` and `fcLogDigProgress` for callers. [`FCGameplayBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FCGameplayBridge.java): BreakSpeed applies BTW's penalty (1.0 down to 0.125) on both sides to every block without an FC identity. [`ProxyItem`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyItem.java): FC tool families get DIG ToolActions (axe `AXE_DIG`; mattock `PICKAXE_DIG` and `SHOVEL_DIG`; pickaxe, shovel, hoe), and on blocks without an FC id a dig speed of max(FC efficiency, base speed) and drops by tag and FC harvest level (0-3 as wood to diamond, 4 and up as netherite). [`FCLogRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FCLogRules.java): FC's log rule as an API (harvest gate, work-stump tool, bark, sawdust, sticks, work-stump state, harvest exhaustion), used by Dynamic Trees (#027) and, since 2026-10-20, by the log rule for post-1.5.2 logs (#034). Load-time [`FCBlockCropsDailyGrowthMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCBlockCropsDailyGrowthMixin.java) and [`FCBlockHempCropMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCBlockHempCropMixin.java) (`@Pseudo`, on FC classes) send each growth roll through [`FcCropGrowthHooks`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/fcext/FcCropGrowthHooks.java). [`ModernBlockHooks`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernBlockHooks.java): integrations register a hook for the states they own, and `ModernBlockStandIn`'s 7-argument `OnBlockSawed`, `GetDoesStumpRemoverWorkOnBlock` and `AttemptToApplyFertilizerTo` ask it (a throw answers false); the stand-in also reads a block's live collision shape when 1.20.1's cached shape is empty, so Dynamic Trees trunk shells reach the saw and the stump remover. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Tick ownership (TickPolicy), dispatch points (FC crop events), and Mod compatibility (BTWCompat), seam ModernBlockHooks |
| **Effect** | With no listener, FC digging and harvesting are bit-identical: the same random stream and the same progress values. A cancelled BreakEvent, or an `onBlockStartBreak` that claims the break, keeps the FC block and resends it; spectators, adventure players without CanDestroy and a sword in creative cannot break FC blocks, also through direct `destroyBlock` callers; another mod's handler that throws refuses the break (logged once per block id). The event fires once per FC destroy call, each log-chew stage included, so a thirst mod charges per hit. BreakSpeed listeners scale FC dig speed; FC axes are axes to other mods; FC tools dig modern blocks at FC speed and get their drops by tier. In creative an FC block is just removed (no drops, conversion or wear); in survival FC's removal hooks run again (a vase explodes, a mining charge drops itself, stairs forward, FC animals panic). BTW wheat and hemp ask `CropGrowEvent.Pre` at FC's growth roll, after FC's own light, weed and hydration conditions passed: DENY fails the roll, ALLOW passes it, and a DENY keeps FC's night reset of the grown-today flag (Common FCBlockCropsDailyGrowth.java:41-49); Post follows each growth, hemp's top at the cell above. BTW's mining penalty slows digging of every block |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's farming, harvesting and tools take part in the modern mod ecosystem, and BTW's machines act on another mod's blocks once its integration registers hooks (Dynamic Trees, #027) |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — the events, ToolActions and 1.20.1's break rules are unchanged; blocks without an FC identity keep vanilla `destroyBlock`, which posts the event itself, so it never fires twice |
| **Data or code?** | Code: two load-time mixins on FC classes, edits to an existing mixin, Forge events, one Modern-Common port |
| **Alt rejected** | Firing CropGrowEvent around the whole `ProxyBlock.randomTick`: a DENY could not separate growth from FC's upkeep (the night reset of the grown-today flag), and an ALLOW could not be honoured. A patch on `FCBlockCrops.AttemptToGrow`: dead code, as its only subclass overrides it and never calls super. Mapping modded logs to legacy 17: FC writes into the world on that id, and Dynamic Trees then collapses the tree |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Folding a mod's mining factor into `FCMiningSpeed`: it reads that mod's internals and helps no other mod. The raw FC efficiency as the dig speed on modern blocks: FC's low-tier values are balanced against FC's bare-hand divisor of 200, while 1.20.1 divides a bare hand by 30 as well, so a wooden chisel would dig slower than a hand |
| **Known limitation** | Post-1.5.2 vanilla logs (acacia, dark oak, mangrove, cherry, crimson and warped stems) did not follow FC's log rule; since 2026-10-20 they do (#034: the four woods as FC wood types 5-8; stems, hyphae and modded logs as class S). A log may flicker for a frame on a chew: the BreakEvent sends the client air before FC's conversion arrives (by code reading). The wheat top (block_1090) grows inside FC's `IncrementGrowthLevel` and fires no Post at its own cell. BreakSpeed carries the block at the position, so a query about a hypothetical state gets the world's. FC hoes count as efficient on unmapped `#mineable/shovel` blocks (FC's dirt flag). The penalty on non-FC blocks syncs the full FC player on every server dig tick. 2026-10-28: deepslate, cobbled deepslate and the deepslate ores have FC identity since #050 (BTW's deep-strata stone, cobblestone and ores), so FC's own harvest rules decide what FC tools get from them; the deepslate rows of Conflict and Verified by describe the state before #050, and tuff and copper ore are examples of the modern blocks that remain |
| **Verified by** | Refmap still maps `destroyBlock` to `m_9280_`. Dev-server proof with a fake player, 7/7 checks plus records: a cancelling BreakEvent keeps stone and an oak log; adventure and spectator keep FC rough stone (block_1027); survival breaks; a bare-hand hit on a log is one chew stage (block_1010, one bark), one BreakEvent per break; creative with a refined axe removes a log with no drops and no wear; a mining charge drops itself (block_217); every FC dig row equals 50b0f34's, now with one BreakSpeed post; a listener scaling by 0.5 halves FC and modern rows, a cancel gives 0; the refined axe digs acacia and stripped oak logs at speed 12 (`axe=true`); the steel pickaxe gets a diamond from deepslate diamond ore, the mattock cobbled deepslate, the iron chisel nothing. Crop events: under a DENY listener 912 Pre events reach BTW crops and all 16 wheat and 16 hemp stay at level 0 (at 50b0f34 they grew); with no listener result every wheat grows once (FC's daily rule); under ALLOW every hemp grows its top, 16 Post events into air; `server.log` names both hooked classes. Production smoke (release jar, clean Forge 1.20.1-47.3.0 dedicated server, 2026-10-18): `fcext.FCBlockCropsDailyGrowthMixin` and `fcext.FCBlockHempCropMixin` mix into FCBlockCropsDailyGrowth and FCBlockHempCrop. Not runtime-tested: BTW's penalty on non-FC blocks (the fake player never ticks, so its penalty level stays 0), the isolation of a throwing BreakEvent or BreakSpeed listener |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | BreakEvent fires once per FC destroy call, log-chew stages included; spectator and adventure rules now apply to FC blocks; creative breaks behave as in 1.5.2 |

### #023 — BTW food bar in Forge's HUD stack

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Forge's right-hand HUD stack (`ForgeGui.rightHeight`) and the overlays that stack on it next to the food row: vanilla's air bubbles and mount hearts, AppleSkin, LSO's thirst and cold-hunger bars, Cold Sweat's gauge |
| **Added** | Forge's GUI overlay API (`RenderGuiOverlayEvent`, the `VanillaGuiOverlay` ids) |
| **Conflict** | BTW cancelled `minecraft:food_level` and drew its pips at a fixed `height - 39` without advancing `rightHeight`, so air bubbles, mount hearts and LSO's thirst bar drew on the pips' row. LSO cancels `FOOD_LEVEL` while Cold Hunger is active and draws a vanilla-scale bar. The pips' hunger shake ran per rendered frame (at 144 fps about 7x FC's rate): the port advanced FC's counter every frame and never reseeded its random |
| **Pattern** | **D** (BTW's food row joins Forge's stack). The layout follows 4.B where FC decided it: the pips stay drawn while mounted (FC always drew the food row; the mount hearts stack above), and the penalty text stacks above the air bubbles instead of hiding (FC hid it only because the row was shared, vanilla/client GuiIngame.java:352) |
| **Implementation** | [`BTWHudOverlay`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/BTWHudOverlay.java): the pips are drawn in the `FOOD_LEVEL` Pre handler (`HIGHEST`, `receiveCanceled = true`) at `height - rightHeight`, then `rightHeight += 10` and the vanilla bar is cancelled; the penalty and gloom text are drawn in the above-all `btw_food` overlay at the top of the stack; `addFoodHudExtension`. [`FoodHudExtension`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FoodHudExtension.java): `renderUnder`, `renderOver` and `foodPipTint` (the first tint other than white wins, in registration order); an extension that throws is removed after one WARN and the render state is reset. [`FCHudRendererBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FCHudRendererBridge.java): FC's counter from `Gui.getGuiTicks()` with the random reseeded every frame (vanilla/client GuiIngame.java:137); `pipY`, `drawFoodPreview` (1/6-shank resolution), `drawFatPreview` and the penalty names for extensions. [`FoodHudProbe`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FoodHudProbe.java): a dev diagnostic (`-Dbtw.hudProbe` or `BTW_HUD_PROBE`) that logs the layout. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Mod compatibility (BTWCompat), seam FoodHudExtension |
| **Effect** | Without other mods the layout is FC's (vanilla/client GuiIngame.java:167-168): pips at H-39, text at H-49. Underwater the bubbles take H-49, FC's row, and the text moves to H-59; mounted, the mount hearts stack above the pips and the text moves up the same way. The selected item's name sits 10 px higher while the stack is taller (underwater, mounted), as with vanilla's own bars. Other mods' bars stack above BTW's food row, and food-row extensions line up with the shaking pips. The hunger shake follows game ticks, as in 1.5.2 |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's 60-point food bar with 1/6-shank pips becomes the food row other mods draw against; AppleSkin's previews (#029) and LSO's frost tint (#031) are extensions of it |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — vanilla's and other mods' overlays are untouched; only BTW's own food row moved into the stack |
| **Data or code?** | Code (client events, no mixin) |
| **Alt rejected** | Moving the vanilla food canceller to `LOWEST` so that other mods' food-row handlers fire: they would draw a vanilla bar that is never shown, and the Post event never fires while the overlay is cancelled |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Vanilla's rule of hiding the food row on a living mount, and FC's rule of hiding the penalty text while the air gauge shows: both drop information 4.B showed, and the stack gives each its own row |
| **Known limitation** | The pips depend on the `food_level` Pre event reaching BTW's listener: another mod's earlier `HIGHEST` listener that throws skips them for that frame. An extension that throws an Error other than `LinkageError` crashes the client instead of being removed. The text needs `canHurtPlayer` and the row `shouldDrawSurvivalElements`, so a camera mod's non-player camera outside spectator would show the text without the row. An extension that leaves a shader colour set, or returns a tint with alpha 0, fades or hides the pips. The 20-frame shake still starts on a food-level drop, where FC started it on any added exhaustion. GUI scale 3 is not checked |
| **Verified by** | Client run with the probe at GUI scale 2, all eight states as expected: H1 pips H-39, text H-49; creative hides the row; underwater text H-59 with the bubbles shown; mounted, pips drawn and text H-59; the penalty names Peckish to Dying at H-49 under the hunger effect; fed, the text clears. A throwing extension is removed after exactly one WARN (`[BTWHud] food HUD extension btw.forge.client.FoodHudProbe$ThrowingExtension threw; removed`, in the AppleSkin throw run of #029). Dedicated-server boots load no client class |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None |

### #024 — FC food values for every food

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Forge's stack-sensitive `Item.getFoodProperties(ItemStack, LivingEntity)`, foods added after 1.5.2 and foods from other mods, and food stack sizes |
| **Added** | Forge's stack-sensitive food query; the post-1.5.2 foods in BTW's shipped table are MC 1.7 – 1.17 |
| **Conflict** | FC-only foods (ProxyItems) had no `FoodProperties`, so food mods could not see them. Post-1.5.2 and modded foods fed 1.20.1 nutrition x 3 with no FC food poisoning, and their headless FC items were plain items, so the cauldron, dung spoiling and animal fodder ignored them. Raw meat lost its poisoning once crafted into a modded meal. Foods stacked to 64, not BTW's 16 (CHANGELOG known issue) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (one resolver for every food, values as data). The post-1.5.2 vanilla foods take their nearest FC relative's values, under the post-1.5.2 ruling (#018) |
| **Implementation** | [`FcFoodValues`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/food/FcFoodValues.java), resolved in this order: `FC_ITEM` (the FC ItemFood's own values and effects, the enchanted golden apple's three included), `FC_DRINKABLE` (milk 6, chocolate milk 9, used as FC feeds them, Common FCItemBucketDrinkable.java:73, no x 3), `TABLE` (a `btw_foods` entry: hunger x 3 with its fat and poison), `MODERN_CONVERTED` (1.20.1 nutrition x 3, saturation as fat, effects mapped back to FC potion ids); `toFoodProperties` never sets `fast()`; `foodStackLimit`. [`BtwFoodTable`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/food/BtwFoodTable.java): `data/<ns>/btw_foods/*.json` with optional Forge `conditions`, item and tag entries, `"poison": true` as FC's standard poisoning, fodder values and `wolf_meat` (and per-namespace `tiers`, #032). [`BtwFoodEvents`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/food/BtwFoodEvents.java): reload, tag binding, sync to clients, and table or raw-crafted poisoning on `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Finish`. [`FoodDataMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/FoodDataMixin.java): `TABLE` foods feed their FC value. `ProxyItem.getFoodProperties(stack, entity)` answers with the FC value while `isEdible()` stays false. [`ModernFoodItem`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/food/ModernFoodItem.java) / [`ModernFodderItem`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/food/ModernFodderItem.java): a headless modern food or fodder is an FC food or fodder item. [`ItemStackMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ItemStackMixin.java): `getMaxStackSize` (`m_41741_`) RETURN, lowering only. `BTWNetwork.FoodTableSync`. Shipped data: `data/betterthanwolves/btw_foods/minecraft.json` (18 entries). Format: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Food values (BtwFoodTable, FcFoodValues) |
| **Effect** | Every BTW system asks one resolver. FC-only foods answer Forge's food query with their FC value in 1.20.1 units (nutrition ceil(FC hunger / 3)), so food mods can read them, while no vanilla eat path opens; foods on a 1.20.1 item keep their own `FoodProperties` (AppleSkin shows their FC values through #029). Post-1.5.2 vanilla foods feed by the table, in 1.5.2 units (x 3 on BTW's bar): raw mutton and rabbit 3 with FC poisoning, cooked 4; salmon, tropical fish and pufferfish 3 with poisoning, cooked salmon 4; rabbit stew 10; beetroot soup and suspicious stew 3; beetroot, sweet berries, glow berries, chorus fruit and dried kelp 1; honey bottle 2 at fat 0.5; mutton, rabbit and rabbit stew are wolf food, beetroot is pig fodder and beetroot seeds are chicken fodder. Other modern or modded foods feed 1.20.1 nutrition x 3. Headless modern foods count as FC foods for the cauldron, dung spoiling and animal fodder. A crafted food made from a poisonous raw ingredient keeps FC's poisoning (#025). Foods stack to 16; an FC food keeps its own FC limit (BTW's cured meat 32, Common FCItemFoodCured.java:27); a limit is only ever lowered |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's 60-point hunger, overflow-only fat, food-poisoning odds and husbandry fodder apply to the foods of every mod |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — every food item and recipe stays; the table is data that a pack can override file by file |
| **Data or code?** | Both: the `btw_foods` data table, plus code |
| **Alt rejected** | A global mixin that makes vanilla `FoodProperties` report FC values: it would change what `Item.use`, `FoodData.eat` and `addEatEffect` feed for every mod and clash with the double-feed guard. Mixins into other mods' food registries (Protocol #4, licences) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | A flat 16 for every food, which would shrink BTW's cured meat from 32. Multiplying the drinkable buckets by 3: FC feeds their values already adjusted |
| **Known limitation** | A 1.20.1 or modded food without a table entry is not capped: it feeds nutrition x 3 (HarvestCraft's foods are banded by `tiers`, #032). An explicit `"poison": false` does not stop FC's raw-crafted poisoning. `FC_DRINKABLE` reports the buckets as not always edible, although FC's `CanDrink` has no hunger gate (to be proved first). A headless item's class (food, fodder or plain) is fixed when its id is created, so a `/reload` that gives a non-edible item a table value applies from the next world load. On a dedicated client a non-edible item with a table hunger value is uncapped until `FoodTableSync` arrives; a table too large for one payload fails to sync (one WARN), and only such items then differ |
| **Verified by** | Refmap emits `m_41741_`. Dev-server proof with a fake player, 55/55 checks (with #021): bread `FC_ITEM` 9, milk `FC_DRINKABLE` 6, chocolate milk 9, pumpkin seeds 1, the donut's fat 0.5, the enchanted golden apple Regeneration IV for 600 ticks plus Resistance and Fire Resistance for 6000, the golden apple Regeneration I for 100 ticks, no value for a potion or a stick, cooked mutton `TABLE`; stack limits bread, pumpkin seeds and chorus fruit 16, cured meat 32, mushroom stew and milk bucket 1, stone 64; the headless cooked mutton is a `ModernFoodItem` capped at 16, netherite stays a plain item, beetroot seeds are a `ModernFodderItem`; eating bread +9, the donut at full +0.5 fat, cooked mutton +12, milk +6; raw beef poisoned 11 of 40 eats, raw mutton 16 of 40, bread 0 of 20. The donut (an FC-only item) answers nutrition 1, not fast, `isEdible` false; pumpkin seeds (a 1.20.1 item) answer null |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Foods stack to 16 as in 4.B, fixing the known issue; post-1.5.2 foods feed at their FC relatives' values |

### #025 — Recipe bridge for other mods' recipes

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | JSON crafting, smelting, smoking and campfire recipes of 1.20.1 and of other mods, held in the 1.20.1 `RecipeManager` |
| **Added** | MC 1.12 (data-driven recipes) |
| **Conflict** | The FC workbench, work stump and soulforge are the port's only 3x3 and 4x4 grids, and they ask FC's own `CraftingManager` alone (the soulforge also FC's anvil list). So 499 of 822 1.20.1 crafting recipes, every one that needs 3x3, and every modded 3x3 recipe could not be crafted anywhere: the 1.5.2 recipes 4.B kept (gold, diamond, emerald and lapis blocks, glass panes, bottles, flower pots) and the lantern #019 had widened included. Modded raw foods cooked only in the vanilla furnace and smoker, outside BTW's heat machines; modded grains and fibres had no millstone recipe; a mod's recipe could be rerouted through BTW's chains only by data-pack order, which Forge does not keep when a mod is added to an existing world. The 6-stick fence record of FC's removals matched no 1.20.1 recipe, so `minecraft:oak_fence` stayed craftable |
| **Pattern** | **B** (other mods' recipes route through BTW's own stations; FC always wins) with **D** for the machine imports. Maintainer decision D-B (a): the fallback covers every recipe except FC's injected copies, FC first, and a removed id passes its ingredient role to its BTW successor (#019): the furnace to the brick oven, the tripwire hook to one FC iron nugget |
| **Implementation** | Modern-Common [`CraftingManager`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/CraftingManager.java) (`ExternalResolver` / `ExternalCraft`; `findMatchingRecipe` asks the resolver only when FC's own list has no match), [`SlotCrafting`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/SlotCrafting.java) (an external craft's crafted hooks, remainders and consumption) and `InventoryCrafting.getInventoryWidth`. [`WorkbenchFallback`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/WorkbenchFallback.java), server only: candidates are every CRAFTING recipe except `betterthanwolves:fc_*`, sorted by id and cached per RecipeManager and generation; it declines in the soulforge whenever the anvil list matches, and retries once with the modern spellings of the same 1.5.2 items (`RecipeBridge.legacyAlias`: one-way, built from `LegacyVariants.baseSubtype`, RecipeBridge.java:309, :320; the 4.B item stands in for its modern spelling, never the reverse, which since 2026-10-20 means the ink sac, cocoa beans, lapis and bone meal for black, brown, blue and white dye, each of which keeps its own FC stack, #038, and the stone slab and smooth sandstone). [`RecipeBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/RecipeBridge.java) runs the steps at ServerStarting and after `/reload`, each isolated (a step that edits the map restores it on failure), and logs one `[BTW-Recipes]` summary. [`FcRecipeRemovals`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcRecipeRemovals.java): a record with no ingredient match whose result FC makes its own way removes the `minecraft:` crafting recipes with that result. [`RemovedContent`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/removed/RemovedContent.java) and `RetiredIngredientWidening`: ingredient successors on every tier. [`RecipeReplacements`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/RecipeReplacements.java) (`data/<ns>/btw_recipe_replacements`), [`ModernCookingImport`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/ModernCookingImport.java), [`TagMachineRecipes`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/TagMachineRecipes.java) (`data/<ns>/btw_machine_recipes`), [`RawCraftedFoods`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/RawCraftedFoods.java), [`FcCraftingManagers`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/FcCraftingManagers.java) (reflective access to FC's anvil, campfire and millstone managers). Since 2026-10-28 [`FCCraftingRecipes`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FCCraftingRecipes.java)`.Shaped` / `Shapeless.matches` also ask FC's own recipe on the FC reading of the grid (`fcMatches`, FCCraftingRecipes.java:320-321: square grids only, after the item-level test), so the 2x2 grid and any 1.20.1 crafting container match FC's recipes by id and damage, as the FC grids and 1.5.2's ContainerPlayer do (#051). Hooks and formats: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Recipe bridge (RecipeBridge, WorkbenchFallback) |
| **Effect** | At the FC grids FC's recipes win, then the soulforge's anvil list, then every other crafting recipe, with its own remainders (a honey bottle leaves its glass bottle) and the 1.20.1 crafted hooks. Where 1.20.1 and 1.5.2 differ, 1.20.1's recipe applies (the golden apple costs 8 ingots; the enchanted golden apple has no recipe). Ink sacs, cocoa beans, lapis and bone meal again stand in for black, brown, blue and white dye in dye recipes at the FC grids, as in 1.5.2's RecipesDyes. Since 2026-10-20 FC grids give real white dye for `white_dye` (from bone meal) and `white_dye_from_lily_of_the_valley`, blue dye from a cornflower and black dye from a wither rose, where they gave bone meal, lapis and ink sacs (#017, #038). The blast furnace, smoker and furnace minecart take the brick oven and the crossbow one FC iron nugget; the furnace minecart gains a route 4.B lacked (brick oven + minecart, also in the 2x2 grid). Replacements by id apply after every pack has loaded and before 4.B's removals, so a replacement cannot bring back a removed recipe, never adds a recipe, and a removal needs a reason. Edible inputs with no FC id of one-ingredient smelting, smoking and campfire recipes cook in FC's furnace, brick oven and campfire spit (and in the cauldron, which takes FC foods only); an item tag feeds the millstone for its members with no FC id; a crafted food made from a poisonous raw item keeps FC's poisoning (#024). Recipe ids stay; BTW's own uses (HarvestCraft's reroutes) are listed in #032 |
| **BTW adds** | Modded content goes through BTW's own stations and chains: the workbench and soulforge, the millstone, the cauldron, the brick oven and the campfire spit |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — every recipe id stays; a replacement keeps its id and states why, and a removal must give a reason |
| **Data or code?** | Both: the `btw_recipe_replacements` and `btw_machine_recipes` data formats, plus code |
| **Alt rejected** | Limiting the fallback to namespaces other than `minecraft` and `betterthanwolves`, the earlier filter: it existed only while 4.B's removals were missing from the RecipeManager, and it left every vanilla 3x3 recipe uncraftable, the ones 4.B kept included. Per-id JSON routes for the four stranded vanilla recipes only: modded recipes that need a furnace or a hook would stay stranded. Overriding recipes by data-pack order, which is not deterministic for existing worlds |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Installing the resolver on the client: the client's FC containers mirror the server's slots. Running the millstone import at `TagsUpdatedEvent`: that comes before the world's headless ids are restored, which would key the recipes to evicted ids |
| **Known limitation** | External recipes ignore FC's per-item crafting rules (`OnUsedInCrafting`, consumed-in-crafting, crafting damage, container items): an FC tool in such a recipe is consumed by the recipe's own remainder rules, as in the 2x2 grid. `onCraftedBy` and `ItemCraftedEvent` see a converted copy, so a listener's edits to the crafted stack do not reach the player. 19 of the 708 vanilla shaped and shapeless recipes the proof sweeps stay uncraftable (the 2026-10-28 census; 27 of 708 on 2026-10-20, and the C1 sweep counted 34 of 712, including dye results that read back as ink sac, lapis, cocoa or bone meal) because 1.20.1 split one 1.5.2 item into several: the 16 dyed beds (one bed), and the stone slab and the smooth sandstone slab and stairs. The seven flower dyes and the flower banner pattern craft since #045 (every flower keeps its kind), which also keeps the red tulip's red dye recipe (+1 recipe), and the bamboo block -> planks recipe goes (#052, -1 recipe). The recipe book and JEI place ingredients by item; the server's result is FC's (the exact match above). A replacement aimed at an FC-injected id changes only the 2x2 grid and JEI, not FC's own list. Another mod's later `replaceRecipes` is not seen until the next injection. Every edible unmapped smelting input joins the campfire spit too (chorus fruit included). Imported cooking and millstone recipes live on the server, so JEI on a dedicated-server client does not list them. 1.20.1's iron nugget and FC's iron nugget are still separate items |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proof, 37/37 checks, with test packs added at launch and mid-run: `minecraft:oak_fence` removed; the FC workbench crafts a gold block, a glass pane, a lantern (1.20.1 nuggets around a nether-coal torch) and a chain, and the soulforge a vanilla gold block; a honey bottle leaves its bottle; no vanilla torch, crafting table or furnace; FC's fence from planks and the axe's log chopping kept; sweeps over FC's crafting and anvil lists find no override; the blast furnace, smoker, crossbow and furnace minecart craft, the furnace still does not; replacements with a placeholder, a removal with a reason, a removal without one rejected, a false condition not applied, an unresolved placeholder keeping the original; mutton cooks in the furnace and on the campfire, salmon in the furnace, potatoes and inedible kelp are not imported, FC's own campfire recipe stays; an unmapped tag member mills and a mapped one keeps FC's recipe; after `/reload` no duplicate smelting recipe, a replacement beats a later pack, and cooking, milling and the fallback cache still work. A sweep of every `minecraft` shaped and shapeless crafting recipe that fits 3x3, each built from its first listed items: 664 of 712 craft at the FC workbench, 14 through FC's own recipe, 34 fail as listed above. Production smoke (release jar, clean Forge 1.20.1-47.3.0 dedicated server, 2026-10-18): `FC vanilla-recipe removals on: 94/94 records matched (3 count-insensitive, 1 result-only, 0 unmatched)`, 131 recipes removed, 17 widened, `[BTW-Recipes] 1735 recipes installed ... FC workbench fallback on`. 2026-10-20 census after #038 (scratchpad fu_runs/INV/BDD_after2/P-RV.txt, the same on the stage-C tree): 708 recipes, 663 craft at the FC workbench, 18 through FC's own recipe, 27 fail as listed above (32 before #038, fu_runs/INV/cur/P-RV.txt); `sweep fc total=675 skipped=3 fail=0` (663 before the dye twins). 2026-10-28 census (round 3; scratchpad fu_runs/R3/W7/P-RV.txt): 708 recipes, 669 craft at the FC workbench, 20 through FC's own recipe, 19 fail as listed above (16 `dye_<colour>_bed`, `smooth_sandstone_slab`, `smooth_sandstone_stairs`, `stone_slab`); `sweep fc total=707 skipped=3 fail=0` (FC's 32 sub-block recipes, #051). The exact 2x2 match: P-W1 rows M3-M11 (two acacia or spruce sidings make their own wood's planks and nothing else, three cherry mouldings cherry stairs; an ink sac and a red FC candle, or lapis and red FC wool, make nothing, where the white one makes the colour) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | The FC workbench crafts what 4.B's crafting table crafted (gold blocks, glass panes, bottles, flower pots), and modded content enters BTW's processing chains |

### #026 — BTW soil and growth rules for modded plants

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Modded crops, saplings and wild plants on BTW soil, and Forge's plant contract: `CropGrowEvent` (fired by `CropBlock.randomTick`), `IForgeBlock.isFertile`, `BonemealEvent`, bee pollination, block loot |
| **Added** | Forge (the plant contract) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | The 2026-10-15 ruling of #018 applies to modded plants: "Each plant takes the rules that make sense for its lore: the nearest 1.5.2/FC relative's rules, adapted." Settled under it: the crop rule is the root crop's (D1 (b)), modded saplings are sapling twins (D2 (b)), and unripe crops drop nothing, twin crops included (D3 (b)) |
| **Conflict** | Modded crops can be planted on BTW farmland and planters (PlantSoilMixin, #013), but there they grew by their own rules: no weeds, dry soil worked, no End ban, a bee advanced a stage per visit, bone meal grew them instantly, unripe crops dropped produce, and harvesting left FC farmland tilled. FC's soil saw them as air, so no weeds grew under them. `ProxyBlock` answered Forge's `isFertile` with false, so FC farmland never counted as moist to 1.20.1 growth-speed code. Modded saplings (HarvestCraft's 50 fruit-tree saplings) grew at 1.20.1's pace, also in the End |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): BTW's soil rules reach modded plants through Forge's events, a plant stand-in, a loot modifier and the sapling twin. Rebalance: none beyond 4.B; bone meal is FC fertilizer by the #017 ruling |
| **Implementation** | [`FcSoil`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/farming/FcSoil.java): the governed plants, a CropBlock, a sapling (`SaplingBlock` or `#minecraft:saplings`) or a `#betterthanwolves:fc_soil_plants` member with no FC id and no twin, standing on BTW soil that takes its kind. [`ModdedPlantRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/farming/ModdedPlantRules.java): `CropGrowEvent.Pre` at LOW priority, acting only on a DEFAULT result; Post tells the soil when a crop reaches full age; the weed pull on right-click (not for spectators); `data/<ns>/btw_plant_rules/*.json`; the `[MODDED-PLANTS]` audit. [`ModernPlantStandIn`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernPlantStandIn.java): FC's view of a governed modded crop (weeds, grazing, fertilizer relay, legacy-soil conversion), one free legacy id allocated by `ModernBlockStandIn` only when the pack has a modded crop. [`ProxyBlock`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyBlock.java): FC farmland untills when a governed modded crop above is removed; `isFertile` answers FC's hydration, read at the soil cell whichever cell the caller passes (Forge's `CropBlock.getGrowthSpeed` passes the cell above, CropBlock.java:102-105); the weed particles on the client. [`FCImmatureCropLootModifier`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/data/FCImmatureCropLootModifier.java) (`betterthanwolves:immature_crop`, instance `immature_crops_drop_nothing`). The positional `BoneMealPolicy.route`, and the [`BonemealableGateMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BonemealableGateMixin.java) and [`BeeGrowCropGoalMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BeeGrowCropGoalMixin.java) gates. `TwinRegistry.isFallbackSapling` (#018). Hooks and formats: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Modded plants (FcSoil, ModdedPlantRules) |
| **Effect** | A modded crop on BTW soil that takes it grows by FC's root-crop rule, the one beetroot's twin runs: not in the End, not while the soil has weeds, light of 9 or more above (torchlight counts; sky light follows the time of day), hydrated soil, then a roll of 0.1 (Common FCBlockCarrot.java:30-33) x the soil's multiplier (fertilized soil doubles it) x maxAge / 7, so ripening takes FC's seven levels whatever the crop's age count. Another mod's ALLOW or DENY (a season mod) stands. A crop reaching full age unfertilizes its soil (1088 to 1087, planters too). Weeds grow under modded crops, and a right-click pulls them before the held item is used. Removing the crop untills FC farmland to loose dirt. An unripe CropBlock with no FC id on BTW soil, or on FC loose dirt, drops nothing (the twin beetroot and torchflower crops included; the pitcher crop is not a CropBlock). Bone meal on a governed plant fertilizes the FC ground below (used up only when it takes it, the plant never grows); on FC grass or sand it is kept. Each bee visit is one growth roll. Every SaplingBlock with STAGE and no legacy id grows by FC's sapling rule (light 9, four stages at 1/64, not in the End) into the mod's own tree. Plants on modern or modded soils keep 1.20.1's rules |
| **BTW adds** | Every modded crop joins BTW's loop of tilling, watering, fertilizing and weeding, and BTW planters take them; modded saplings grow at BTW's forestry pace |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — modded blocks, items, placement and mature loot stay; only the timing, the conditions and the unripe loot are BTW's, and the base chance is data |
| **Data or code?** | Code (Forge events, a loot modifier, one stand-in) and data (`btw_plant_rules`: `modded_crop_base_chance` 0.1; the `#betterthanwolves:fc_soil_plants` tag, which HarvestCraft's data fills, #032) |
| **Alt rejected** | Mixins into other mods' crop classes (Protocol #4). Replacing modded crops with FC ProxyBlock crops (Protocol #5; it would also break `sereneseasonsphc2crops` and `dtphc2`). The natural-light-11 rule of `FCBlockCrops.AttemptToGrow`, which is dead code in 4.B |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Thinning modded saplings through `SaplingGrowTreeEvent` at FC's mean chance: a mean pace without FC's stages, light rule or End ban, which the ruling asks for; the sapling twin runs FC's own rule. Excluding twin crops from the loot rule: their nearest FC relative, the carrot, drops nothing unripe |
| **Known limitation** | The client still predicts a modded seed's placement on FC farmland by 1.20.1's rule (#013). A modded SaplingBlock that overrides its random tick (a Nether or End sapling) follows FC's sapling rule instead of its own conditions. `isFertile` answers FC's hydration on every ProxyBlock soil on the server, so any mod that reads it sees FC's answer. The plant stand-in takes one id from the shared free range, leaving three flammable stand-ins (#014); two since log2 took 165 on 2026-10-20 (#034) |
| **Verified by** | Plain BTW: `[MODDED-PLANTS] crops=0 saplings=0 modded-saplings-on-FC-rule=0 fc_soil_plants=0 plant stand-in=none base=0.1`, the twin audit unchanged, the T2 bone-meal set unchanged (88/92, the same four misses) and weeds 6/6. With HarvestCraft, 17/17 checks: tomatoes on wet FC farmland grow by day, not at night and not on dry farmland; fertilized soil reverts when the crop ripens; an unripe tomato drops nothing and its soil untills to loose dirt, a ripe one drops tomatoes, off BTW soil HarvestCraft's loot stays; bone meal is used once, the soil takes it, the crop does not grow, and a second use is kept; on an HC sapling it is kept and the sapling unchanged; HC saplings grow at FC's sapling pace and none in the End; `[MODDED-PLANTS] crops=97 saplings=0 modded-saplings-on-FC-rule=50 fc_soil_plants=6 plant stand-in=162 base=0.1`. With LSO: bone meal on a sun fern on FC grass (12 uses) and a water plant on FC sand is kept and their age unchanged; unripe, both still drop their seeds. Not runtime-tested: torchlit growth, the End ban for modded crops, weeds and the weed pull under a modded crop, the client weed cue and swing prediction |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None in plain BTW; packs with farming mods keep BTW's farming loop |

### #027 — Dynamic Trees

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Dynamic Trees `dynamictrees`, optional (`mods.toml` accepts `[1.20.1-1.4.10,)`): trees that grow branch by branch on rooty soil (branches of radius 1-8; thick trunks of radius 9-24 add trunk shells around their core), rooty-soil fertility 0-15 and a bone-meal growth pulse on the tree, felling of everything above a cut as a falling-tree entity one level tick after the break (DT's FutureBreak, DT 1.4.10 BranchBlock.java:618-624), axe stripping, seeds that plant `DynamicSaplingBlock`s (which are not 1.20.1 SaplingBlocks), Nether fungus species, and apples and cocoa pods as fruit blocks. The dtphc2 add-on builds HarvestCraft species and fruit on the same API |
| **Added** | Third-party mod. Built against 1.20.1-1.4.10 (the newest build on the author's maven, with sources); 1.20.1-1.4.11 (Modrinth, 2026-06-10) has the same API by bytecode comparison |
| **Decisions** | dt D1 (Fortune on a DT axe fell), settled by the maintainer on 2026-10-17: option (b), keep DT's bonus logs, while FC hunger and wear count only the real (pre-Fortune) logs. dt D2 (DT leaf loot): settled 2026-10-20 by the general leaf rule (#035): DT leaves follow BTW's leaf rule; a fruit species gives no seed; orphan leaves give the family's common seed; DT's seasonal seed chance is a veto; no Fortune bonus. Compat-wave decisions #6 (no logs without an axe, wear exactly 1 per log, the saw at radius 8 or more, the remover and chisel on dead stumps only) and #7 (modded fruit is kept). The maintainer's standing rulings apply: bone meal is 4.B fertilizer (#017), post-1.5.2 plants grow at 4.B speeds by their lore (#018), integrate, don't disable (Protocol #8). Settled 2026-10-17/18: DT's Nether fungi take BTW's 1-in-50 growth, as vanilla fungi do; DT saplings honour a tree pack's `sapling_grows_naturally false` |
| **Conflict** | DT blocks have no legacy id, so none of FCBlockLog's rules reached them. Measured on HEAD 3985147 (the `dt_p1`-`dt_p8` baselines): any hit, bare hands included, felled the whole tree with its logs; an axe fell cost no FC hunger and 4 durability on a radius-8 cut (vanilla's 1 plus 3 from DT's default `axeDamageMode` THICKNESS), charged one level tick later to whatever was then in the main hand (DT BranchBlock.java:597-613); there was no stump, bark, sawdust or chew stage, and digging the rooty soil deleted a living tree with no drops; FC's saw and stump remover did nothing; bone meal on a branch, rooty soil or trunk shell ran DT's growth pulse and was used up even at fertility 15, from either hand and from machines, because ServerPlayerGameModeMixin leaves bone meal to 1.20.1's order and DT's own `use()` runs first; a DT sapling grew on its first random tick, at night, in the dark and in the End (DT DynamicSaplingBlock.java:80-85 has no light, End or pace gate), and bone meal grew one on soil FC refuses for saplings (mycelium); DT fruit and pods ripened at DT's own pace (0.2 per random tick) and in the End. DT's Nether fungi never grew from random ticks: DT NetherFungusSpecies.java:63 sets `canSaplingGrowNaturally` false and DynamicSaplingBlock.java:82 checks it, the same as vanilla 1.20.1 fungi, which grow only by bone meal |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's own log, stump, saw, fertilizer, sapling and mushroom rules act on DT's blocks through DT's API and Forge events, reading FC's data rather than copies of it. **B** (gate): DT's whole-tree harvest needs an FC-harvesting axe; every other hit chews the branch as FC chews a log. What it takes away is 4.B's own rule: bare-hand felling (FC's log material needs a tool) and DT's bone-meal growth pulse (bone meal is fertilizer only, #017). Rebalance: none, 4.B parity, except D1 |
| **Implementation** | [`btw.forge.compat.dynamictrees`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DynamicTreesCompat.java), every DT reference in one package, with no Forge mod or automatic bus-subscriber annotation. [`DynamicTreesCompat`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DynamicTreesCompat.java)`.init` is the only entry, called from [`BTWCompat`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/BTWCompat.java) (:106-114) inside its own try/catch. It registers the leaf drop hook first (`LeafDrops.register` with [`DTLeafDrops`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTLeafDrops.java), Minecraft types only, answering KEEP until the integration is active; added 2026-10-20 with #035), then runs `DTTrees.probe()` and `DTLeafDrops.probe()`, which resolve every DT member the integration calls by reflection with its exact types (a DT override of a vanilla method is checked as that vanilla supertype, because release DT jars carry it only under its SRG name), and registers nothing else if one is missing. Any handler failure switches the integration off with one ERROR, and DT then keeps its own rules. [`DTHarvestHandler`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTHarvestHandler.java) (`BreakEvent` and `BreakSpeed` at LOWEST, the axe-strip `RightClickBlock`, the falling tree's `EntityJoinLevelEvent`, ServerTickEvent END for wear), [`DTGrowthHandler`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTGrowthHandler.java) (the `TransitionSaplingToTreeEvent` guard and the bone-meal `RightClickBlock`), [`DTBlockHooks`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTBlockHooks.java) (a `ModernBlockHooks.Hook`: saw, stump remover, fertilizer), [`DTSaplingTwin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTSaplingTwin.java) (a `TwinRegistry.ModdedSapling`) and [`DTTrees`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTTrees.java) (logs, stumps, dead stumps, shells). Shared seams: [`TwinRegistry`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/TwinRegistry.java)`.ModdedSapling` (`isSapling`, default `isFungus`, `growTree`) and `registerModdedSapling` (:330-359): a registered sapling block that is not a SaplingBlock is a SAPLING twin (`isFallbackSapling`, :302-308), or a FUNGUS twin when `isFungus` answers true (`familyFor`, :315-321), and FC's rule calls `growTree` from `growSaplingTwin` (:574-579) and from `fungusTick` after its 1-in-50 roll (:614-620); with nothing registered the check is one empty-list test, so plain BTW and HarvestCraft saplings are unchanged. One [`BoneMealPolicy`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BoneMealPolicy.java)`.route` clause (:114-115): a modern block with a registered FC machine hook is FC_TARGET. [`ModernBlockStandIn`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernBlockStandIn.java)`.idFor` asks a non-dynamic block for its live collision shape when MC's cached one is empty (:139-143): MC builds that cache in an empty world, where a DT trunk shell, whose shape follows its core, is empty, so shells read as air to FC and never reached the saw or the stump remover. Consumes `FCLogRules` (since 2026-10-20 the shared log rule of #034: DT families pay bark and take work stumps by FC wood type, `FCLogRules.fcWoodMeta` answering `ProxyRegistry.getWoodType`, and the log rule's own machine hook registers after BTWCompat, BTWForgeMod.java:66-67, so DT's hook keeps DT's blocks), `FCMiningSpeed.fcLogDigProgress`, `ModernBlockHooks` and ProxyItem's `AXE_DIG` for FC axes (#022), the modded-plant rules (#026), `SeasonGate` (#028), `BoneMealPolicy` (#017) and the twins (#018). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Mod compatibility (BTWCompat), seams ModernBlockHooks and TwinRegistry.ModdedSapling |
| **Effect** | Chewing: a survival hit on a DT branch without an FC-harvesting axe (`FCLogRules.canHarvestLog`) chews it as FCBlockLogDamaged.ConvertBlock does (Server FCBlockLogDamaged.java:164-207): hit 1 strips the branch and ejects 1 bark toward the hit face, each following hit takes one radius and gives a stick, sawdust, then a stick, and the last hit (radius 4 or less) gives sawdust and cuts through; the falling tree then drops no logs (its sticks and leaf drops stay). Each chew wears the held item as FC's convert does. A chew hit digs at FC's log rate (bare hand 0.004 progress per tick, where DT gave 0.0020833), a chewed (stripped) branch at FCBlockLogDamaged's hardness 2.0 against the log's 1.25 (0.0025), a trunk shell as its core; BTW's mining penalty and other mods' BreakSpeed factors apply once. Axe fell: with an FC-harvesting axe, a hit above the stump fells the tree the DT way, and BTW charges FC's harvest exhaustion (0.025 per log by default) and exactly 1 durability per real log, n = max(1, floor(wood volume / (Fortune factor x DT's `harvestMultiplier` x the family's loot multiplier))), to the stack that made the break. BTW writes that stack's damage at ServerTickEvent END, after DT's deferred `damageAxe`, so DT's wear is overwritten on it; a stack swapped into the main hand in between gets DT's wear compensated back to its earlier damage. Stump: the trunk base on rooty soil is a BTW stump. Every tool, axes included, only chews it, the chisel case below aside (bark on the first chew, then 2 sawdust per stage) at a third of the log dig rate (0.0013333), and its rooty soil cannot be dug while a log stands on it (DT's RootyBlock.playerWillDestroy would delete the tree with no drops). A dead stump (no branch above) takes FC's stump remover, from its core or any trunk shell, and an FC chisel of harvest level 2 or more turns an unchewed dead stump into FC's work stump at FCItemChiselIron's convert wear (10, Server FCItemChiselIron.java:44-54). Stripping: DT's axe strip pays 1 FC bark and exactly 1 durability, FC's 1 per convert, instead of DT's thickness-scaled wear (no wear in creative). Saw: a powered FC saw fells a DT trunk of radius 8 or more away from the saw (a trunk shell resolves to its core); the cut block gives FC's log saw output (4 planks of the species, 2 sawdust, 1 bark if unstripped; Server FCBlockSaw.java:618-639) and the falling tree carries one log fewer; younger trunks are left to grow. Bone meal: on a branch, root, rooty soil or trunk shell it is FC fertilizer for the tree's rooty soil (+2 fertility, used up only when the soil took it, nothing at 15), from either hand and from machines (`BoneMealItem.applyBonemeal`); DT's growth pulse never runs, and the client predicts the swing from the root's fertility. On a DT sapling it is relayed to the soil, as on FC's saplings, and never grows it. DT pods and fruit never grow from it, whatever DT's `canBoneMealFruit` and `canBoneMealPods` say: they are paced plants BTW governs (#017), and the hook refuses pods and fruit in any case. Without BTW a fresh server already refuses both, because DT applies its server config only after the first datapack load, but after a `/reload` pods (`canBoneMealPods` defaults to true) and any fruit configured true would grow. Saplings: every `DynamicSaplingBlock` (DT's and dtphc2's) is a SAPLING twin (#018): FC's survival (light 8 or sky; in the dark a sapling pops and drops its seed), never in the End, light 9 above, four stages at 1/64 (the stage is kept in BTW's metadata overlay, as DT saplings have no stage property), then DT's own tree through `Species.canSaplingGrow` and `transitionToTree`. SeasonGate (#028) is asked at that step, keyed by the seed item: DENY keeps the sapling at FC's last stage to retry, DESTROY removes it with no drop. A species a tree pack sets to `sapling_grows_naturally false` stays a sapling: BTW's rule paces natural growth, it does not create it. A DT transition BTW did not start (DT's own tick, a direct `performBonemeal`) is cancelled. Nether fungi: DT's crimson, warped, mega crimson and mega warped fungi (`NetherFungusSpecies`) are FUNGUS twins on FC's mushroom rule, as vanilla crimson and warped fungi are (#018): 1 in 50 random ticks, never in the End, then DT's own tree where it can grow. Fruit: DT's apple and cocoa pods are in `#betterthanwolves:paced_like_sweet_berries` (with dtphc2's 43 fruit and pods), so DT's own random tick, a 0.2 growth chance, runs at the sweet-berry pace 0.25, FC cocoa's 1/20 (Server FCBlockCocoa.java:33-58), never in the End. Creative and spectator players keep DT's own break path. Since 2026-10-20 bark, the work stump and saw planks follow the shared log rule (#034): the acacia, dark oak, mangrove and cherry families are FC wood types 5-8, with their own bark, work stump and planks, so FC's saw output covers eight woods; the crimson and warped fungus families give no bark and no work stump (the chisel chews their dead stump); other modded families pay their `#betterthanwolves:bark/<wood>` tag or oak bark, and their saw planks come from the log's planks recipe. DT leaves drop by BTW's leaf rule (#035): only the species' seed, 1 time in 20 (1 in 40 when the family's log is FC's jungle), with DT's seasonal seed chance as a veto; no sticks and no Fortune bonus, on a direct break and in a fell alike. Since 2026-10-28 a blasted vanilla oak, spruce, birch or jungle leaves block (legacy 18) takes FCBlockLeaves' own roll through BTW's blast rules (#049), not DT's seed hook, as FC's harvest of it already did; DT's own leaves are unchanged |
| **Lore mapping** | A DT branch is a log: the trunk base standing on rooty soil is FC's stump, and the stub a fell leaves is a dead stump. A stripped branch is FC's chewed log (FCBlockLogDamaged). A trunk shell is part of its core. Rooty soil is the tree's soil, so bone meal fertilizes it, as FC's plants pass fertilizer to theirs. A DT seed's sapling is a sapling: the species only chooses the tree (#018). DT's Nether fungi are mushrooms, the nearest FC relative of crimson and warped fungi (#018); DT never grows them by itself, so the twin adds growth rather than slowing it. Apples and cocoa pods ripen on the tree, like FC cocoa |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's log economy on DT forests: the axe gate and chewing (bark, sticks, sawdust), the stump and work stump, per-log hunger and wear, and FC's saw as a mechanical tree feller. FC's sapling conditions, BTW's fertilizer loop on rooty soil, and natural growth for DT's Nether fungi, which DT never grows by itself. FC axes are axes to DT (`AXE_DIG`, #022) |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — DT keeps its world generation, growth after the sapling stage, falling trees, seeds and its own planting, stripping (now paying bark), fruit and pods (decision #7), its own Serene Seasons scaling, dtphc2's species and its configs (its leaf loot follows BTW's leaf rule since 2026-10-20, #035). DT drops 1.20.1 logs, which BTW already handles |
| **Data or code?** | Code: Forge events and DT's public API, the two shared seams (TwinRegistry.ModdedSapling, one BoneMealPolicy.route clause) and the stand-in's live collision read. No mixin. The fruit pace is data: `dynamictrees:apple` and `dynamictrees:cocoa` in the generated `#betterthanwolves:paced_like_sweet_berries` tag (HarvestCraftDataProvider, #032), as `required: false` entries, so they are inert without DT |
| **Alt rejected** | Mapping DT branches onto legacy id 17: FC's world code (the saw, ConvertBlock, smouldering) would write over DT's states, DT's `onRemove` then collapses the tree with no drops, and FC never sees radius, rooty soil or shells. Loot-only rules: they cannot express chew stages, stumps or wear. A BTW tree pack: it changes DT's species data for every pack and would conflict with dtphc2's own `oak.json`. A mixin into DT's `BranchBlock.futureBreak` or `ItemUtils.damageAxe` to cancel DT's axe wear: a third foreign mixin that switches DT behaviour off, where the absolute wear target at END gives the same count with events only |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Thinning DT's own sapling tick (the earlier 1-in-256 roll): a mean pace without FC's survival, light, End and stage rules, which #018 asks for. A DT `BonemealEvent` listener: BoneMealPolicy keeps the only one, and a single route clause reuses it. DT's fungi on the sapling rule: their nearest FC relative is the mushroom, as for vanilla fungi. D1 (a), trimming the Fortune bonus to 4.B's drop of one log per log: it switches off an enchantment the player earned, while BTW's costs already count real logs |
| **Declared deviations** | From 4.B, accepted. The stump remover and the work-stump chisel act only on dead DT stumps: FC's remover writes air, which collapses a living DT tree with no drops, and a chisel on a living stump chews it. A species FC never had (a family whose log is not oak, spruce, birch or jungle) gave no bark and took the oak work stump, and under the saw gave 2 sawdust and kept its log, until 2026-10-20: bark, the work stump and saw planks now follow the shared log rule (#034, see Effect). DT leaves of a fruit species (no leaf seeds) keep DT's verdict and drop no seed, and orphan leaves drop their family's common seed (leaves D5 (c)); leaves get no Fortune bonus (leaves D9 (a), 4.B). Thick trunks (radius 9-24) chew one radius per hit: about 9 hits for a 3x3 trunk, where FC would need 45 for its 9 logs. DT growth can thicken a chewed trunk again. Dendro potions, `#dynamictrees:enhanced_fertilizer` and any non-bone-meal item a pack adds to `#dynamictrees:fertilizer` keep DT's substance effects (only `minecraft:bone_meal` is FC fertilizer). Direct `performBonemeal` callers on branches and rooty soil keep DT's growth, as for every modded BonemealableBlock outside #017's 31 gated classes; a twin sapling's transition is still cancelled. DT's strip settings shape the chew: a branch below `minRadiusForStrip` (default 6), or every branch when it is 0, has no bark stage, and `enableStripRadiusReduction=false` costs a full trunk one extra hit. D1: an axe fell keeps DT's Fortune bonus logs, and the bonus from DT's `harvestMultiplier` and the family's loot multiplier, while FC hunger and wear count only the real logs |
| **Known limitation** | DT leaves kept DT's loot (a seed at 1/64, scaled by Fortune; sticks at 2%, 1-2) instead of FCBlockLeaves' (a sapling at 1/20, 1/40 for jungle; no sticks or apples; Server FCBlockLeaves.java:63-85) until 2026-10-20 (#035); after a switch-off of this integration they drop DT's own loot again. DT internals beyond its events are tied to 1.4.10 and 1.4.11 and must be re-checked on every DT update: the one-tick FutureBreak, the cut position being the trunk core, a mutable falling-tree payload, and futureBreak's single main-hand read (DT BranchBlock.java:597) before the tree joins (:610) and `damageAxe` runs (:613); the probe catches removed or changed members, not reordered code. A protection mod that cancels `BreakEvent` at LOWEST after this handler acts too late. DT's `replaceVanillaSapling=true` (default false) is not supported: its grow-time replacement sees only saplings that reach 1.20.1's `SaplingBlock.advanceTree` (the #018 twins and modded SaplingBlocks), never FC's own oak, spruce, birch and jungle saplings (by code reading). SeasonGate acts once, at the grow step, and only for seeds a pack lists in Serene Seasons' crop tags. FC's survival rule pops DT saplings with no sky and light below 8, so some seeds DT plants under dense canopies pop. FC code sees a DT sapling as the sapling twin (id 174), so FC's block dispenser and saw treat it as an FC sapling, as for HarvestCraft saplings (by code reading). In single player the integrated server and client share the switch-off: a failure in a client-side handler (dig speed, the bone-meal prediction) also switches off the server half. A stack swapped into the main hand may sync once with its temporary headroom before END restores its damage, and a single DT charge of at least the stack's maximum (`axeDamageMode` VOLUME on a very large tree) can still break it. The follow-up that gives post-1.5.2 vanilla logs FC's log rule had to exclude DT branches, which are in `#minecraft:logs`: done 2026-10-20 (#034), since class S needs the log rule to be the first FC machine hook for the state (`FCLogRules.isForeignLog`, FCLogRules.java:308-315) and DT registers first |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs with `-PbtwDevMods=dynamictrees` (C2 after-runs, 2026-10-17/18). Every gap check fails on HEAD 3985147 (dt_p1's T6 by recount after it was retagged) and every check passes after C2: `dt_p1` harvest 39/39 (28 gap, 10 control and the D1 (b) check: Fortune III keeps DT's 4 logs, at wear 2 and exhaustion 0.05; the axe fell gives 2 logs, wear 2, exhaustion 0.05; the chew gives 1 bark, 2 sticks, 1 sawdust, a last sawdust and no logs; the iron chisel's work stump costs 10); `dt_p2` saw 26/26 (oak: 4 planks, 2 sawdust, 1 bark, 2 of 3 logs; a radius-12 spruce felled from a trunk shell; dark oak: 2 sawdust, no bark, its log kept; the stump remover on a shell of the dead radius-12 stump; the S3b block and one S3 check are after-only); `dt_p3` bone meal 14/14; `dt_p4` saplings 19/19 (3 of 100 grew in 64 random ticks; 0 of 50 at night and 0 of 50 in the End in 3,000 random ticks each; DENY, ALLOW, DESTROY and a throwing provider through a test SeasonGate provider; 16 DT crimson fungi in the dark Nether all grown within 600 random ticks each, with rooty nylium under each, and none in the End in 600 each); `dt_p5` dig 9/9; `dt_p6` with falling trees on 5/5; `dt_p8` with HarvestCraft and dtphc2 12/12 (DT apples, DT cocoa and dtphc2 apricots at the paced rate, DT apples not growing in the End, bone meal kept on a DT apple, which does not grow); `dt_p7` on 1.4.11 8/8. The first after-run found the trunk-shell collision read; after the fix the log shows `[STAND-IN] dynamictrees:trunk_shell reads as legacy id 161 to FC` (stand-in ids are per session). Logs: `[BTW-Compat] dynamictrees 1.20.1-1.4.10 present: integration #027 enabled`, `[STAND-IN] FC machine hook registered: Dynamic Trees (#027)`, `[TWIN] modded sapling kind registered: Dynamic Trees saplings (#027)`, `[MODDED-PLANTS] crops=0 saplings=0 modded-saplings-on-FC-rule=19` (DT alone: 15 saplings and 4 fungi; 114 with HarvestCraft and dtphc2), no `switched off` line. Plain BTW (`dt_p9`): no #027 or modded-sapling line, `modded-saplings-on-FC-rule=0`, the twin audit unchanged (`6 twin families (11 blocks), 41 twin states, 173 paced states, 0 warnings`). The all-mods server passes the DT fell checks (wear 2, exhaustion 0.05). Production smoke (2026-10-18): the reobfuscated release jar on a Forge 47.3.0 dedicated server with DT 1.4.10, dtphc2 and the other optional mods logs `#027 enabled` and `modded-saplings-on-FC-rule=114`, with 0 mixin errors; the C2 review had found that the probe named `performBonemeal`, which release DT jars carry only as `m_214148_`, so #027 would never have started there. Client: joins with `#027 enabled` and 0 mixin errors. Not runtime-tested: the client's dig timing and bone-meal swing prediction (the client run checked only the join and the log); bone meal on DT pods after a `/reload`; a tree pack with `sapling_grows_naturally false`; Serene Seasons with DT seeds in its crop tags; a powered saw in play (the proofs call `FCBlockSaw.SawBlockToFront` directly); the fruit pace with DT alone (proved with HarvestCraft and dtphc2 installed); 1.4.11 in production, and on 1.4.11 the trunk-shell fix (`dt_p7` ran before it). 2026-10-20 (follow-ups; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage B): P-LEAF-DT 10/10 (2/10 on 0292e38), dt_wood 4/4 (the DT dark oak family's work stump is wood type 6 and its chew pays bark 6), dt_p2 27/27 (the three new S4 checks: a sawn DT dark oak gives 4 dark oak planks, 2 sawdust and bark 6, where it gave no bark and kept its log), dt_off 2/2; dt_p1 39/39 and dt_p5 9/9 unchanged; log `[LEAVES] leaf drop hook registered: Dynamic Trees leaves (#027)` |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | DT forests feed BTW's wood chain at BTW's costs: logs need an axe, cost hunger and wear per log, and leave a stump. Old chunks keep vanilla trees under FC's own log rules |

### #028 — Serene Seasons

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Serene Seasons `sereneseasons`, optional (`mods.toml` accepts `[9.0.0.0,)`, loaded after it): a season clock with per-season crop fertility (its season crop tags, greenhouse glass in the 16 cells above a crop, an underground rule, and `out_of_season_crop_behavior` 0, 1 or 2 in `config/sereneseasons/fertility.toml`), a bone-meal veto on out-of-season crops, seed tooltips, and a seasonal biome temperature that decides where it rains, snows, freezes and melts |
| **Added** | Third-party mod. Built and tested against 1.20.1-9.1.0.3 with GlitchCore 0.0.1.1; the 9.1 line runs on GlitchCore, the legacy 9.0.0.46 line does not. javap of the SS classes BTW calls is identical in 9.3.0.27 |
| **Decisions** | Compat-wave decisions (2026-10-11): #8 (a) SS's cold-position rule applies to BTW seeds and is documented; #9 (a) BTW snow and farmland are always seasonal, whatever SS's `generate_snow_ice` says; #10 (a) out-of-season behaviour 0 is one FC growth day in six; #19 (a) the range `[9.0.0.0,)` with capability probes. ss D1 (SS's bone-meal veto on FC-owned plants), settled 2026-10-17 as (b), the veto mixin below, by the maintainer's ruling that bone meal is 4.B fertilizer (#017); LsoRegenGateMixin (#031) is the precedent for a `require = 0` mixin into another mod |
