Logs: `[BTW-Compat] pamhc2crops 1.0.3, pamhc2trees 1.0.2, pamhc2foodcore 1.0.5, pamhc2foodextended 0.0NONE present: integration #032 enabled`; `[BTW-Food] loaded 1324 entries (9 tag, 4 tiered), 0 unknown ids skipped`; `[BTW-Food] sources: FC_ITEM 63, FC_DRINKABLE 2, TABLE 1304, MODERN_CONVERTED 0, capped 1323` (TABLE 17 and MODERN_CONVERTED 1287 at 3985147); `RawCrafted: 181 crafted food(s) marked as made from raw ingredients (27 poisonous raw item(s))` (159 and 21); `Recipe replacements: 5 replaced, 1 removed, 0 skipped (6 file(s))`; `Tag machine recipes: 40 millstone recipe(s) added from 2 file(s)`; `[MODDED-PLANTS] crops=97 saplings=0 modded-saplings-on-FC-rule=50 fc_soil_plants=6 plant stand-in=162 base=0.1`; `[SELF-AUDIT/twin] 103 blocks of other mods grow by random tick or bone meal at their own pace` (153 at 3985147: the 50 fruit left the count); `[BTW-Compat] HarvestCraft #032: 1287 foods at BTW values (1287 table, 0 converted), 97 crops on the BTW soil rule, 50 saplings on the FC sapling rule, 50 fruit at the sweet-berry pace, 6 gardens spread by dung; reroutes bread_grain=replaced bread_dough=replaced torch_cattail=replaced flour_x1_flour_plants=replaced string_fiber=removed` (95 fruit with Dynamic Trees and dtphc2). The all-mods server keeps bone meal on a sand garden. Production smoke (2026-10-18): the reobfuscated release jar on a Forge 47.3.0 dedicated server with the release HarvestCraft jars, dtphc2, `sereneseasonsphc2crops` and the other optional mods logs `#032 enabled`, `Recipe replacements: 5 replaced, 1 removed`, `40 millstone recipe(s)` and `[BTW-Food] loaded 1324 entries (9 tag, 4 tiered)`, with 0 mixin errors and no BTW stack frame; its only ERROR lines are HarvestCraft and Dynamic Trees recipe parse errors |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | HarvestCraft widens BTW's farming, forestry and diet at BTW's cost: its crops and trees grow by BTW's rules, its grains and fibres go through the millstone, its kitchen runs on milled BTW flour at 4.B's ratio, and its meals feed at BTW scale with BTW's poisoning. Gardens become a find in the world that dung, not bone meal, spreads. Without HarvestCraft nothing changes |

### #033 — A right-click uses the block before the held BTW item

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's right-click on a block, `ServerPlayerGameMode.useItemOn`: Forge's `RightClickBlock` event (a cancel, or a `useItem` DENY), the spectator check, the sneak rule, the clicked block's `use`, then the held item's `ItemStack.useOn`; the off hand |
| **Added** | MC 1.9 (the off hand); the `useItemOn` order and Forge's `RightClickBlock` are 1.20.1's |
| **Conflict** | At 0292e38 `ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.btw$useItemOn` ran FC `onItemUse` at HEAD of `useItemOn` for every FC item, ahead of `RightClickBlock`, the spectator check and the block's activation (`STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (aj), and (at) caveat BM8). Whenever the FC item acted it consumed the click first: flint and steel on a lever or on weedy FC crops, an axe on a note block, any item on a millstone. Claim mods' cancel and DENY did not stop it, Stage 2 (the FC item inside the block's `use`) ignored a DENY, an off-hand click ran the main-hand FC item, and no route awarded 4.B's item-used statistic. 4.B activates the block first and uses the held item only when the activation declined or the player sneaks with an item (vanilla/server ItemInWorldManager.java:376-407); #017's early return restored that order for bone meal only |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's item use runs where 1.20.1 calls the held item, and nowhere else: one click runs at most one FC block activation and one FC item use, main hand only. Rebalance: none, 4.B's order |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_item_use_order`. [`ServerPlayerGameModeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java): `btw$beginClick` (HEAD of `useItemOn`, never cancels, opens the per-click frame; :359-360); `btw$fcItemStep` (a cancellable inject at the INVOKE of `ItemStack.useOn`, `require = 2`; :376-379), which skips the off hand, BlockItems (their FC route is Stage 2, and a Stage-2 refusal is final, #040), items without an FC identity, a block Stage 2 already ran FC on, and bone meal (`BoneMealPolicy.isFcBoneMeal`, #017); after an FC success it opens an FC container, writes back every changed slot, resends the held slot (`InventoryBridge.resendHeldSlot`), syncs the ProxyBlockEntity, fires `ITEM_USED_ON_BLOCK` (:456) and returns CONSUME; `btw$afterUseItemOn` (RETURN; :483-484) finishes an FC placement (#040) and closes the frame. The HEAD pre-emption `btw$useItemOn` is deleted. [`ProxyBlock`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyBlock.java)`.tryPlaceHeldItem` (ProxyBlock.java:617) is the single FC `onItemUse` point of a block click: the DENY check, the click mark, the 1.5.2 creative restore (`runFcItemUse`, :738) and `Stats.ITEM_USED`; `ProxyBlock.use` (:527) answers PASS for the off hand on the server, and [`BlockBehaviorMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BlockBehaviorMixin.java)`.btw$use` (:231) keeps Stage 1 and Stage 2 to the main hand. [`FcItemUseOrder`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcItemUseOrder.java) (FcItemUseOrder.java:41-45): a server-side LOWEST `RightClickBlock` listener that records the `useItem` verdict. The click frame on [`PlayerBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/PlayerBridge.java): `UseClick` (:395), `beginUseClick` (:437), `endUseClick` (:443), `isFcUseDenied` (:448), `setFcUseDenied` (:454), `markFcItemUse` (:464), `fcItemUseRanAt` (:472). Built on the inventory re-sync fix of 2026-10-20 (`STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (az), triage CF2-I1), which landed first. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Use order (FC item step and Stage 2) |
| **Effect** | The block acts first: flint and steel on a lever toggles the lever, and a stump remover or a stick on grass is answered by the block with the item kept. Holding a tool, flint and steel or a stick while right-clicking a lever, a door, a chest, a crop or an FC machine activates it; using the item on such a block takes a sneak-click, as in 4.B. A claim mod's cancel or `useItem` DENY stops BTW items too, in Stage 2 as at the item step. An off-hand click on a block runs neither the block's FC activation nor the main-hand FC item. A successful FC use counts in the item-used statistic and the item-used-on-block advancement trigger. A bed click in the Nether or the End explodes whatever is held (`BedBlock.use` consumes every server click); a sneak with an item still skips the bed and reaches the FC item, as in 4.B |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's right-click order for every BTW item, and BTW's item use made visible to the mods that guard right-clicks (claims, protection) and to the statistics and advancements |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's `useItemOn`, every block's `use` and every item's `useOn` stay; BTW runs at 1.20.1's own item step. Items and blocks without an FC identity are untouched |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one rewritten mixin, one Forge listener and the click frame on `PlayerBridge` |
| **Alt rejected** | Keeping the HEAD pre-emption, or widening #017's bone-meal early return to every FC item: FC would still run before the event and the spectator check. FC inside `ProxyItem.useOn` with an `Item.useOn` mixin, which would run FC under Forge's snapshot capture that defers `onPlace` (ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java:351-356) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | A `@Redirect` of `useOn`: exclusive, so it fails against another mod's redirect of the same call. HEAD of `ItemStack.useOn`: it has no click boundary for the per-click state and changes every third-party caller |
| **Known limitation** | Residuals the maintainer accepted (decision D6), with the activation audit queued next: on vanilla classes with an FC identity that override `use`, 1.20.1's `use` now comes before the FC item, so redstone ore, an empty flower pot, signs and a tuning fork on a note block no longer reach the FC item; flint and steel priming TNT costs 1 durability (4.B: none), and a fire charge primes TNT at once. After a server-side FC success the 1.20.1 client may still send its own `useItem` in the same click (D8; 4.B's client did not). When the FC item declines, 1.20.1's `useOn` still runs, as before, and can act where FC refused for a 4.B reason (D4: mid-air flint and steel places vanilla fire; its own audit is queued). With an empty main hand, an off-hand sneak-click skips the activation (1.20.1), where 4.B activated (D9). Beds stay in D6's "closer to 4.B" group: `BedBlock.use` consumes the click as FCBlockBed did, now with 1.20.1 sleep (#037). A refused FC placement is #040's |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-20; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage I1; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (ba)): the use-order plan P-USE 39/39 (21/39 on 0292e38; all 18 gap checks flip); in P-HOOK, P1a (flint and steel on a lever toggles it) and P1b (a `RightClickBlock` cancel gates FC items) flip, 19/34 at that stage; every other regression plan the same, apart from the intended replies (the stump remover and the stick on grass answer SUCCESS instead of CONSUME, with the same items left). Refmap emits `m_7179_` (useItemOn) and `m_41661_` (useOn). A development client joined to the dedicated server: the placement-count run 30/30, its stake row S1 passing through the item step (triage CF2-I6 closed); the windmill, hook, bed and hold-to-craft client runs unchanged. Not runtime-tested: a claim mod's DENY in a real pack |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Right-clicks behave as in 4.B: the block first, then the held BTW item |

### #034 — Acacia, dark oak, mangrove and cherry are BTW woods; one log rule for every log

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | The woods added after 1.5.2: acacia, dark oak, mangrove and cherry logs, their wood (bark on all sides) and stripped forms, and their planks; crimson and warped stems and hyphae; other mods' `#minecraft:logs`; the Dynamic Trees families of those species |
| **Added** | MC 1.7 (acacia, dark oak), 1.13 (stripped logs, wood blocks), 1.16 (crimson and warped stems), 1.19 (mangrove), 1.20 (cherry) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-19, ruling 1: acacia, dark oak, mangrove and cherry become real FC wood types through runtime patches, each with its own bark, tanning, work stump, chewed log and saw output; no mapping onto oak, spruce, birch or jungle. Ruling 2: crimson and warped stems take the log rule with no bark and no work stump. 2026-10-20: the new woods' sub-blocks (sidings, mouldings, corners, decorative blocks) are approved as a follow-up (FU-W1) |
| **Conflict** | The four logs had no FC identity (`getBlockId` 0; FC read them as `ModernBlockStandIn`s), so none of FCBlockLog's rules reached them: a bare hand took whole logs, their vanilla log -> 4 planks recipe stayed (FC removes it for its species 0-3 only), and there was no bark, tanning, work stump, saw planks, chopping, BTW fuel or FC harvest hunger. Stems and modded logs had no log rule at all (#022's known limitation; triage CF2-PR-WOOD) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): the four woods run FC's own FCBlockLog on a second log block, through load-time patches keyed on its id and wood types; every other log takes FC's log rule through one shared API. Rebalance: the per-wood numbers under Lore mapping |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): modern_woods` and `fc_log_rules` (#022's tag, extended). [`ModernWoods`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernWoods.java) (class javadoc ModernWoods.java:16-54; `LOG2_ID = 165` :61; `BARK_PER_LEATHER` :92; `PLANKS_BURN_TIME` :98; `create` :199; `addRecipes` :266; `audit` :335), built at BTWLifecycle step 5e (BTWLifecycle.java:167-169), after FC's table and the twins and before any stand-in id is allocated: log2 is a plain FC `FCBlockLog` with FC's `FCItemBlockLog` at legacy id 165, species = wood type - 5. [`ProxyRegistry`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyRegistry.java)'s log API: `getBlockId` (:288) answers 165 for the four logs only while `ModernWoods.isBuilt`; `LogForm` (:324) and `LOG_FORMS` (:338) map the wood and stripped forms to (log id, species); `isLogForm` (:368), `getTreeLogId` (:378), `getTreeLogSpecies` (:391, now public), `getWoodType` (:404: 0-3 or 5-8). The item rows in [`LegacyVariants`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/LegacyVariants.java) (:138-144: the four planks are FC planks 5:5-8 at item level, and in the world too since 2026-10-28, #051). Nine `@Pseudo` load-time patches on FC classes in `btw.forge.mixin.fcext` (`remap = false`): M1 [`FCBlockLogWoodMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCBlockLogWoodMixin.java) (the log), M2 `FCBlockWorkStumpWoodMixin`, M3 `FCItemBarkWoodMixin`, M4 `FCItemBlockLogWoodMixin` (the log item in crafting), M5 `FCRecipesLogChoppingWoodMixin`, M6 `FCBlockSawWoodMixin`, M7 `FCBlockPlanksWoodMixin`, M8 `FCBlockLogDamagedWoodMixin` (the chewed log), M9 `FCItemChiselIronWoodMixin`. [`modern-woods.gradle`](Forge/gradle/modern-woods.gradle) (`deriveModernWoodTextures`, :30, part of processResources; `-PbtwClientExtraJar=<path>`) derives the four work-stump textures; the bark icons are item models on `minecraft:block/<wood>_log`; names in `lang/BTW_modern_en_US.lang`; `[SELF-AUDIT/wood]` (BridgeSelfAudit.java:44-45). The log rule: [`FCLogRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FCLogRules.java) (`fcWoodMeta` :165, which answers `getWoodType`; `bark` :185; `workStumpState` :218; `chargeLogHarvest` :240; `isForeignLog` :313; `isLogRuleTarget` :318; `isStump` :330, the port's one stump rule; `sawPlanks` :450; the bark tags :91, :95), [`ModernLogHooks`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernLogHooks.java)`.register` (ModernLogHooks.java:33; registered after `BTWCompat.init`, BTWForgeMod.java:66-67, so an integration's own hook, Dynamic Trees', is asked first), [`ModernLogRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernLogRules.java) (BreakSpeed, BreakEvent and BlockToolModificationEvent at LOWEST, :100, :147, :255; strip bark settled at ServerTickEvent END, :289), [`LogBarkTagProvider`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/data/LogBarkTagProvider.java) (runData: `#betterthanwolves:bark/<wood>` for oak, spruce, birch, jungle, blood_wood, acacia, dark_oak, mangrove and cherry, and `bark/none` for the stems), and the WorldBridge log derive (WorldBridge.java:386-400). Dynamic Trees: `DTTrees`, `DTBlockHooks`, `DTHarvestHandler` (#027). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Woods and logs (ModernWoods, FCLogRules) |
| **Effect** | The four woods are FC wood types 5-8 (log2 at 165; their wood and stripped forms read as 165): chewing (any hit without an FC-harvesting axe), bark 5-8 for tanning, the work stump, saw planks, chopping, BTW fuel, FC's dig rate and FC's 0.025 harvest hunger; their vanilla log -> 4 planks recipe is removed, as FC removes it for oak, spruce, birch and jungle. Class F, the wood and stripped forms of all eight woods (FC reads them as log 17 or 165): FC's dig rate and FC's own `ConvertBlock` chew, with 1.20.1's harvest drop. Class S, the stems, hyphae and other mods' logs no earlier integration owns: the axe gate (a hit without a harvesting FC axe, or on a stump, chews: the wood's bark and FC's chewed log at once), the work stump, and FC's saw and stump remover through `ModernLogHooks`; stems give no bark and leave no work stump; saw planks come from the log's one-ingredient planks recipe, and with none the log drops as itself. A modded log pays its `#betterthanwolves:bark/<wood>` tag, or oak bark. An axe strip pays bark only for barked modded logs; for BTW's woods the chew or the saw pays it. The stump is one rule (`FCLogRules.isStump`), so an oak log standing on a stem or a DT branch is no longer a stump. The Dynamic Trees families of the four woods get their own bark, work stump and planks (#027) |
| **Lore mapping** | A log is a log: the four newer woods are FC woods, with numbers taken from their nearest relatives. Tannin, bark per leather (FC: oak 5, spruce 3, birch 8, jungle 2): acacia 2 (wattle bark is among the richest tannin sources), dark oak 5 (an oak), mangrove 2 (mangrove bark, cutch, is very rich in tannin), cherry 8 (thin, ring-peeling bark, like birch); cut leather takes the same. Planks fuel (FC: oak 400, spruce 300, birch 500, jungle 200): acacia and dark oak 400 (the oak tier), mangrove 200 (the jungle tier), cherry 500 (the birch tier); logs burn four times their planks (ModernWoods.java:86-102). Stems are fungus stalks: they chew and saw like logs, but have no bark to tan and leave no work stump |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's whole wood economy on the newer woods and on every other log: chewing, bark and tanning, work stumps, the saw, chopping, BTW fuel and FC's harvest hunger. Each new wood's bark tans leather at its own rate |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — the four woods keep their 1.20.1 blocks, items, planks, world generation and other recipes; only the log -> 4 planks shortcut goes, as for BTW's own woods. Stems and modded logs keep their blocks and 1.20.1's harvest drop, and the log rule acts only on states no earlier integration owns (Dynamic Trees first) |
| **Data or code?** | Code (ModernWoods, nine load-time patches, the log rule) and data: 10 generated bark tags, 4 derived work-stump textures, 4 bark item models and lang entries |
| **Decisions** | Logs design: 2(a), which is ruling 2; 3(b) (a class-S chew gives FC's chewed log at once); 4(c) (a class-S harvest keeps 1.20.1's 0.005); 5(a); 6(a) (a barked log with no bark tag pays oak bark and makes an oak work stump); 7(a) as amended by the woods design; 8(a) (an axe strip of an FC wood pays no bark: the chew or the saw does); 9(a) (the stump rule stays geometric) |
| **Alt rejected** | Tag-based bark for the four (logs design decision 1(a): the nearest species' bark and work stump, no wood of their own), which ruling 1 replaced. A new FC block id at 175 or above: it would become a registered ProxyBlock with a second, duplicate log item. Editing FC's sources (Protocol #1) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Mapping stems or modded logs onto legacy 17: FC writes into the world on that id, and Dynamic Trees would then collapse its trees (#022) |
| **Known limitation** | Legacy id 165 must never change: FC's NBT stores numeric ids. If 165 is taken at start-up, one ERROR is logged and the four woods stay class S. The flammable stand-in pool shrinks by one: three remain (162-164), two with the plant stand-in (#014, #018, #026). FC's own work stumps (META 0-3) still hit the block-dispenser stub (`STUB_CATALOGUE.md:895`), while 5-8 are retrievable (M2). Blood wood planks 5:4 still become oak planks. Acacia logs FC stored before this change (headless stacks) do not stack with new (165, species) stacks until they pass through a 1.20.1 inventory. The work-stump sides are derived textures with Mojang pixels that do not follow resource packs (the bark icons do). Kept from 4.B: a broken work stump drops an oak plank, and the chewed log is oak-textured, so stems chew into FC's oak-looking, flammable chewed log. Class F and class S harvests keep 1.20.1's 0.005 hunger (#021's known limitation). Class-S logs, which are stand-ins to FC, have no kiln and no smouldering; an ordinary blast drops them whole at the blast's chance, as 4.B drops FC's logs, and since 2026-10-28 a mining charge breaks them into 6 sawdust and, while barked, their bark (#049); a chew can flicker for one frame; `FCLogRules.barked` reads with a synthetic context that has no player. Follow-ups: FU-W2 (lightning), FU-W3 (FC leaves support from log2), FU-W4 (endermen), FU-W5 (general harvest hunger). Done since 2026-10-28: FU-W1, the sidings, mouldings, corners and decorative blocks of the new woods (approved 2026-10-20; #051), and the upward chew-eject (triage CF2-DR2), closed by #044 |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-20; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage B; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (ay)), every gap check failing on 0292e38 and passing after: the woods plan wood_p1 67/67 (14/67 on 0292e38), the modern-log plan mlog_p1 73/73 (18/73), Dynamic Trees dt_wood 4/4 (the DT dark oak family's work stump is wood type 6 and its chew pays bark 6) and dt_p2 27/27 (the three new S4 checks: a sawn DT dark oak gives 4 dark oak planks, 2 sawdust and bark 6), dt_off 2/2; dt_p1 39/39 and dt_p5 9/9 unchanged. Development clients: wood_c1 (the work-stump sprites `fcblockworkstumpacacia` and `fcblockworkstumpcherry`, bark drawn from `minecraft:block/acacia_log` and `cherry_log`, and a bare-hand dig progress of 0.004 per tick on an acacia log; on 0292e38 the stumps drew spruce and oak, the bark blood wood, and the dig ran at 0.0167) and mlog_c1 (the client's dig progress equals the server's). Logs: `[WOOD] log2 FCBlockLog at 165: acacia, dark oak, mangrove and cherry are FC wood types 5-8`, `[WOOD] tanning with bark 5-8 ([2, 5, 2, 8] bark per leather) and the 4 vanilla log -> planks removals added`, `[STAND-IN] FC machine hook registered: the log rule (stems and modded logs, fc_log_rules)`, `[SELF-AUDIT/wood] log2 ... at 165: 4 woods (wood types 5-8), 0 warnings (0 expected)`; the build's `deriveModernWoodTextures: 8 textures checked (0 written)` (the four work-stump sides, and since #051 the four column sides; 4 work-stump textures before); runData wrote the 10 bark tags. The wave-C hook scan finds the four logs at 165 with OAK_LOG's hooks. On the final tree (2026-10-21) wood_c1 and mlog_c1 are unchanged |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | The newer woods feed BTW's wood chain at BTW's costs, and every log in the game follows one log rule |

### #035 — Leaves drop by BTW's leaf rule

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Every `#minecraft:leaves` block on every loot path: the leaves of the newer trees (acacia, dark oak, mangrove, cherry, azalea and flowering azalea), Dynamic Trees leaves and other mods' leaves, and 1.20.1's leaf loot (saplings at 1.20.1's odds with a Fortune bonus, sticks, apples from oak and dark oak) |
| **Added** | MC 1.7 – 1.20 (acacia and dark oak 1.7, azalea 1.17, mangrove 1.19, cherry 1.20); Dynamic Trees |
| **Conflict** | 4.B's FCBlockLeaves drops its own sapling 1 time in 20 (1 in 40 for jungle), never sticks or apples, and the leaves themselves only to shears (Server FCBlockLeaves.java:63-85). In the port, Modern-Common BlockLeaves lacked 1.5.2's drop methods, so FC's own leaves dropped oak leaves, shears and Silk Touch did not give the leaves, and blood wood leaves dropped a sapling on every break. The newer and Dynamic Trees leaves kept 1.20.1's or DT's loot: sticks, apples, Fortune-scaled saplings and seeds. #027 left DT's leaf loot open (its decision D2) |
| **Pattern** | **D** with **C**, justified: D because FCBlockLeaves' own roll reaches every leaves block through one loot modifier and a hook API that takes Minecraft types only; C because it subtracts the sticks, apples and Fortune bonus from the newer and DT leaves, which is 4.B's leaf rule applied to them by the post-1.5.2 lore ruling (#018). Rebalance: none beyond 4.B |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): leaves_drop_like_fc`. [`LeafDrops`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/LeafDrops.java) (LeafDrops.java:67; `Resolution` :87, `KEEP` :90, `Hook` :96, `register(Predicate<BlockState>, Hook)` :115, `apply` :121; logger `BTW-Leaves`): a registered hook decides first; otherwise FC's leaves (legacy id 18) use FC's own `idDropped` and `damageDropped`, and any other leaves block the name rule; shears or Silk Touch loot is kept; saplings, sticks (`#forge:rods/wooden`), the hook's byproducts and, for minecraft leaves, apples are stripped, and one roll of 1 in 20 (1 in 40 for FC's jungle species) gives the sapling; Fortune and the blast radius are never read. The loot modifier [`FCLeavesLootModifier`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/data/FCLeavesLootModifier.java) (`betterthanwolves:fc_leaves`, instance `leaves_drop_like_fc`; FCLeavesLootModifier.java:33, BTWLootModifiers.java:40, FCLootOverrides.java:103). Modern-Common [`BlockLeaves`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/BlockLeaves.java) gains 1.5.2's drop methods (BlockLeaves.java:54-103: `quantityDropped` 1 in 20, `idDropped` the sapling, `damageDropped` `meta & 3`, `harvestBlock` the leaves for shears, `createStackedBlock`, and `canSilkHarvest` true). Dynamic Trees: `DynamicTreesCompat.init` registers the DT hook before its probe, and [`DTLeafDrops`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/compat/dynamictrees/DTLeafDrops.java) (`probe` :58, `resolve` :80) gives the species' seed (#027). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Leaves (LeafDrops) |
| **Effect** | BTW's leaves drop their own sapling (they dropped oak leaves); shears and Silk Touch give the leaves; blood wood leaves drop a sapling 1 time in 20 (it was every break). The newer and DT leaves drop only their sapling or seed, 1 time in 20 (1 in 40 for a jungle-type tree): no sticks, no apples from minecraft leaves, no Fortune bonus. Blasts no longer reduce leaf drops. Mangrove leaves drop propagules. The FC block dispenser takes the right species of leaves. A leaves block with no FC identity and no hook finds its sapling by name: the same namespace's `<stem>_sapling`, then `<stem>`, then the only `<stem>_*` |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's leaf rule on every tree of every mod, through a hook any integration can register with Minecraft types only. Mangrove leaves become a propagule source |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — FC's own paths (harvest, decay, machines, the block dispenser) run FCBlockLeaves on the Modern-Common BlockLeaves methods; the table paths (1.20.1 breaks and decay of the newer leaves, 1.20.1 explosions of leaves FC has no blast rule for, stand-in machine drops, other mods' `destroyBlock`) go through the loot modifier, and since 2026-10-28 FC's own leaves (legacy 18) take FCBlockLeaves' roll on every blast through BTW's blast rules (#049); nothing drops twice. A leaves block whose sapling is not in `#minecraft:saplings` keeps its own loot, with one INFO line |
| **Data or code?** | Code (one loot modifier, the hook API, Modern-Common ports) and generated data (`leaves_drop_like_fc.json` and its `global_loot_modifiers.json` entry; runData is required) |
| **Decisions** | Leaves design: D1(b), D2(a), D3(a), D4(a) (DT's seasonal seed chance is a veto), D5(c) (a seed only when DT's `shouldDropSeeds` allows it: a fruit species gives none, orphan leaves give the family's common seed), D6(b) (the interim `canSilkHarvest`), D7(a), D8(b), D9(a) (no Fortune bonus, 4.B), D10(a) (the DT hook registers before the probe), D11(a) |
| **Alt rejected** | A twin or an FC identity for the modern leaves (the twins keep modern leaves and their decay on 1.20.1, TwinRegistry.java:96). Per-table data-pack overrides, which need one table per leaves block and cannot reach other mods' leaves or Dynamic Trees' species |
| **Known limitation** | The `canSilkHarvest` override on Modern-Common BlockLeaves is interim (D6(b)) until a general `canSilkHarvest` (D6(a)) and the drop-parity follow-up (D7, 66 candidate classes) replace it. A mining charge on leaves runs FC's own path, where `Block.DropItemsOnDestroyedByExplosion` was a stub for leaves until 2026-10-28; since #049 it is 1.5.2's body, so the charge reaches FCBlockLeaves (triage CF2-DR5 closed). An FC identity other than 18 inside `#minecraft:leaves` would keep its loot with one INFO line (none exists). After a Dynamic Trees switch-off, DT leaves drop DT's own loot again |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-20; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage B; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (ax)): the leaves plan P-LEAF-V 32/32 (12/32 on 0292e38) and the Dynamic Trees leaves plan P-LEAF-DT 10/10 (2/10). Its row F6 (blood wood saplings at 1 in 20) reads 100 saplings in 2000 breaks once the harness counted FC item entities (blood wood saplings spawn as an FC entity item); the 0292e38 row had been blind. runData wrote `leaves_drop_like_fc.json` and its `global_loot_modifiers.json` entry. Logs: `[LEAVES] leaf drop hook registered: Dynamic Trees leaves (#027)`, and no `[LEAVES] minecraft:` or `[LEAVES] dynamictrees:` line (every vanilla and DT leaves block resolves). Unchanged through the later stages |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Leaves are a sapling source only, as in 4.B, whatever the tree |

### #036 — Each block hook has one owner: FC or 1.20.1

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's block hooks (`use`, `attack`, `onPlace`, `onRemove`, `neighborChanged`, `entityInside`, the signal methods, `stepOn`, `fallOn`, explosion resistance) and the 1.20.1 classes that override them without calling super; the anvil's repair menu; golem building from a carved pumpkin or jack o'lantern (`CarvedPumpkinBlock.onPlace`); the enchanting table's menu; fence-gate use; farmland trampling; lava meeting water (Forge's `FluidInteractionRegistry`, `LavaFluid.spreadTo` and `canBeReplacedWith`) |
| **Added** | The hooks predate 1.5.2; these executors and Forge's `FluidInteractionRegistry` are 1.20.1's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-19: ruling 3, bed USE is 1.20.1 sleep (the spawn part is #037); ruling 4, the NEIGHBOR hook of doors, trapdoors and fence gates keeps 1.20.1 redstone, oak included, although 4.B's wooden ones ignore redstone. Hook-design decisions taken with them: D16(b) (the fence-gate notify adapter), R15 anvil (c), D5(a) (the enchanting table opens), D3(a) (no golems from pumpkins), D9(b) (one farmland trample roll), D14(b) (lava pillows through Forge's interaction), P0 (every FC call caught) |
| **Conflict** | The port ran FC's 1.5.2 hooks only from the 1.20.1 base bodies (`BlockBehaviorMixin`, `BlockMixin`), so whether FC ran depended on 1.20.1's class layout (231 override-without-super pairs bypassed FC, 108 of them hooks 4.B changed), and nothing could say "FC replaces 1.20.1 here" (the anvil, golems) or "1.20.1 owns this hook" (FC bodies that cast to FC block entities: breaking, burning or blowing up a beacon or ender chest threw `TileEntityBridge cannot be cast`, and TNT next to a beacon stopped the server). A night bed click threw a ClassCastException (triage CF2-U1). FCGameplayBridge cancelled the enchanting table, which 4.B kept. Farmland was trampled by two rolls (1.20.1's at 0.5 and FC's at 0.75; #015). Lava meeting water made cobblestone or stone where 4.B makes obsidian, a lava pillow or nothing |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend), shaped like the tick ownership of `TickPolicy` (`STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (as)): each hook of each block state gets one owner, decided once from 4.B and cached on the state, and FC's bodies run only where they own or share the hook. Rebalance: none; 4.B's rules, with ruling 4 keeping 1.20.1 redstone |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): hook_ownership`. [`HookPolicy`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/HookPolicy.java) (HookPolicy.java:116; the tag :39): two bits per hook in one int (UNDECIDED, NOTIFY, FC, VANILLA) for ten hooks, read through `Access` (:168) on every block state; `decide` (:223) in rule order: (1) not ready, a ProxyBlock or legacy id 0: no owner; (2) a removed T1 block (#019): VANILLA; (3) the block-entity guard (`isTeGuarded` :281: USE and REMOVE of a block whose FC block is a `BlockContainer`, whose block entity is always 1.20.1's; since 2026-10-28 lifted where that block entity carries FC's tile entity, the beacon's, `isTeGuarded(Block, fc)`, #053): VANILLA; (4) the ruled and adapter rows (`ruledRule` :297, `RULED_IDS` :197): VANILLA; (5) metadata prerequisites still pending: VANILLA; (6) an equivalent 1.20.1 body (every FallingBlock's PLACE): NOTIFY; (7) the general rule: FC when the 1.20.1 class overrides the hook and FC's class declares it, else NOTIFY; RESIST stayed undecided until 2026-10-28 and is now #049's (FC for a block whose FC class reads its position for blast resistance, else NOTIFY). `notifyRuns` (:365); `EXPECTED_FC` (:209). `activate` (:411), the USE executor: Stage 1 (FC `onBlockActivated`) only when FC shares or owns USE and the block is not guarded, then Stage 2 (the held FC item, #033) whatever the owner, then the menu, the write-back and the held-slot resend; a Stage-1 throw discards FC's pending inventory edits and answers 1.20.1. `runFcOwnedUse` (:486): the three anvils, where an empty-handed sneak-click falls through to 1.20.1's AnvilMenu (R15). `runFcOwnedPlace` (:508): the carved pumpkin and jack o'lantern, which still cancel 1.20.1's body if FC throws. [`BlockStateHookMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BlockStateHookMixin.java) (priority 1100; owners cached at the `initCache` TAIL, :68; FC-owned `use` :88 and `onPlace` :100). [`FenceGateBlockMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/FenceGateBlockMixin.java) (:46, RETURN of `use`: a gate with an FC identity updates its neighbours, as FCBlockFenceGate did with flag 3). [`BlockBehaviorMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BlockBehaviorMixin.java)'s executors gated on the owner, with every FC call in try/catch (`btw$onRemove` :194, with the block-entity guard and the derived metadata; `btw$use` :231, which calls `HookPolicy.activate`; `btw$attack` :378, now with a synced PlayerBridge); [`BlockMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BlockMixin.java) STEP and FALL gated (:78, :112); [`FarmBlockMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/FarmBlockMixin.java) :87-90 (`@ModifyArg` of argument 3 of `ForgeHooks.onFarmlandTrample` in `fallOn`, -0.25, `remap = false` on the `@At`; the class also carries #015's tag); `BridgeSelfAudit.auditHookPolicy` (BridgeSelfAudit.java:66, :376; checks (a) to (e)); FCGameplayBridge's enchanting-table cancel deleted (FCGameplayBridge.java:46-49). Lava: [`LavaRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/LavaRules.java) (LavaRules.java:19; `contactRule` :105; `hardensOnContact` :130: a source, or non-falling lava with amount 4 or more, 4.B's meta 1-4; `flowOntoWater` :152; the pillow found through `FcRefs`), [`FluidInteractionRegistryMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/FluidInteractionRegistryMixin.java) (:50-53, `@ModifyArg` on the first `addInteraction` in `<clinit>`, ordinal 0, `remap = false`), [`LavaFluidMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/LavaFluidMixin.java) (:91, a `@ModifyArg` of argument 3 of `fireFluidPlaceBlockEvent` in `spreadTo`; :101-103, `canBeReplacedWith` HEAD answers false: no fluid displaces lava, vanilla/server BlockFlowing.java:443-447). Rows today: `ADAPTER(D16b)=[107]` (gate USE), `ADAPTER(D9b)=[60]` (farmland FALL), `RULED(3)=[26]` (bed USE), `RULED(4)=[64, 71, 96, 107]` (door, trapdoor and gate NEIGHBOR), `RULED(5)=[26]` (bed NEIGHBOR, #039). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Hook ownership (HookPolicy) |
| **Effect** | A click on an anvil opens BTW's 3x3 crafting station; an empty-handed sneak-click opens 1.20.1's repair menu. No iron or snow golem is built from a pumpkin or jack o'lantern, dispensers included, and a jack o'lantern placed from now on goes out next to water. The enchanting table opens. Fence gates keep 1.20.1's sounds and the sculk game event and update their neighbours. Farmland is trampled by one roll per fall, at 0.75. A beacon or ender chest breaks, burns and explodes without a crash. Doors, trapdoors and gates keep 1.20.1 redstone. Lava: a source meeting water becomes obsidian; flowing lava within two blocks of its source (four in the Nether) becomes a drop-less lava pillow; lava flowing down onto water makes a pillow; thinner and falling lava stays lava; water no longer flows into lava, so a lava fall beside water stays lava; cobblestone and stone generators make pillows |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's anvil crafting on the 1.20.1 anvil, with the repair menu one sneak-click away; 4.B's obsidian and lava pillows on Forge's fluid interactions; 4.B's neighbour updates from fence gates; FC block code that can no longer stop the server |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — a hook FC does not own keeps both bodies where 1.20.1 reaches the base (NOTIFY); 1.20.1 owns the ruled rows outright; the anvil's repair menu, the enchanting table and Forge's other fluid interactions (basalt, other mods') stay |
| **Disables (justification)** | Iron and snow golems can no longer be built from a carved pumpkin or jack o'lantern: 4.B overrides the pumpkin's placement "to prevent vanilla golem creation" (Common FCBlockPumpkinCarved.java:39-43), and decision D3(a) keeps that 4.B rule. Golems that villagers spawn are not touched |
| **Data or code?** | Code: three new mixins (`BlockStateHookMixin`, `FenceGateBlockMixin`, `FluidInteractionRegistryMixin`), injects in four existing ones, and the start-up audit |
| **Alt rejected** | A table of FC rows per block (every future mapping or override would need a row; the general rule plus the audit's WARN for an unclassified pair covers them). Cancellable injects on every hook (each allocates a callback per call on hot paths such as `neighborChanged`; injects exist only where a row resolves to FC). A NONE owner (cancel 1.20.1 and run nothing: its only users were withdrawn by ruling 4) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | For lava: a HEAD answer on `FluidInteractionRegistry.canInteract`, which stops at the first match, so it would have to skip basalt and other mods' lava interactions; restoring the lava from the place event (D14(a)) |
| **Known limitation** | RULED(5) (bed NEIGHBOR is 1.20.1's through `VanillaPairDrops.isRuled`) is the bed-and-door design's recommendation, pending the maintainer's answer to its question Q7; nothing changes in play, since FCBlockBed declares no neighbour hook. Four known block-entity gaps run 1.20.1's body until FC's block entities are bridged (Phase 4): note block USE, furnace USE, and ender chest USE and REMOVE (six until 2026-10-28, when the beacon's were bridged, #053). RESIST is decided since 2026-10-28 (#049). A VANILLA USE owner drops Stage 1 only, so a BlockItem clicked against a spawner, a skull, an end portal or a removed T1 block now places through FC's ItemBlock (no proof row). Lava never fizzes where nothing hardens (4.B fizzed). Breaking a lava pillow is not exercised by any plan. HookPolicy must keep lava's and water's `onPlace` and `neighborChanged` NOTIFY. Queued: P3(iv) lamp, D12(c) crafting-table hardpoint veto, P4 (the door codec, then door USE; cake; workbench; flower pot; the block-entity bridge for the ender chest), P5 (pistons). Done since 2026-10-28: P3(iii) detector rail (#056: every detector rail's INSIDE hook is 1.20.1's equivalent body), the beacon's block-entity bridge (#053), and explosions with RESIST (#049) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-21; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage C; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (be), (bf)): the hook plan P-HOOK 34/36 (20/36 on the tree before wave C; 17/34 on 0292e38, before rows P3g and P3h existed); its two FAILs are the inverted rows P0b and P0c, which recorded the orphaned beacon and ender-chest block entities. Rows: P0a (a broken beacon drops), P2b and P2c (no golems), P2f and P2f2 (the anvil's crafting station; the sneak-click repair menu), P2l (the enchanting table), P2d, P2e, P2i, P4a and P4b (gates, trapdoors and doors on redstone and by hand), P5a and P5b (no trample below 0.75, FC's trample above), P3a to P3h (lava). The crash plan P-HOOK-CRASH 1/1 (TNT beside a beacon; it crashed before), with the smoke checks after it 4/4. `[SELF-AUDIT/hook] 242 blocks, 5 FC-owned and 182 VANILLA hook pairs, rows {ADAPTER(D16b)=[107], ADAPTER(D9b)=[60], RULED(3)=[26], RULED(4)=[64, 71, 96, 107], RULED(5)=[26]}, 6 known block-entity gaps, 0 warnings (0 expected)`; `[LAVA] 4.B lava contact rule installed on Forge's lava+water interaction: ...` at the first fluid update. Refmap: `BlockStateHookMixin`, `FenceGateBlockMixin`, `m_5486_` (canBeReplacedWith), `m_6364_` (spreadTo). No ClassCastException, "invalid for ticking" or "Failed to handle packet" in the logs; the place sweeps now run past the ender chest (1440 -> 4172 lines, none THREW). Build: `Mixin config OK (54 entries)`. 2026-10-28 (round 3; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W5-W8): the hook audit counts 257 blocks, 22 FC-owned and 180 VANILLA hook pairs and 4 known block-entity gaps, after #049 (RESIST on the strata blocks), #050 (the deepslate forms), #051 (the newer woods' planks) and #053 (the beacon's USE and REMOVE); at W10 the vanilla detector rail's INSIDE reads `NOTIFY[equivalent:detector-rail]` (#056) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | FC's blocks keep 4.B's behaviour where FC owns a hook and 1.20.1's where 1.20.1 does, an FC failure no longer stops the server, and lava meeting water makes 4.B's obsidian and pillows |

### #037 — Beds let you sleep but never set the spawn

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 sleep: a bed click at night sleeps, and the night is skipped once enough players sleep; the action-bar refusals (not at night, monsters nearby); the bed explosion in the Nether and the End; `ServerPlayer.startSleepInBed` setting the respawn point on every bed click |
| **Added** | Sleep and bed spawns predate 1.5.2 (4.B removed both); 1.20.1's sleep rules and Forge's `PlayerSetSpawnEvent` |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-19, ruling 3: players may sleep, and a bed never sets the spawn point. A deliberate middle path, neither 4.B (no sleep) nor 1.20.1 (bed spawns) |
| **Conflict** | 4.B removed sleeping: FCBlockBed only prints "You are too troubled to rest." (Client FCBlockBed.java:32-40). At 0292e38 a bed click set the spawn before 1.20.1's time check (ServerPlayer.java:853-854), so a day click set it too, and a night click then threw a ClassCastException in `PlayerMixin.btw$disableBeds` (triage CF2-U1). FC's hostile mobs are ProxyMobs (a Mob, not a Monster), so 1.20.1's monsters-nearby check never saw them |
| **Pattern** | **C** (rebalance, keep the feature), by ruling: beds keep 1.20.1 sleep, and the spawn write is vetoed. Rebalance: no spawn from any bed click |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): beds_sleep_no_spawn`. [`BedSpawnPolicy`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BedSpawnPolicy.java) (BedSpawnPolicy.java:15, :50; `onSetSpawn` :61) cancels a non-forced `PlayerSetSpawnEvent` (posted at the head of `ServerPlayer.setRespawnPosition`, before the write and its message) whose block is a bed (`#minecraft:beds`, or a modded block reporting `isBed`); it reads the block only where the chunk is loaded and never throws. [`ServerPlayerMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ServerPlayerMixin.java)`.btw$fcHostilesPreventRest` (ServerPlayerMixin.java:290-293): at the INVOKE of `Player.startSleepInBed` inside `ServerPlayer.startSleepInBed`, after the time check and 1.20.1's own monster check, a living ProxyMob whose FC entity is an `EntityMob` within 8 blocks across and 5 up or down of the bed answers NOT_SAFE, 1.5.2's rule (vanilla/server EntityPlayer.java:1410-1414); creative players are exempt. `PlayerMixin.btw$disableBeds` is deleted; `PlayerBridge.isPlayerSleeping` reads the real player (PlayerBridge.java:754-763). Bed USE is 1.20.1's by the row RULED(3) of #036, so FCBlockBed's line never runs. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Hook ownership (HookPolicy) |
| **Effect** | A day click sets no spawn and is refused as not night; on a safe night the player sleeps and the night is skipped, with no spawn; a vanilla or FC hostile nearby gives the monsters-nearby refusal; in the Nether and the End the bed explodes. The night skip advances world time at once (ServerLevel.java:278-289), so FC's world-time clocks, the moon phase, gloom nights and Serene Seasons' calendar move with it, as in 1.20.1. A respawn point set on a bed before this change was used for one more respawn, then dropped; since 2026-10-28 it is ignored at the next respawn (#054). Phantoms stay off in new worlds (`doInsomnia=false`, WorldGamerulesHandler.java:58; #002). With Legendary Survival Overhaul, its sleep recovery applies (#031) |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's hostile rule on 1.20.1 sleep: BTW's own monsters keep a player awake. The night skip carries BTW's world-time systems (gloom, the moon, FC's clocks) along with the world |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Partly, by ruling: sleep, the night skip, the messages and the explosion are 1.20.1's, unchanged, and respawn anchors, `/spawnpoint` and every other forced spawn are untouched; bed spawns are removed |
| **Disables (justification)** | Bed spawns, by ruling 3: 4.B had none either (its beds only printed a line, and 4.B respawns players at a bound BTW beacon or else at a random hardcore spawn point, which the port has had since 2026-10-28: #054, triage CF2-U1-HS) |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one Forge event listener and one mixin inject |
| **Alt rejected** | Redirecting the spawn write at ServerPlayer.java:853 (vanilla beds only, and an exclusive redirect; D13(b)). `PlayerSleepInBedEvent` or `SleepingTimeCheckEvent`, which can only refuse sleep, while the spawn is written before the time check (D13(c)); `SleepingLocationCheckEvent`, which decides whether a sleeping player stays asleep (D13(d)). Clearing old bed spawns at login (a chunk read per player) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | `PlayerSleepInBedEvent` for the hostile check (D15(b)): it fires before the range, obstruction and time checks, so a daytime click would answer "monsters nearby" |
| **Known limitation** | A modded bed that reports neither `#minecraft:beds` nor `isBed` still sets the spawn. A forced spawn on a bed (`/spawnpoint`) is kept. Since 2026-10-28 players respawn by 4.B's hardcore respawn (#054) unless a charged anchor, `/spawnpoint` or a bound beacon (#053) applies, and in a world created as creative at their respawn point or the world spawn (before, at the world spawn or a charged respawn anchor; triage CF2-U1-HS). Not runtime-tested: the night skip with several players, or with Serene Seasons or LSO installed |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-21; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage C; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (be)): in P-HOOK, P2g (a day click sets no spawn), P2n (refused by day, no spawn), P2h (a night sleep without a spawn; on 0292e38 the ClassCastException) and P2m (a penned FC zombie gives NOT_SAFE) pass, with the controls P2o (a respawn anchor still sets the spawn) and P2a (a Nether bed explodes). A development client joined to the dedicated server (P-BED-C): the night is skipped and no bed spawn is set. Refmap emits `startSleepInBed`; no ClassCastException in the logs |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Nights can be slept through, but a bed is never a respawn point, as in 4.B |

### #038 — 1.20.1 dyes are colours

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's white, black, brown and blue dye items, which 1.14 split off the 4.B dye subtypes 15, 0, 3 and 4 (bone meal, ink sac, cocoa beans, lapis lazuli), and the recipes that make or use them (cornflower, wither rose and lily of the valley dyes; dyed wool, candles, beds, banners) |
| **Added** | MC 1.14 (the dye split) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-20: modern black, blue and brown dye are colours only; the ink sac (book and quill), lapis (polished lapis) and cocoa beans (chocolate) keep those roles; 1.5.2's subtype stacking is restored (option A). White dye follows #017: only bone meal is fertilizer |
| **Conflict** | The item bridge read the four dyes as the 4.B items, so in FC storage and at FC grids they turned into ink sacs, cocoa beans, lapis and bone meal: a cornflower made lapis, a wither rose ink sacs, and nine blue dyes made a lapis block. Modern-Common's `ItemStack.isItemDamaged` (damage above 0) also stopped subtyped FC items without durability from merging in FC hoppers and chests, so dyes, wool, candles, vases and slabs did not stack there |
| **Pattern** | **C** (rebalance), by ruling: the four dyes keep their colour, and only their colour; the 4.B items keep every role they had |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): modern_dyes` (white dye: #017's `bone_meal`). [`LegacyVariants`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/LegacyVariants.java): `MODERN_SPELLING = 0x100` (LegacyVariants.java:61), `spelling` (:110), the rows (:222-233; white dye at :233 since 2026-10-20), `SPELLED` (:73), `baseSubtype` (:335) and `spelledSubtypes` (:340); a subtype is spelled only when both directions hold. So black, brown, blue and white dye are FC 351:256, 259, 260 and 271, both ways. [`ModernSpellingRecipes`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernSpellingRecipes.java) (ModernSpellingRecipes.java:20, :45; `apply` :66), run at common setup by `BTWForgeMod.commonSetup` (`event.enqueueWork`, BTWForgeMod.java:127-128), gives each spelled dye a twin of every FC colour recipe of its subtype in FC's crafting, anvil and bulk-machine lists. Modern-Common [`ItemDye`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/ItemDye.java) declares subtypes and no durability, as 1.5.2 does, and [`ItemStack`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/ItemStack.java)`.isItemDamaged` (ItemStack.java:111-117) answers false for an item with subtypes and no durability, 1.5.2's rule. Comments in RecipeBridge, WorkbenchFallback and MerchantRecipeList. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Dyes (LegacyVariants spellings, ModernSpellingRecipes) |
| **Effect** | The four dyes keep their identity in BTW storage and at BTW grids, both ways. The colour-family rule on FC's own data: grid twins are appended after FC's list (the existing `fc_recipe_*` ids keep their place), bulk-machine twins are inserted right after their original (FC's first-match order), and metadata-exclusive bulk recipes never take part. One-colour recipes stay with the 4.B item: the book and quill, polished lapis, chocolate milk, chocolate, chicken feed and dog food. The librarian's ink-sac trade refuses black dye. FC's entity colours (sheep, collars, windmills) mask the damage. Black dye passes wicker and slat filters like the other dye powders (D3). BTW grids give blue dye from a cornflower, black dye from a wither rose and white dye from a lily of the valley (#017, #025). Subtyped FC items without durability (dyes, wool, candles, vases, wool slabs) merge in FC hoppers and chests again |
| **BTW adds** | The 1.20.1 dyes join BTW's colour recipes (wool, candles, vases, wool slabs, the filters), and BTW grids turn an ink sac, lapis or cocoa beans into the 1.20.1 dye where a recipe asks for it (#025) |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — every dye item and recipe stays; the 4.B items keep their own roles, and the 1.20.1 dyes gain the colour roles of their 4.B subtype |
| **Data or code?** | Code: a spelling table, and recipe twins built from FC's own lists at common setup |
| **Decisions** | Dye design: D1(a) (the colour-family rule on FC's own data), D2(a) (grid twins appended, bulk twins right after their original), D3(a) (black dye filters like the other dye powders), D4(a), D5(a) (the pass runs at common setup on both sides, so JEI and the client's list see the twins), Q2 option A (subtype stacking). D6(a), its own MOD-bus listener, was registered by Forge but never called (cause not found), so the pass runs from a line in `BTWForgeMod.commonSetup` (D6(b)), and `apply` WARNs when no spelling is registered |
| **Alt rejected** | A hand list of recipes (D1(b)). Twins appended everywhere (D2(b): it breaks 4.B's first-match order in the cauldron), or inserted everywhere (D2(c): it shifts the `fc_recipe_<index>` ids) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Full `isItemDamaged` parity for every item (Q2 option C): un-subtyped wool 35, logs 17, coal 263 and skulls would cross-merge. An NBT marker for the modern spelling (the white-dye design's decision 2(d)) |
| **Known limitation** | Dyes stored in FC containers before this change reload as the ink sac, cocoa beans or lapis (R1; no migration; white dye as bone meal, #017). An older build reads Damage 256, 259 and 260 as item 351 with that damage (R2). In the 2x2 grid FC's colour recipes matched FC candles, wool, vases and wool slabs by item only, so any colour was accepted where 4.B needs the white one (R11); since 2026-10-28 the exact 2x2 match (#025, #051) requires the white one, as 4.B does (Q5 done). The entity colour paths are checked by code reading only (Q3). Cocoa beans versus cocoa powder is an observation (Q4). R3 (black dye not stacking) is not an issue: option A landed. Since 2026-10-28 (#045) the plain block items, ItemMultiTextureTile and the skull item declare 1.5.2's subtype flag too, so their subtypes stack apart in BTW storage, and FC's log trades take only the species they ask for; ItemBlockWithMetadata (stone, cobblestone, ores, snow), coal, spawn eggs, potions, filled maps and BTW's anvil item still lack it (follow-up F-SUBTYPES) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-20; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage BDD; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bc)): the dye plan P-DYE3 60/60 (35/60 before) and the stacking plan P-DYE3Q2 10/10 (6/10 without the `isItemDamaged` change). Log: `[MODERN-DYES] colour twins for the 1.20.1 dye spellings {351=[0, 3, 4, 15]}: crafting +12 ... anvil +0 ... Cauldron +9 ... CauldronStoked +0 ...`, with 8, 1, 1 and 1 one-colour recipes left with the 4.B item. The recipe census (#025): `sweep fc total=675 skipped=3 fail=0`, and `sweep ns minecraft total=708 pass=663 fc=18 shadow=0 fail=27` (32 before this change; scratchpad fu_runs/INV/cur/P-RV.txt and fu_runs/INV/C_after/P-RV.txt). Unchanged on the stage-C tree. 2026-10-28 (round 3, W7): P-DYE3's two R11 lines now read `no 2x2 crafting recipe matches` (4.B), and P-W1 rows M7-M11 prove the white-only match on FC candles and wool (#025) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | The 1.20.1 dyes are colours, and ink, polished lapis and chocolate stay with their 4.B items |

### #039 — Beds and doors break and drop as in 1.20.1

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's "one item, two cells" handling of beds (`BED_PART`) and doors (`DOUBLE_BLOCK_HALF`): the loot comes from one half, `updateShape` pops the partner, and `playerWillDestroy` keeps a creative break from dropping anything |
| **Added** | Beds and doors predate 1.5.2; this handling is 1.20.1's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-20: beds and doors take 1.20.1's drops (BTW's harvest is skipped for them: one item, and a bed keeps its colour), in place of a bed and door codec with 1.5.2's drop rules |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-DR1 and CF2-DR6: FC's harvest dropped legacy 26, 64 or 71 (a red bed, a door) and 1.20.1 popped the partner with its own loot, so a break gave two beds or two doors, the bed's colour was lost, creative breaks dropped items, and iron doors could be melted into iron and gold. The FC saw on a door's top gave two doors; an FC mining charge gave nothing (an empty stub) |
| **Pattern** | **E** (leave vanilla intact), justified by the ruling: 1.20.1 breaks and drops the 16 beds and the oak and iron doors; FC keeps its dig speed, harvest hunger, tool wear and the panic of FC animals |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): vanilla_pair_drops`. [`VanillaPairDrops`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/VanillaPairDrops.java) (VanillaPairDrops.java:27; `isPairBlock` :84; `isRuled` :95: not a ProxyBlock, a `BED_PART` or `DOUBLE_BLOCK_HALF` block with an FC identity; `afterPlayerBreak` :122; `chargeHarvest` :145; `audit` :174, run from BridgeSelfAudit.java:71). [`ServerPlayerGameModeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java)`.btw$destroyBlock` (:225-226) lets vanilla `destroyBlock` run for a ruled block, and `btw$afterPairRemoved` (:216-218, RETURN of Forge's `removeBlock`, `remap = false`, locals captured) runs FC's animal panic. [`BlockPlayerDestroyMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BlockPlayerDestroyMixin.java) (:39-51): FC's 0.025 harvest hunger, with 1.20.1's 0.005 zeroed. [`ItemStackMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ItemStackMixin.java)`.btw$mineBlock` (:353): FC's tool wear on the live FC stack for an FC-identity block (`ProxyItem.onBlockDestroyedLive`, ProxyItem.java:740), in place of 1.20.1's `Item.mineBlock`. Modern-Common `World.UsesModernBlockLoot` (World.java:902-907, false in the base) and its WorldBridge override (WorldBridge.java:3333-3340); Modern-Common Block.java:660-661 (`dropBlockAsItemWithChance`: a ruled live cell drops its own 1.20.1 loot) and :1579-1597 (1.5.2's `DropItemsOnDestroyedByExplosion` body, every block's since #049; a ruled cell drops its 1.20.1 loot through the hook above). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Beds and doors (VanillaPairDrops) |
| **Effect** | Either half gives exactly one bed or door; a bed keeps its colour; creative breaks drop nothing. The FC saw on an oak door's top gives one door; a mining charge on a door, or on a bed whose head it reaches first, now leaves the item. A tool BTW breaks while mining fires Forge's `PlayerDestroyItemEvent`. The 18 ruled blocks are the 16 beds, the oak door (64) and the iron door (71); FC's own door block 1036 is outside the rule. 1.20.1's side effects of the break come with it: the mined-block statistic, the block-destroy game event (sculk sensors hear it) and other mods' loot modifiers on beds and doors |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's dig speed, harvest hunger, tool wear and animal panic on 1.20.1's bed and door breaks; FC mining charges now break beds and doors into their item |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's own break, loot and partner handling run unchanged for the ruled blocks; FC adds only its costs and the panic |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one new mixin, injects in two existing mixins and two Modern-Common hooks |
| **Alt rejected** | A bed codec plus 1.5.2's `BlockBed` and `BlockDoor` drop rules (design D7, the triage's own direction), which the ruling replaced. A field-based panic hook: it is not re-entrant (review L1) |
| **Known limitation** | A door whose top half burns first drops itself (1.20.1; 4.B dropped nothing; Q6). An iron door's top broken by hand drops the door (1.20.1 and 4.B agree; Q1). FC's own door 1036, a ProxyBlock, would still drop twice if placed (not reachable in survival; Q4). A future FC identity on a `DOUBLE_BLOCK_HALF` block would switch its drops to 1.20.1 (the audit WARNs, W1; Q3). A mod that changes the locals of Forge's `removeBlock` fails this mixin at load (`CAPTURE_FAILHARD`), loudly. The wear rule applies to any `ItemStack.mineBlock` caller with an FC item on an FC-identity block (Q10). Bed NEIGHBOR is 1.20.1's through #036's RULED(5), pending Q7. Mining charges on every other block were CF2-DR5, in its own slot (Q9): since 2026-10-28 they run FC's blast body (#049) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-20; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage BDD; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bb)): the bed and door plan P-BEDDOOR 288/288 (all 63 gap checks flip; 225/288 on the tree before), over survival and creative breaks of both halves of the 16 beds and both doors, TNT, the saw, mining charges, pistons, a protection cancel and the harvest hunger. `[SELF-AUDIT/pairdrops] 18 ruled blocks (16 BedBlock, 2 DoorBlock, 0 other); FC drops of ids [26, 64, 71] at a live cell take that cell's 1.20.1 loot; 0 warnings (0 expected)`. Build: `Mixin config OK (51 entries)`. Every regression plan the same; unchanged on the stage-C tree. Not runtime-tested: the FC animal panic (no proof verb reads it), `PlayerDestroyItemEvent` on a bed or door |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | One bed or door per break, at BTW's harvest costs |

### #040 — BTW placement follows 4.B's validity rules

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 block placement (`BlockItem.place`, `getStateForPlacement`) beside the port's Stage 2, where an FC ItemBlock places the held block through FC's code |
| **Added** | Placement predates 1.5.2; `BlockItem.place` and `getStateForPlacement` are 1.20.1's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-20: other mods' fake players keep 4.B's mid-air rule (a known issue). 2026-10-23: 4.B's isOpaqueCube, renderAsNormalBlock and normal-cube answers are restored on the Modern-Common base classes, and leaves always count as opaque (4.B's server answer) |
| **Conflict** | `WorldBridge.canPlaceEntityOnSide` was a stub (triage CF2-P8a): no free or replaceable target test and no `canPlaceBlockOnSide`, so flowers went onto stone and slabs and tripwire hooks into solid cells, while the one-argument `checkNoEntityCollision` counted dropped items. A refused Stage 2 fell back to 1.20.1's BlockItem. The facing rewrite after every placement read the held stack, so a block placed from the last item of a stack was never rotated, 1.20.1's own stairs, doors, gates, beds and observers came out reversed, and a click that placed nothing rotated the block already there. The tripwire hook had no metadata codec. FC refused swimming or riding players (FC's `inWater` and riding state were never synced; triage CF2-I5). FC used up an oak door without placing it (an empty `ItemDoor.placeDoorBlock`). An FC-placed oak trapdoor or gate against a live signal stayed closed, and FC-placed logs stood upright whatever side was clicked |
| **Pattern** | **D** (closest; 4.B parity restored in port code, as #015): FC's placements run 4.B's own validity rules, and 1.20.1's placements are left to 1.20.1. Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): placement_validity`. [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java): `canPlaceEntityOnSide` (WorldBridge.java:2229; 1.5.2's World.java:3377-3407 with the port's corrections: a cell FC does not model is free by 1.20.1's own replaceable rule, and a throw refuses; the placing player counts, :2239-2240); `checkNoEntityCollision` with one and two arguments (:2148, :2167: entities that block building, 1.5.2's `preventEntitySpawning`; spectators skipped; a failed scan refuses); `isMetaFullyDerived` (:304, now with the tripwire hook) and `getBlockMetadata` (:235: a fully derived block never reads the metadata overlay); the hook codec (:569, :884: the direction, plus 4 when attached and 8 when powered). Modern-Common [`BlockTripWireSource`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/BlockTripWireSource.java) (BlockTripWireSource.java:38-60: 1.5.2's `canPlaceBlockOnSide`, `canPlaceBlockAt` and `onBlockPlaced`) and [`BlockLog`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/BlockLog.java)`.onBlockPlaced` (BlockLog.java:69). [`ServerPlayerGameModeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java): a REFUSED Stage 2 at the clicked block resends the held slot, closes the click frame and answers FAIL (:398); the facing rewrite runs only on a PLACED record, never on a hook, and only to a facing the block survives in (:491-515); the trapdoor and gate power sync (:517-531). [`ProxyBlock`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyBlock.java)`.tryPlaceHeldItem` (ProxyBlock.java:617): an FC door item (item 324) is declined, so 1.20.1's DoorItem places the door; for an item of the 4.B ItemBlock family it records PLACED with the cell, or REFUSED for a player who may build. [`PlayerBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/PlayerBridge.java): `Stage2` (:413), `setStage2` (:484), `stage2At` (:498), the `inWater` sync (:226), `canPlayerEdit` for a riding player (:917). Since 2026-10-25 the Modern-Common base classes answer 1.5.2's block properties: each carries its 1.5.2 class's `isOpaqueCube`, `renderAsNormalBlock`, `getRenderType`, `getRenderBlockPass`, `getBlocksMovement` and `getMobilityFlag` where the port answered otherwise (81 methods on 40 classes); a normal cube is an opaque material rendered as a normal block unless the FC class answers `IsNormalCube` itself (Modern-Common Block.java:1164), and FC's World asks the block (`World.isBlockNormalCube`, `WorldBridge.isBlockNormalCube`, WorldBridge.java:1138); the pane and wall connection rules consult those answers as 1.5.2's do (`BlockPane.canThisPaneConnectToThisBlockID`, `BlockWall.canConnectWallTo`); `BlockLeaves` keeps 4.B's server answer, always opaque. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Use order (FC item step and Stage 2) and Placement validity (WorldBridge) |
| **Effect** | FC's placements follow 4.B: the target must be free or replaceable and the new block must accept the side (a plant its soil, a hook its wall, a rail its support); only entities that block building obstruct the new block (items and XP orbs no longer do; the placing player does); and the mid-air rule's refusal is final. A `/give`'d tripwire hook places as in 4.B, on the side of a full opaque block, facing away from it and never turned by the player's look. Falling sand no longer deletes rails. Stairs, doors, beds, gates and observers placed the 1.20.1 way are no longer reversed. The oak door is placed by 1.20.1's DoorItem. FC-placed logs, the four newer woods included (#034), lie along the clicked side. An FC-placed oak trapdoor or fence gate against a live signal opens and is powered, as 1.20.1 places it (ruling 4). Facing no longer depends on the stack size. Windmills and water wheels are refused where a mob or player stands in their bounds. FC squids treat a swimming player as in water. Flowers that earlier versions placed on stone or slabs pop and drop their item, as in 4.B (CF2-P8b). Since 2026-10-25, with 1.5.2's block answers: BTW's grates, wicker panes and slats collide and outline as panes and no longer join doors, beds or other pane kinds; walls have arms for BTW mobs; BTW mobs walk over pressure plates and past signs; a BTW chest opens with glass on top; BTW torches and other FC attachables lose glowstone, redstone lamps, doors, beds and panes as supports, as in 4.B (and 1.20.1's torch and ladder placement asks the same rule, and since 2026-10-31 its snow layer, pressure plate and rail placement too, #055); BTW panes' items are flat icons |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's placement rules, its tripwire hook placement included, on BTW's placement of 1.20.1 items |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's own placement (`BlockItem.place`) is unchanged for everything that does not go through FC's ItemBlock family; sneak placement and other mods' items keep 1.20.1's rules, apart from #055's support veto |
| **Data or code?** | Code: WorldBridge, two Modern-Common ports, the click frame's Stage 2 record and the item step |
| **Alt rejected** | Keeping 1.20.1's BlockItem fallback after FC refused: it placed what 4.B refuses. Fixing the tripwire hook's routing alone: the recorded miss was the proof plan's geometry, and the real defect was every FC placement skipping 4.B's checks |
| **Known limitation** | FC-route stairs still face the wrong way until the facing rewrite is retired (6(c); row H14 KNOWN). Sneak placement uses 1.20.1's rules (8(b)), except that torches, soul torches, ladders, snow layers, pressure plates, rails and FC's own blocks of those families take #055's support veto. FC placements post no `EntityPlaceEvent` to other mods (9(b)). FC-route trapdoors take metadata 0 (look-based facing, bottom half) until decision 3's second stage. An FC-placed hook shows as attached only after a neighbour update (4(b')). Other mods' fake players that call `useItemOn` are never ticked, so FC's mid-air rule refuses their 4.B ItemBlock-family placements on FC-modelled blocks (kept by the maintainer's ruling; Create's deployer calls `useOn` directly and is not affected). Not runtime-tested: swimming or riding players, a real player's own collision box, adventure mode's CanPlaceOn, a hook saved before this change. With 1.5.2's block answers (2026-10-25): corner, T and cross panes collide as the box of their arms, not 1.5.2's two thin boxes; the BTW door 1036 (commands only) takes its bounds from the stubbed `getFullMetadata` until the door codec; FC entities collide with 1.20.1 walls and panes by 4.B's connection rules while 1.20.1 draws them joined; Block-level face culling (`ShouldRenderNeighborFullFaceSide`) is a follow-up (FU-RANB-CULL) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (follow-ups, 2026-10-21; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, stage C; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bd)): the placement plan P-PLACE 27/29 (8/29 before; the two FAILs are the KNOWN rows H4 and H14), including P8-1 to P8-5 (a poppy refused on stone and on a smooth stone slab, a dandelion on cobblestone; the controls on deepslate and grass; triage CF2-P8a). A development client joined to the dedicated server (P-PLACE-C): a hook clicked onto a block's top is refused and the client's hand shows it kept (`hand minecraft:tripwire_hook x1`). The place sweeps now run to the end (1440 -> 4172 lines, none THREW; #036); of the 1436 lines both runs have, 52 changed, each by a 1.5.2 rule: logs on a north face lie along z; on stone's top, saplings, grass, fern, dead bush, all flowers, mushrooms, ladders and cactus are refused; on a north face over grass, dead bush, cactus and mushrooms are refused (their soil and light rules); twisting vines' age is random, and the lily pad's reply changed with no block either way. Every other regression plan the same. 2026-10-25/26 (round 3, W1; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W1): the block-answer plan P-RANB 117/117 (45/117 on 4558220), and P-PLACE 28/29: row H4 (glass refused as a support) now passes, since 1.5.2's normal-cube answer makes glass no full cube, so its known issue is gone; H14 stays KNOWN |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | BTW placement obeys 4.B's rules again: nothing is placed where 4.B would refuse |

### #041 — Block quads take their plane's normal where FC set none; per-meta digging particles; 4.B's breaking crack

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's block-model lighting and breaking crack, which read each baked quad's direction and normal: the face shade (`ClientLevel.getShade`), smooth lighting (`ModelBlockRenderer` ambient occlusion, Forge's per-quad switch) and the crumbling pass, which re-tessellates each cracking block every frame into a `SheetedDecalTextureGenerator` that projects the crack by position on the axis nearest each quad's packed normal, and lays the same decal on every crumbling-capable buffer of a block entity's renderer; and the digging particle taken from the block model (`BlockModelShaper.getTexture`) |
| **Added** | MC 1.8 (baked models); the normal-driven crack decal and Forge's per-quad ambient-occlusion flag are 1.20.1's and Forge's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-22: the breaking crack on BTW's web, and on every BTW block 4.B cracked differently, takes 4.B's exact look through an extra renderer patch, not 1.20.1's cobweb bake (OD2 (b)). 2026-10-23: the crack images stay 1.20.1's `destroy_stage_0..9` textures (OD-C3 (a)). The quad normals and the particles follow 4.B with no ruling needed |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-R3. 1.5.2 meshed chunks without GL lighting and shaded faces through vertex colour, so FC's renderers never set normals for chunk quads. The port's capture kept a sticky normal (UP from `runFcRender`, an UP set on vertical planes by the crossed-square, crop and cross-hatch ports, or the last face's normal), and FCBakedModel turned it into each quad's direction and packed normal. BTW's web (crossed planes marked UP), ladders (FC's `RenderLadder`, no normal), panes and crop planes therefore showed a crack smeared into vertical streaks, and every such quad was smooth-lit, or shaded by the wrong face. 4.B drew the crack by running the block's own `RenderBlockWithTexture` with the destroy icon every frame (RenderGlobal.drawBlockDamageTexture): the crack lay on each face with that face's own UVs, on the live geometry, and never on tile-entity geometry. 1.20.1's projection stretches and crops it on the web's diagonal planes, mirrors it on the bottom, west and east faces of BTW cubes and on west- and east-facing ladders, and lays it on the static quads, overlays and fire planes included, and on FC TESR geometry. Every BTW block also shed meta 0's top-icon particles, where 1.5.2 used `getIcon(0, meta)` |
| **Pattern** | **D** (closest; 4.B parity restored in port code, as #015 and #040). Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_quad_normals` and `fc_crack_4b`. [`FCBakedModel`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FCBakedModel.java)`.convertCapturedQuad` (FCBakedModel.java:1225): a world quad (static or live capture) keeps its tessellator normal only if it is the quad's own (`planeNormalUnlessOwn`, :1300: the diagonals' cross product, cos >= 0.9999); otherwise it is baked as 1.20.1 bakes its own cross, crop and ladder faces: `Direction.getNearest` of the plane, the normal Forge's `fillNormal` packs (the direction's axis, or the exact plane normal under `calculateAllNormals`), no face shade and no ambient occlusion. Item captures keep their normals. A per-state `StateView` (:1568, a `BakedModelWrapper`), put on every state by [`BTWClientEvents`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/BTWClientEvents.java) (:296), gives each meta its `getIcon(0, meta)` particle. The crack: [`BlockRenderDispatcherCrackMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/client/BlockRenderDispatcherCrackMixin.java) (client; cancellable HEAD of Forge's `renderBreakingTexture`) asks [`FCBreakingCrack`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FCBreakingCrack.java): a block whose model is FCBakedModel (or its StateView) is cracked by `FCBakedModel.renderCrack` (:709), which runs FC's `RenderBlockWithTexture` with the named icon "destroy" (`CRACK_ICON`, :683) through `runFcRender` over the live world and writes the quads drawn with that icon straight into the decal's buffer ([`SheetedDecalAccessor`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/client/SheetedDecalAccessor.java)); 1.20.1's crack is drawn instead only if FC throws (one WARN per block id). [`FCBlockEntityRenderer`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FCBlockEntityRenderer.java)`.render` hands FC TESRs the buffers without the crack decal (`FCBreakingCrack.withoutCrumbling`, FCBreakingCrack.java:55, through [`VertexMultiConsumerDoubleAccessor`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/client/VertexMultiConsumerDoubleAccessor.java)). Modern-Common [`Block`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/Block.java)`.RenderBlockWithTexture` (Block.java:1339) is 1.5.2's body verbatim; the comments of `Block.RenderCrossHatch` and `RenderBlocks.renderCrossedSquares` / `renderBlockCrops` are corrected (no code change). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Block model quads and the breaking crack (FCBakedModel, FCBreakingCrack) |
| **Effect** | Every BTW block FC draws cracks exactly as in 4.B: the web's crossed planes carry the crack edge to edge like its texture, ladders and cube faces in 1.5.2's own orientation, culled faces and connected shapes as they stand in the world; BTW's wicker panes, grates and slats crack on their own pane geometry, not on a full cube (their 1.5.2 `renderAsNormalBlock` false, #040); no crack on campfire fire, overlays or block-entity parts (basket lids, placed tools, cooking items). Vanilla blocks keep 1.20.1's crack. BTW quads drawn without their own normal (crossed plants, crop hatches, panes, ladders, cement sides, fire planes) are lit flat and unshaded, as 4.B drew them and as 1.20.1 bakes its own plant, ladder, vine and torch models; every face with a set normal is unchanged, byte for byte, and no block's world quads change for the crack. Damaged webs and every variant block shed their own particles |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's look on 1.20.1's lighting and crumbling pass: flat-lit plant, ladder and pane planes, 4.B's crack geometry, and per-variant digging particles |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's lighting, its crack images and its crack on every block FC does not draw are unchanged; BTW's quads give 1.20.1's lighting correct input |
| **Data or code?** | Code: client classes and their registration, three client mixins, one Modern-Common port, comment corrections |
| **Alt rejected** | Fixing each renderer's `setNormal`: case by case, and FC code is read-only (FC's own `RenderLadder` and `RenderPane` set none). Shade off only for non-axis quads: ladders, crops and panes would keep the smear, or be darkened to 0.6/0.8. Always packing the exact plane normal: on exact 45-degree planes the crack plane would then depend on Forge's `stabilizeDirectionGetNearest` option, where vanilla's cobweb does not. Per-meta particles through ModelData: an allocation per FC block per chunk rebuild |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Keeping 1.20.1's decal with the corrected normals (stretched, cropped and mirrored; ruled out). Quads supplied through `getQuads` (the decal discards their UVs). A per-quad decal-UV override from each model quad's sprite-relative UV (1.20.1's geometry, overlays and fire would still crack, and the mixin is wider). A LevelRenderer `@Redirect` (exclusive against other redirects). `RenderLevelStageEvent` (cannot remove 1.20.1's decal). A per-position crack cache (the neighbours are live) |
| **Known limitation** | The crack stage images are 1.20.1's, so resource packs change them (ruling 2026-10-23). A block whose model another mod wraps keeps 1.20.1's crack over the corrected quads. The plane test is geometric: a quad drawn with no normal of its own whose plane happens to face the inherited normal stays smooth-lit (a flat wet-cement top beside a sloped one), and a correct normal on a reversed winding is turned around (none known). FC tile-entity geometry keeps its captured normals for lighting. Untested with Embeddium, Oculus or shader packs |
| **Verified by** | Development-client proofs (round 3, 2026-10-25/26; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W1; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bg)): the crack plan P-CRACK-C at stage AB (tally GATE OK: K3 passes, the crack rows fail as designed until the crack patch) and at stage ABC (GATE OK: K1-K5, KG and KB pass; KB reads `lit=72 crack=0`, the open wicker basket's 72 lid quads uncracked, where 4558220 read `lit=72 crack=72`; KG, the grate cracking on its own post, needs #040's pane answers). The quad dump of 4558220 against stage AB passes its gate, with 96 converted quads whose captured normal was anti-parallel to their plane, 636 whose face shade changes and 674 per-meta particle sprites reviewed; stage AB against ABC: 36943 lines, 0 differ. Build: `Mixin config OK (58 entries ...)`; refmap `SheetedDecalAccessor` `f_85867_`, `VertexMultiConsumerDoubleAccessor` `f_86171_` / `f_86172_`. Every regression plan the same apart from P-PLACE's row H4 (#040); wood_c1's block-sprite lines now name `FCBakedModel$StateView`, as planned |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None (visual): BTW blocks light, crack and shed particles as in 4.B |

### #042 — BTW's own particles are 1.20.1 particle types with 4.B's limits; one table for FC's particle names

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's particle system: registered `ParticleType`s with client `ParticleProvider`s and JSON texture lists, and the limiter that culls particles beyond 32 blocks and honours the Particles setting unless a type overrides it |
| **Added** | MC 1.13 (registered particle types); the limiter as 1.20.1 has it |
| **Maintainer ruling** | None needed: 4.B parity, the round's default |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-R5b: FC's client display code reached three different name tables. The block table (`ProxyBlock.mapParticle`, also used by FC tile entities' client ticks) drew FC's white cloud and white smoke as 1.20.1 campfire smoke, a large grey puff that lives 80-129 ticks and overrides the limiter, so loose snow, campfires, kilns, cauldrons, crucibles, smouldering logs and drying bricks smoked grey even at Minimal; it had no entry for FC's candle flame, ignored the detector block's dust colour and mapped item breaks to snowballs. The aux-FX and server tables drew FC's smoke as vanilla clouds that never rise and its cinders as ash. The port's block-entity renderer also spawned its own forced campfire smoke every frame over cauldrons and crucibles on a fire and over lit campfires holding food.
