And 1.20.1's limiter drew FC's particles up to 32 blocks away and its overload puff at every setting, where 4.B stopped at 16 blocks and honoured the setting |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's own four FX classes and 1.5.2's snow-shovel puff are ported as BTW particle types on 1.20.1's particle framework; every other 1.5.2 name maps to its 1.20.1 descendant; 4.B's `RenderGlobal` limit sits in front of 1.20.1's `LevelRenderer` for FC's client-side calls. Rebalance: none (4.B's limits) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_particles`. [`FcParticles`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcParticles.java): the one table of FC's 1.5.2 particle names and 4.B's limit class per name (`limit`, FcParticles.java:137: explosions anywhere, FC's cinders within 32 blocks at every setting, every other name within 16 blocks and by the setting), `addLocal` (:168) for the client worlds, `send` (:196) for server-side FC calls, `audit` (:210, `[SELF-AUDIT/particles]`, run from BridgeSelfAudit.runCommon). [`FcParticleTypes`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcParticleTypes.java): `betterthanwolves:white_smoke`, `white_cloud`, `small_flame`, `cinders` (overrides the limiter) and `snow_shovel`, registered by BTWForgeMod, with JSON texture lists (vanilla sprites) in `assets/betterthanwolves/particles/`. Client `btw.forge.client.particle`: [`LegacyParticleLimiter`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/particle/LegacyParticleLimiter.java) (4.B's limit, then `LevelRenderer.addParticle` with 4.B's force flag), `LegacyFxParticle` (1.5.2 EntityFX geometry and FC's moveEntity), the five particles and [`FcParticleProviders`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/particle/FcParticleProviders.java), installed by BTWClientEvents. The sinks: FC's display ticks (ProxyBlock.java:1224), FC tile entities' client ticks (ProxyBlockEntity.java:218), ClientFcWorld's aux-FX and client notifications (ClientFcWorld.java:310), WorldBridge's server-side calls (WorldBridge.java:2531). FCBlockEntityRenderer spawns no particles (FCBlockEntityRenderer.java:104), and `ProxyBlock.mapParticle` is gone. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, FC particles (FcParticles) |
| **Effect** | Loose snow gives off faint white wisps; campfires cooking food, kilns, cauldrons and crucibles on a fire, smouldering logs, mill stones and drying bricks give off white smoke that rises 1-3 blocks, and nothing else; candles burn with FC's small flame; a bursting smouldering log throws glowing cinders that show at any Particles setting within 32 blocks; a snow golem made with a soul urn shows 1.5.2's falling snow puffs; a powered detector block shows its own dim red dust. BTW blocks' particles show within 16 blocks and follow the Particles setting, as in 4.B; only explosions and the cinders ignore it, and an overloaded gearbox's puff no longer shows at Minimal |
| **BTW adds** | FC's particles become first-class 1.20.1 particles (`/particle betterthanwolves:white_smoke`), usable by other mods and retexturable by resource packs |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — vanilla particles and their limiter are untouched; BTW adds five particle types and limits only FC's names |
| **Data or code?** | Code plus five JSON texture lists (vanilla sprites) |
| **Alt rejected** | Mapping FC's names to vanilla `cloud`, `small_flame` and `lava` (the triage's minimum): the smoke would not rise, the cinders would spread less and vanish at Minimal, and the snow puffs have no counterpart. Keeping 1.20.1's 32-block cull and forced `poof` for FC's names: a departure from 4.B with no ruling |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Routing the renderer's own smoke emitters through the table: FC's display ticks already give 4.B's smoke for the same blocks, so it would double. Wiring FC entities' client-only particles here: it needs a decision on the server forwarding for entity calls first, or particles double (follow-up) |
| **Known limitation** | FC entities' client-only particles (the smoke of a falling smouldering log or burning netherrack, #043; villager and witch effects) are still dropped by the entity client world (`STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (ab) caveat; follow-up FU-PE). Particles the port forwards from server-side FC code (item breaks, hearts; 4.B's server sent none) follow 1.20.1's limiter: 32 blocks |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-25/26; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W1; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bh)): the table plan P-R5B-T 32/32 (19/32 on 4558220) and the client plan P-R5B-C 35/35 with screenshots (16/35 on 4558220). Every regression plan the same apart from P-PLACE's row H4 (#040) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None (visual): BTW's blocks look as in 4.B and respect the player's particle setting |

### #043 — BTW falling blocks fall on the client, as in 4.B

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's entity sync: Forge's spawn packet with `IEntityAdditionalSpawnData`, `ServerEntity`'s periodic position updates applied by `Entity.lerpTo` as a snap, block changes broadcast at the start of the next tick, entity-type lighting of entity renders, and `EntityType` persistence (`noSave`) |
| **Added** | Entity tracking predates 1.5.2; these mechanics are 1.20.1's and Forge's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | OD1, 2026-10-22: a block saved mid-fall is restored: it reloads, keeps falling and lands by FC's rules (4.B lost it) |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-R1. The client built an FC entity only through a `(World)` or no-argument constructor. `FCEntityFallingBlock` has neither; 4.B built it from Packet23 with its 6-argument constructor. So every falling block (loose dirt, cobble, slabs, stairs, snow, and sand, gravel and the dragon egg since 9c05019) drew as an orange debug box, the proxy moved only on the 3-tick position updates, and the landed block reached the client a tick after the entity's removal (a blink). A block saved mid-fall loaded as an inert orange box that stayed for ever. `[SELF-AUDIT/renderer]` passed. Below y = 0 a burning netherrack fell without its fire: FC finds its row with `(int) posY` (Server FCBlockNetherrackFalling.java:70), which truncates toward zero, so FC looked for the fire two cells above the block (4.B never has y < 0) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (closest; 4.B's client-side falling block restored in port code, as #015 and #040): the client builds, simulates and draws FC's own falling block, and the server keeps FC's landing rules. Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_entity_construction` and `falling_blocks`. [`FcEntityFactory`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcEntityFactory.java): the constructor rule ((World), else no-argument, else positional with the proxy's position and zeros, then the state), used by ProxyEntity and the three mob proxies, each with a no-retry flag. [`ProxyEntity`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyEntity.java): `ensureFcEntity(withState)` (ProxyEntity.java:107); a positional entity is rebuilt on the server only from its saved FCData (`REBUILD_SAVED_POSITIONAL`, :64, ruling OD1), and one without state is removed at the server's first tick (:412); the client simulates FC's fall (:452) and its `lerpTo` / `lerpMotion` (:383, :397) keep a server update that fits that fall; `syncFromFc` (:340) sends the landed cell before the removal. [`FCEntityRenderer`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FCEntityRenderer.java): the audit checks client construction and builds positional classes once (:139); falling-block captures are shaded by block face (:441). [`BTWEntityRegistration`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BTWEntityRegistration.java): `fc_lightning_bolt` is `noSave` (:183-185). Modern-Common `Block.ShouldRenderWhileFalling` (Block.java:1443), `RenderBlocks.RenderStandardFallingBlock` (RenderBlocks.java:2577) and `RenderFallingSand`'s dragon-egg branch: the 1.5.2 code. Load-time [`FCBlockNetherrackFallingMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCBlockNetherrackFallingMixin.java) (`@Pseudo`, `remap = false`): the first `posY` read of `onStartFalling` answers `Math.floor(posY)`, the value FC's `(int)` cast has at every 4.B height. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Plain FC entities (ProxyEntity, FcEntityFactory) |
| **Effect** | Falling blocks are textured, shaded like placed blocks and move every tick without hitches. A falling slab, stairs or dragon egg keeps its shape. A falling block is hidden while its own block still stands in its cell, and the landed block appears with the entity's removal, with no blink. Falling blocks can be targeted, as in 1.5.2 and 1.20.1. A block saved mid-fall is restored and lands. Old inert falling-block entities and summoned ones without state are removed. A BTW lightning bolt is never saved. A burning netherrack that starts to fall below y = 0 keeps its fire and relights it where it lands, as at every 4.B height |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's client-side falling block, with its shape, shading and hiding rules, in place of a debug box |
| **Dual-sourced?** | No: 1.20.1's `FallingBlockEntity` is not used for BTW falls; its landing order and shading are mirrored |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one new class, Forge edits, three Modern-Common 1.5.2 ports and one load-time patch on an FC class |
| **Alt rejected** | Spawning 1.20.1's `FallingBlockEntity` (loses FC's landing rules). A `(World)` constructor on `FCEntityFallingBlock` (FC code is read-only). An interpolating `lerpTo` without client simulation (trails the server by about 3 ticks and vanishes above the ground). Client simulation with every update applied (hitches whenever an update lands a tick off the client's phase). 1.5.2's face shades in `RenderStandardFallingBlock` (misses the loose snow, netherrack and egg paths, which draw through `renderStandardBlock`) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Dropping a block saved mid-fall, as 4.B did (ruled out by OD1). A general rewrite of FC's truncating y casts: the other 14 sites are sound or particle positions, spawn checks that are false below 0 either way, or exact for an entity standing on a full block, so each needs its own proof rows first |
| **Known limitation** | Falling smouldering logs and burning netherrack give no smoke while they fall: the entity client world still drops FC entities' client-only particles (#042; follow-up FU-PE). FC's 1.5.2 void rule still turns a fall of more than 100 ticks that ends below y = 1 into an item (follow-up FU-VOID). No ambient occlusion on a falling block; a burning netherrack's embers are not full-bright while falling. The pick box sits 0.49 above the drawn block (the yOffset follow-up FU-YOFF, #044). Not runtime-tested: the falling bucket, lava receiver, smouldering log and loose snow slab renderers |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-26/27; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W2 and W2 fix; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bi)): P-FALL 18/18 (11/18 on 4558220); P-FALL-RESTART A 2/2 and B 2/2 (0/2 on 4558220: a block saved mid-fall reloads and lands); the client plan P-FALL-C 16/16 with its probes: 7 falling blocks built and drawn (0 and 0 on 4558220), a falling slab 0.5 high, the dragon egg's 48 quads, a smooth fall (0 stalls, 0 irregular steps, 0 of 78 server updates applied), no landing blink (4 on 4558220), a cow drawn. P-FALL's server.log: no `Could not create FC entity`, no `saved while falling`, no failed reads, no `onUpdate() threw`. The burning netherrack: DIAG-K3D 6/6 (the fire relit, D3 and D6 failed before) and P-FALL-C's K3d `meta=1 quads=7 height 1.0` (meta 0 before). Build: `Mixin config OK (59 entries ...)`. Every regression plan the same, apart from P-BM's flaky row P15a, which #060 fixed |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None on gameplay; visual, persistence and targeting only |

### #044 — BTW's drops leave through the face you dig, from inside the broken block

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's dig protocol (`ServerPlayerGameMode.handleBlockBreakAction`: every START and STOP dig packet carries the face, and a STOP short of 0.7 progress leaves the break to `tick()`) and 1.20.1's dropped item (`ItemEntity`: its position is the box bottom, and `tick` pushes it out of any block its box overlaps, without collision) |
| **Added** | The sequenced dig protocol and the delayed destroy are 1.19's; the feet position and the push rule postdate 1.5.2, whose items were pushed only from a normal cube (FC's FCMOD restored that) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | None needed: 4.B parity. 1.20.1's item push physics stays (triage CF2-DR2 (C)), as for the Hardcore Buoy known issue |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-DR2. `destroyBlock(BlockPos)` carries no face, so the port ran FC's harvest with face 1 (UP) for every break, and every FC conversion (stone and rough stone under a pickaxe that cannot harvest them, staged ores, a hoe on grass, chewed logs and the work stump) ejected into the block above: in a tunnel the drops went sideways through the rock or up through the ceiling, and on a trunk the bark left a random side of the log above. `EntityProxyFactory.createItemEntity` also used FC's `posY` (1.5.2's box centre) as 1.20.1's feet, so every FC drop started 0.125 too high and about one block drop in seven began inside the block above |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): 4.B's face and drop box restored at the bridge. Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_dig_face` and `fc_drop_position`. [`ServerPlayerGameModeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java) implements [`DigFaceAccess`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/DigFaceAccess.java): HEAD and RETURN injects on `handleBlockBreakAction` (`m_214168_`; ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.java:164, :178) record the START or STOP packet's face for the cell it digs while the call runs, and keep a STOP's face when that call scheduled the delayed destroy; INVOKE and AFTER injects around `tick`'s (`m_7712_`) `destroyBlock` call hand it to the delayed break (:190, :197); `btw$destroyBlock` reads [`DigFace`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/DigFace.java)`.of` (:235) before the BreakEvent and passes it to `tryHarvestBlock` (:280). `DigFace.of` (DigFace.java:41): the dig packet's face, else the face the breaker's look ray hits, else UP; `DigFace.audit` (`[SELF-AUDIT/digface]`) from BridgeSelfAudit.runCommon. [`FCLogRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FCLogRules.java)`.hitFace` (FCLogRules.java:529) delegates to it, so the log rule (#034) and Dynamic Trees (#027) chew toward the same face. [`EntityProxyFactory`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/EntityProxyFactory.java)`.createItemEntity` (EntityProxyFactory.java:229): feet = `posY - yOffset` (:245, the `ItemEntityBridge` convention), except that a drop whose 1.5.2 box collides while the box at `posY` does not keeps `posY` (entity drops centred on a floor). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Dig face and drop position (DigFace, EntityProxyFactory) |
| **Effect** | Conversion drops come out of the face being dug, toward the player, 0.2-0.3 outside it, as in 4.B: the face of the dig packet that breaks the block (START for an instant break, the finishing STOP otherwise, also when the server finishes the break a few ticks after the client). In a 1x2 tunnel the stone and gravel land in the tunnel; on a trunk the bark lands on the player's side, which closes #034's upward chew-eject. Other breaks (other mods' fake players and machines, a vein miner's extra blocks) eject toward the face the breaker looks at, else up. BTW's block drops start inside the broken block's space (centre 0.15-0.85 up, top at most 0.975); entity drops still start on the ground |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's eject direction and drop height on 1.20.1's dig protocol and item entity |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's dig protocol, reach and protection checks, sequence acks and item physics are unchanged; BTW reads the face and places its own drops |
| **Data or code?** | Code: four injects and a `btw$destroyBlock` edit in an existing mixin, two new bridge classes, one bridge method each in EntityProxyFactory and FCLogRules |
| **Alt rejected** | The look ray alone (FCLogRules.hitFace's old rule): the server's rotation can lag the dig packet, and a delayed break finishes ticks later. A per-player "last face" read by any `destroyBlock`: a direct caller would inherit a stale face. The triage's blanket `posY - yOffset`: every entity drop (centred on the mob's feet) would start 0.125 in the floor and 1.20.1 would push it sideways. Changing `applyPositionFromFc` as well: proxies sync `posY` with their feet both ways every tick |
| **Known limitation** | 1.20.1 still pushes an item out of any block it overlaps, through rock, and items float as in 1.20.1 (Hardcore Buoy). FC plain entities with a non-zero yOffset (dynamite, mining charges, floating items, XP orbs, primed TNT, falling blocks, platforms, anchors, windmills) keep a 1.20.1 box yOffset above FC's (follow-up FU-YOFF). FC edits to a dropped item after it spawned (a sheared sheep's wool velocity) still do not reach it. Not runtime-tested: staged ores, grass under a hoe, the work stump, the chewed log's later stages (the same eject call as the rows proven) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-27/28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W3; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bj)): the dig plan P-DR2 19/19 (9/19 on 4558220), over a bare-hand chew dug north, a break finished by a STOP facing west, one finished in `tick()` with a STOP facing east (also after a START elsewhere), a direct `destroyBlock` looking east, the top face, creative, a stone pickaxe on both blocks of a 1x2 tunnel face, FC drops under a ceiling, on and inside a floor, and 300 FC dirt harvests under a ceiling (none above y+1); the client plan P-DR2-C 2/2 (1/2). `[SELF-AUDIT/digface] ServerPlayerGameMode carries the dig face`; refmap `m_214168_`, `m_7712_`, `m_9280_`, and the shadows `hasDelayedDestroy` / `delayedDestroyPos` reobfuscated to `f_9253_` / `f_9254_` in the class. Rerun at W6: 19/19 (a first batch run there counted two stray items outside the dug cells within its 300-harvest window). Every regression plan the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Early mining and chewing drop at the player's side again, never into the rock |

### #045 — Flowers keep their kind; BTW's drops and storage keep the block's identity

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | The ten small flowers 1.20.1 has beside the poppy and the dandelion that are relatives of 4.B's rose (blue orchid, allium, azure bluet, red, orange, white and pink tulip, oxeye daisy, cornflower, lily of the valley), and every 1.20.1 block that shares a 1.5.2 id with another: wool colours, sandstone, slab, wall and quartz forms, mob heads, anvils, wet sponge, the stone slab, smooth sandstone |
| **Added** | MC 1.7 (eight flowers), 1.8 (wet sponge), 1.14 (cornflower, lily of the valley, stone slab, smooth sandstone); the others are 1.5.2 subtypes 1.13 split into blocks |
| **Maintainer ruling** | Round-3 default, consistent with the rulings (2026-10-21): every 1.20.1 flower keeps its own kind when broken, popped, placed through BTW's code and stored in BTW containers. 2026-10-15: post-1.5.2 plants take their nearest 1.5.2 relative's rules (the cornflower and the lily of the valley take the rose's). 2026-10-20: subtyped items without durability stack in BTW containers. 2026-10-22: millstone grinding of the newer flowers declined |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-DR3 and CF2-P8c: the flowers mapped to legacy 38 with no subtype, so a broken, popped or BTW-placed flower became a poppy, BTW storage turned flowers into poppies, the flower dyes and the banner pattern made nothing at BTW grids, every flower ground into red dye, and the red tulip's dye recipe was removed as if it were the rose's. FC's drops also lost the subtype of wool, slabs, sandstone, walls, quartz and mob heads (the port's 1.5.2 base classes lacked `damageDropped`), and wet sponge, the stone slab and smooth sandstone dropped their id's base block. The port's vanilla block items and skull item lacked 1.5.2's subtype flag, so in BTW storage a coloured wool, spruce slab or spruce plank never stacked, a white wool, oak slab or skeleton skull merged into the first coloured, typed or wither stack, and FC's log trades took any log. Infested blocks dropped themselves |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC places, drops and stores as before; the bridge gives the flowers port-only subtypes, gives each FC self-drop the identity of the block it came from, and restores 1.5.2's subtype flag. Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): flower_subtypes`, `item_subtypes` and `collapsed_identity`. [`ProxyRegistry`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyRegistry.java) (the flowers as subtypes of 38, ProxyRegistry.java:227; the cornflower and the lily onto 38, :682) and [`LegacyVariants`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/LegacyVariants.java) (:192) in 1.7.2's red_flower order, then 9 cornflower, 10 lily of the valley; Modern-Common `ItemBlock` (`getMetadata` places 38:0-10, ItemBlock.java:157; `getHasSubtypes` for the plain block items, :183), `ItemMultiTextureTile` (the flag), `Item.skull` (the flag, Item.java:925), `BlockSilverfish.quantityDropped` 0 (1.5.2). Modern-Common `World.ResolveModernSelfDrop` (World.java:919), asked by `Block.dropBlockAsItem_do` (Block.java:688-690); the WorldBridge override (WorldBridge.java:3362) -> [`CollapsedIdentity`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/CollapsedIdentity.java) (`resolve`, the per-thread harvest record opened in `ServerPlayerGameModeMixin.btw$destroyBlock` around `tryHarvestBlock`, `exempt`, and `audit`, `[SELF-AUDIT/identity]`, from BridgeSelfAudit.runServerStart). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Collapsed identity (CollapsedIdentity) |
| **Effect** | Every flower stays itself when broken, when it pops (soil dug, too dark, wrong ground), when BTW places it and in BTW storage; BTW grids craft the flower dyes and the flower banner pattern, and the red tulip makes red dye at a BTW grid as in 1.20.1; only the poppy grinds into red dye. Cornflowers and lilies of the valley follow BTW's flower rules, like the rose: light or open sky and BTW's soils, or they pop. Broken wool, slabs, sandstone, walls, quartz and mob heads drop their own kind; wet sponge, the stone slab and smooth sandstone drop themselves; a silk-touched chipped or damaged anvil stays chipped or damaged; infested blocks drop nothing. In BTW storage every subtype of these items stacks with itself and never merges into another; FC's log trades take only the species they ask for, as in 4.B |
| **BTW adds** | FC's flower rules (light, soil, grazing, buoyancy, filters) for the ten flowers, now with their identity |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — the blocks, items, loot and recipes stay 1.20.1's; FC keeps every drop decision (whether, how many, silk touch, tools) |
| **Data or code?** | Code: table rows, five Modern-Common edits, one new Forge class, a body edit in an existing mixin |
| **Decisions** | Flowers design: D1 (b) (the red tulip's red dye recipe stays, as the cornflower, lily and wither rose dyes do at BTW grids, #038), D2 (b) (no millstone recipes for the newer flowers; declined 2026-10-22), D3 (a) (every FC self-drop that lost its block's identity), D5 (b) and D6 (follow-ups, below) |
| **Alt rejected** | The 1.20.1 loot table for every collapsed member (as for twins and beds): it overrides FC's count and silk-touch choice and needs the live block, which a player's harvest has already removed. Skipping FC's harvest for non-canonical members: loses FC's harvest hunger, tool rules and animal panic, and fixes no pop. Per-class `damageDropped` ports: miss the 1.20.1-only members. Leaving the cornflower and the lily on 1.20.1's rules: contradicts the lore ruling (a daisy pops in the dark, a cornflower beside it would not) |
| **Known limitation** | The wither rose (it grows on netherrack and soul sand, which BTW's flower rule refuses) and the torchflower (the torchflower crop's last stage) keep 1.20.1's rules. Wet sponge, carved pumpkins, the stone slab, smooth sandstone and coloured beds still turn into their 1.5.2 equivalent inside BTW storage and when BTW places them (follow-up F-COLLAPSE-ITEMS). Drops with no BTW form use 1.20.1's drop offset. A player head drops without its owner. An FC enderman's carried flower draws as a poppy. Data-pack millstone tag rules (`btw_machine_recipes`) reach only items with no BTW id, so not the cornflower or the lily. Stone, cobblestone, ore and snow items, coal, spawn eggs, potions, filled maps and BTW's anvil item still lack 1.5.2's subtype flag (follow-up F-SUBTYPES). Silk Touch reaches only the blocks whose BTW class allows it (1.5.2's general rule is not ported) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-27/28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W3; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bk)): the flower plan P-FLOWERS 107/107 (41/107 on 4558220), rerun at W5 and W7 unchanged. `[SELF-AUDIT/identity] 51 blocks keep their identity ... 0 warnings (0 expected)`, with the ten flower lines (FC 38:1-10). The recipe census (#025): 27 -> 19 uncraftable, 708 -> 709 recipes (the red tulip's dye no longer removed); P-RM's red dye recipes list `red_dye_from_tulip`. Every other regression plan the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Flowers and other split blocks behave as their own kind everywhere BTW handles them |

### #046 — BTW mobs leave creative and spectator players alone

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's targeting rule: `TargetGoal.canContinueToUse` -> `LivingEntity.canAttack` -> `Player.canBeSeenAsEnemy` (no creative or spectator player, nothing invulnerable, dead or removed); `LivingEntity.getVisibilityPercent` and Forge's `LivingVisibilityEvent` |
| **Added** | The rule is 1.20.1's; spectators and the visibility event did not exist in 1.5.2 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-21: BTW mobs never take or keep a creative or spectator player as a target, by any path; survival is unchanged. 2026-10-22 (D1 (a)): only targeting changes; a wolf's hostility from a creative hit or wolf meat, animal panic and enderman screams stay as in 4.B |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-M7 and CF2-MX2. 4.B checked a player only when a mob chose a target (`EntityAITarget.isSuitableTarget`), so a mob kept chasing a player who switched to creative, and a creative player's hit (old-AI mobs), enderman and pigman group anger, a pig hit near pigmen, a fire starter near a wild wolf and wolf meat gave mobs that player as a target. Ranged AI tasks kept their own copy of the target, the FC ghast its private one for up to 20 ticks, wither heads theirs. The port's lookup ignored sneaking, invisibility and the death screen |
| **Pattern** | **B** (1.20.1's rule enforced on FC code, by ruling). Rebalance: none for survival players |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): creative_targets`. [`FcTargetRule`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcTargetRule.java): `isTargetable` (FcTargetRule.java:29) is `canBeSeenAsEnemy`; `sweep` (:53) runs first in `FCEnvHurtGuard.runOnUpdate` (FCEnvHurtGuard.java:84) for every FC mob each server tick and clears attackTarget, entityToAttack, wither heads 1-2, the FC ghast's target and the AI tasks still holding a protected player (`EntityAITasks.resetExecutingTasks`, Modern-Common); a pigman's anger and grunt delay and a head crab are cleared only for a protected living player (`isProtectedLivingPlayer`, :47), so a pigman whose survival target died stays angry, as 1.5.2's anger never decays; `audit` (:166, `[SELF-AUDIT/targets]`, the ghast's reflective slot). [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java)`.getClosestVulnerablePlayer(ToEntity)` (WorldBridge.java:2924-2951): 4.B's lookup with `isTargetable` and 1.20.1's `getVisibilityPercent` (sneak x0.8, invisibility x0.7 x max(armour cover, 0.1), plus Forge's visibility hook). The squid's head-crab latch uses the same predicate (`PlayerBridge.GetCanBeHeadCrabbed`, #047). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Mob targets and riders (FcTargetRule, FcRiding) |
| **Effect** | A mob drops a player the tick they switch to creative or spectator, and a creative or spectator player's hit, anger, a startled wolf or wolf meat never makes a mob target them; this covers the villager zombies of zombified villages (#048), which are BTW mobs. Survival play is unchanged. Sneaking and invisibility hide players from BTW mobs as in 4.B (sneaking cuts how far they notice a player by a fifth, invisibility without armour almost completely), mods' visibility modifiers apply to BTW mobs, and mobs no longer go after a player on the death screen |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's sneaking and invisibility rules on 1.20.1's visibility percent, which other mods' visibility modifiers reach |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — vanilla mobs keep 1.20.1's own goals |
| **Data or code?** | Code |
| **Alt rejected** | A `PlayerChangeGameModeEvent` handler: it would not catch hits, anger, startle or wolf meat, nor task-held copies. Making the creative player's FC view report `isEntityAlive() == false`: FC reads it for far more than targeting. Per-path patches on FC classes: a dozen targets for one rule |
| **Known limitation** | Dispositions a creative or spectator player causes stay as in 4.B (ruling D1 (a)): a wild wolf hit or fed wolf meat stays hostile (to survival players), animals still panic, endermen scream until they calm, silverfish still call allies. A dead player is left to FC's own `isEntityAlive` rules, as in 4.B |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W4; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bl)): the mob plan P-MOBS 65/65 (33/65 on 4558220), rerun at W8 65/65, and the client plan P-MOBS-C 11/11 (5/11 on 4558220). `[SELF-AUDIT/targets]` resolves FCEntityGhast's target slot. Every regression plan the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None for survival players; creative and spectator players are left alone, as in 1.20.1 |

### #047 — FC squids and players on 1.20.1's terms

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 damage types and the `minecraft:bypasses_armor` tag; vehicles (`Entity.stopRiding`); the motion packet for `hurtMarked` entities; `LivingEntity` use state; the level's player list (`level.players()`) |
| **Added** | Damage types and tags are 1.19.4's; the rest predates 1.5.2 in other forms |
| **Maintainer ruling** | None needed (4.B parity); decision D6 keeps 1.20.1's shield out of the disarm |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-MX1 and CF2-MX3. FC's World kept `playerEntities` empty, so squids never hunted or head-crabbed players, and mill-stone nausea and beacon effects never reached a player. Once squids could head-crab players, four 4.B squid rules met port gaps: the bite's `setDamageBypassesArmor` was lost in the damage mapping (armour cut it to 0.42 under full iron); a tentacle fling and a BTW cow's kick wrote only the FC view's motion, which the next sync overwrote; the knock-off from boats read the FC view's riding field, which the port never sets; and the disarm read FC's own use state, which the port never clears, so after one right-click with a BTW sword or refined tool a tentacle would throw whatever the player held. FC riders never followed their mount: a head-crab squid hung where it latched, an animal stayed head-crabbed after its squid died, and an FC spider jockey's skeleton walked away |
| **Pattern** | **D** (4.B parity restored in port code, as #015 and #040). Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): head_crab`. [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java): `playerEntities` is a live view of the level's players, spectators left out (`PlayerEntitiesView`, WorldBridge.java:2874). Data: damage type `betterthanwolves:mob_attack_unblockable` (message `mob`, scaling as `mob_attack`, exhaustion 0) and its entry in `#minecraft:bypasses_armor`; [`DamageSourceMapping`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/DamageSourceMapping.java)'s `"mob"` case (DamageSourceMapping.java:72) maps an FC hit flagged unblockable to it (`[SELF-AUDIT/damage]`). [`PlayerBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/PlayerBridge.java): `OnFlungBySquidTentacle` (PlayerBridge.java:306) and `OnKickedByCow` (:322) unseat the player and put FC's motion on it with `hurtMarked` (never on a spectator); `isBlocking` (:1095) is the real player's use of a BTW block-action item in the main hand; `GetCanBeHeadCrabbed` (:955) asks the real player and #046's predicate; a squid on the head weighs as armour does. [`FcRiding`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcRiding.java), from `FCEnvHurtGuard.runOnUpdate`: a rider stays on only while its mount is live and still carries it, and runs `updateRidden`, as 1.5.2's world update did; a head-crabbed player leaves a 1.20.1 vehicle. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Mob targets and riders (FcTargetRule, FcRiding) |
| **Effect** | As in 4.B: squids hunt players at night and in the dark and latch onto their heads; a squid's bite takes a full point through any armour but the soulforged plate helm, costs no hunger and is not stopped by a shield; tentacles and cow kicks fling players, out of a boat or minecart too; a head-crabbed player cannot stay in a boat, minecart or saddle; a squid knocks a BTW sword or refined tool out of a hand that blocks with it. Mill-stone nausea and beacon effects reach nearby players. A squid that latches onto an animal or a villager rides it and lets go when it dies; skeletons ride BTW spiders as jockeys again |
| **BTW adds** | One damage type, usable by other mods through the vanilla tag |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — vanilla mobs' hits and vanilla knockback are unchanged |
| **Data or code?** | Both: the damage type and tag are data, the bridge hooks code |
| **Alt rejected** | An anonymous `DamageSource` overriding `is(TagKey)` (as `withoutDifficultyScaling` overrides scaling): invisible to mods that read the type holder's tags, and Protocol #3 prefers data. Mirroring the head crab as a 1.20.1 passenger (it would fix the client view and the boat rule for free): passengers of a player are saved in the player's data and never reloaded |
| **Known limitation** | A head-crab squid is drawn as a free squid at its server position, a little behind a fast-moving player (no FC riding link on the client). A 1.20.1 shield is never knocked out (D6). Riders tick in proxy order, so a rider can trail its mount by a tick. 4.B's secondary fling of the vehicle itself is not ported (the vehicle is a 1.20.1 entity) |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W4; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bl)): P-MOBS rows C10-C17 and P-MOBS-C row CL9, within P-MOBS 65/65 and P-MOBS-C 11/11 (#046). `[SELF-AUDIT/damage] betterthanwolves:mob_attack_unblockable loaded, in minecraft:bypasses_armor` |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | The squid's player rules run as in 4.B |

### #048 — Villages are zombified, as in 4.B

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 jigsaw villages (all five biome styles and their zombie variants), and the villagers, zombie villagers and iron golem their templates place with spawn type STRUCTURE; `/place structure`; 1.20.1's zombie siege (`VillageSiege`, a custom spawner that sends zombies to a POI village at midnight); the world's default game type |
| **Added** | Jigsaw villages: MC 1.14; the siege predates 1.5.2 (POI-based since 1.14) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-21: 4.B zombified villages; the soul-urn cure proven end to end first; abandonment by distance from the world spawn (under 2,250 blocks nearly empty, a 1-in-20 farmer per building; 2,250-3,000 farmers, smiths and butchers; beyond, every profession; x4 on Large Biomes); creative worlds keep villagers. 2026-10-22 (D1 (a1)): no iron golem in any new village. 2026-10-23: no zombie sieges, as in 4.B (FC removed the siege, vanilla/server World.java:105-107 and WorldServer.java:183-185) |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-MU3. 4.B spawns every village villager as a persistent BTW villager zombie with its building's class, and empties villages near the spawn (vanilla/server ComponentVillage.java:121-204, ComponentVillageStartPiece.java:89-120; Server FCUtilsHardcoreSpawn.java:30-86). The port made every village villager a plain BTW villager with a random profession, so traders came free. The cure was also broken: `WorldBridge.removeEntity` neither set FC isDead nor found puppets, so a cured villager zombie made by VanillaMobReplacer or loaded from disk turned into a villager every tick and never went away. 1.20.1's siege could still come at midnight to a village whose job sites, beds or bells a Minecraft villager had taken (one from play before the port, or another mod's villagers), never to a BTW village |
| **Pattern** | **C** (rebalance, keep the feature): 1.20.1's villages stay, and their inhabitants follow 4.B's rule, with 1.20.1 buildings standing in for 4.B's components |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): zombified_villages`, `village_sieges` and `creative_world` (shared with #054). [`VillageInhabitants`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/VillageInhabitants.java) finds the village by `StructureManager.getStructureWithPieceAt(pos, #minecraft:village)` or, for a live placement, by the StructureStart that [`StructureStartPlacementMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/StructureStartPlacementMixin.java) (HEAD and RETURN of `StructureStart.placeInChunk`, `m_226850_`) publishes. The level comes from the start piece's centre, with FC's own radii (FCUtilsHardcoreSpawn, by name). Building = the largest piece at the villager; class = the building's nearest job-site block (farmer if none). 4.B's 1-in-20 per building and class filter apply. The result is a persistent villager zombie with its class and no FC init, unless the world is creative (`WorldInfoBridge.isCreativeWorld`, WorldInfoBridge.java:107: its default game type is creative). It is asked by `VanillaMobReplacer.replaceWithProxy` (VanillaMobReplacer.java:380-384) before the proxy type is picked; a new village's iron golem is queued and discarded at the end of the tick (:178-182). `WorldBridge.removeEntity` (WorldBridge.java:2069) is 1.5.2's (unmount, setDead, discard through `proxyFor`). `WorldInfoBridge.getTerrainType` (:86) answers LARGE_BIOMES for the Large Biomes preset, the one source of FC's terrain type, shared with #054. [`VillageSiegeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/VillageSiegeMixin.java): `VillageSiege.tick` (`m_7995_`) returns 0 at its head. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Villages (VillageInhabitants) |
| **Effect** | In a new village every villager is a persistent BTW villager zombie of its building's class (a baby villager a baby villager zombie) that never burns: a thrown soul urn cures one in 3-5 minutes into a trader of that class (a farmer comes out dirty). Within 2,250 blocks of the world spawn a village is nearly empty (a lone farmer now and then), and between 2,250 and 3,000 only farmers, blacksmiths and butchers live there; on Large Biomes both distances are four times as far. New villages have no iron golem, and no village is ever besieged. A creative world (one whose default game mode is Creative, #054's rule) keeps villagers, filtered the same way. Villages generated earlier are unchanged. A cured villager zombie becomes one villager and the zombie is gone, and a villager killed by a zombie disappears when its zombie appears, as in 4.B. The villager zombies are BTW mobs, so #046's rule keeps them off creative and spectator players |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's progression gate on villager trades (the soul urn), its abandonment by distance, and BTW's villager classes, chosen by 1.20.1's own job-site blocks |
| **Dual-sourced?** | No: village inhabitants are BTW's; 1.20.1 villages, buildings and job blocks stay, and raids, patrols, cats and the wandering trader keep their 1.20.1 spawners |
| **Disables (justification)** | The 1.20.1 village iron golem, in every new village (D1 (a1)): 4.B removed village golems in every game mode (vanilla/server Village.java:80-97), and a vanilla golem ignores BTW zombies, leaving a free iron source beside them. Zombie sieges, in every village (ruling 2026-10-23): 4.B has none, because FC removed the siege object and its tick; `VillageSiegeMixin` stops the midnight roll, Forge's VillageSiegeEvent and the zombies, and BTW's village bookkeeping keeps running, as in 4.B |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one class, two mixins (`StructureStartPlacementMixin`: 2 injects; `VillageSiegeMixin`: 1 inject), three bridge edits |
| **Decisions** | Villages design: D3 (a) (a baby villager becomes a baby villager zombie), D4 (a) (the class from the building's nearest job-site block), D5 (a) (live placements included), D6 (a) (igloo basements unchanged, a separate ruling), D7 (a) (villager zombies vanish on Peaceful like every BTW monster, as in 4.B), D8 (a) (a creative world's zombie-variant villages keep their villager zombies) |
| **Alt rejected** | Zombifying by spawn type and position alone (no village, so no per-village level and no buildings; igloo basements would be caught). Professions from template names (fails for modded houses). Keeping FC's random profession (loses 4.B's building classes, which the level-1 filter depends on) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | For the siege: Forge's `VillageSiegeEvent` (posted only after the village test and a successful spawn search, and a cancel makes the setup run again on every tick of a siege night); removing the siege from the spawner list (a call inside `createLevels`, which Forge patches, and other instances would be missed) |
| **Known limitation** | A village straddling land explored before the update can be partly zombified. A village whose start chunk unloads mid-generation can give a level-2 building a second farmer. On Peaceful, villager zombies vanish like every BTW monster, as in 4.B. Igloo basement zombie villagers stay plain zombies (separate ruling). In creative worlds, 1.20.1's rare zombie villages still hold villager zombies (D8). 1.20.1's other POI-based village systems (raids, village cats, golem strolling, fox avoidance) never see a BTW village, whose villagers take no POI ticket |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W4; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bm)): the cure plan P-VCURE 19/19 (16/19 on 4558220, where one cure made 60 villagers), the placement plan P-VPLACE 23/23 (5/23), natural villages P-VNAT 11/11 (4/11), Large Biomes P-VLB 5/5 (2/5) and the siege plan P-VSIEGE 16/16 (14/16: at 4558220 a villager-free cluster of four bells was besieged; the BTW village was not, on either tree); the log line `[VILLAGES] new villages ...` with terrain multiplier 1.0 (4.0 in P-VLB), and no `Failed to create zombie for village siege`. Refmap `StructureStartPlacementMixin` `m_226850_`, `VillageSiegeMixin` `m_7995_`. P-VCURE, P-VPLACE and P-VLB unchanged at W9. Every regression plan the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Villager trades come back behind the soul urn, as in 4.B |

### #049 — Explosions follow BTW's block rules

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 explosions (`Explosion.explode` / `finalizeExplosion`, `ExplosionDamageCalculator`): TNT, creepers, ghasts, the wither, beds and respawn anchors, end crystals, other mods' blasts, and BTW's own dynamite, blasting oil and vases through `level.explode`; the drop-decay gamerules; 1.20.1's merging of explosion drops |
| **Added** | Explosions predate 1.5.2; the decay gamerules are 1.19.3's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-22 (D1 = C): the 1.20.1 decay gamerules decide for BTW's blocks too, and new worlds set `tntExplosionDropDecay` true (4.B's 1 in size for TNT); existing worlds are unchanged. Beds and doors keep 1.20.1's drops (2026-10-20, #039); flowers keep their kind (#045); 4.B strata (2026-10-21, #050) |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-DR4 and CF2-DR5. Every blast dropped 1.20.1 loot and ran 1.20.1's reactions: stone gave cobblestone, dirt and grass vanilla dirt, BTW blocks lost their subtype and ignored `canDropFromExplosion`, and none of FC's 15 blast reactions ran. The rays read FC's position-less resistance, so strata stone and ores resisted like surface stone. FC's mining charge dropped nothing, and blocks with only a hardness (dirt, grass, sand, gravel, water, lava) resisted with 0, so a mining charge deleted water and lava. TNT dropped every block it broke, where 4.B dropped one in its size |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's 1.5.2 per-block blast body runs inside 1.20.1's explosion for every block FC has a blast rule for; FC's positional resistance answers 1.20.1's rays where FC's class has one; 1.20.1 keeps the rest, and its 16-per-stack drop merge now covers FC's drops. Rebalance (C, D1): TNT decays in new worlds |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_blast_rules`. [`BlastRules`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BlastRules.java): rules 0-8, `beforeFinalize` (BlastRules.java:115), `afterFinalize` (:153), `dropRadius` (:172), `addMerged` (:220), `positionalResistance` (:265), `decide` (:314), `audit` (:363, `[SELF-AUDIT/blast]`). [`ExplosionMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ExplosionMixin.java): HEAD and TAIL of `Explosion.finalizeExplosion` (`m_46075_`), on the server thread only: FC's per-block rules first, the cells FC owns air for 1.20.1's loop, FC's queued secondary explosions after the blast. [`ExplosionDamageCalculatorMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ExplosionDamageCalculatorMixin.java) (HEAD of `getBlockExplosionResistance`, `m_6617_`): a block whose FC class reads its position for blast resistance answers it, which is how #050's strata reach 1.20.1's rays. [`HookPolicy`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/HookPolicy.java) decides RESIST (HookPolicy.java:238-242: FC for a positional FC class, `fcDeclaresPositionalResistance`, else NOTIFY; #036). [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java) holds FC's drops during a blast pass for 1.20.1's merge (WorldBridge.java:2055). Modern-Common 1.5.2 base logic: `Block.setHardness`'s resistance floor (Block.java:273), `DropItemsOnDestroyedByExplosion` (:1579), `OnDirtDugWithImproperTool` (:1604) with its slab and neighbour forms, `BlockTNT.canDropFromExplosion`, `BlockStairs.onBlockDestroyedByExplosion`, `Explosion.doExplosionB`'s order. [`ProxyBlock`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyBlock.java) (ProxyBlock.java:946: 1.5.2's `dropBlockAsItemWithChance` for the 1.20.1 paths that roll a BTW block's loot), [`ModernBlockStandIn`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernBlockStandIn.java)`.DropItemsOnDestroyedByExplosion` (ModernBlockStandIn.java:609: under a mining charge a class-S log breaks into 6 sawdust and, while barked, its bark), [`WorldGamerulesHandler`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldGamerulesHandler.java) (WorldGamerulesHandler.java:63-64, D1 = C). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Explosions (BlastRules) |
| **Effect** | A blast drops what 4.B drops: stone gives loose cobblestone at the blast's chance plus a stone and a gravel pile; cobblestone loose cobblestone; dirt, grass and mycelium loose dirt, and the dirt around the crater loosens; BTW blocks keep their colour or type; chewed logs, spike logs, mouldings, sidings and corners break into sawdust; rough stone into its pieces; snow layers drop nothing; a blasted mining charge goes off; smouldering logs flare; ores give no XP. Deep stone and ores resist blasts as in 4.B (the strata, #050). Mining charges break stone into 5 stones and 3 gravel piles; dirt, gravel and sand into 6 piles; logs into 6 sawdust and their bark (other mods' logs too, while barked: their tagged bark, else oak bark); crimson and warped stems into 6 sawdust; they no longer delete water or lava. Digging dirt, grass or mycelium by hand loosens the dirt next to it. Dirt, grass, sand and gravel resist blasts as in 4.B. A blast's drops lie in stacks of up to 16. In a new world TNT drops one in four of the blocks it breaks, like every other explosion (`/gamerule tntExplosionDropDecay false` restores 1.20.1's rule). Beds, doors, blocks with stored contents, collapsed variants and TNT keep 1.20.1's blast |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's piles economy and loose-dirt physics on every explosion of every source, the strata's blast resistance, the mining charge's full yield, and BTW's subtype-faithful drops on every 1.20.1 path that rolls a BTW block's loot |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's explosion, damage, knockback, fire, sounds and packet are unchanged; blocks FC has no blast rule for keep 1.20.1's drops; the decay gamerules stay the players' knob |
| **Disables (justification)** | XP from blasted FC-owned ores when a player set the blast off: 4.B gives no XP for breaking blocks |
| **Data or code?** | Code: two mixins, one class, Modern-Common ports, bridge edits, one gamerule default |
| **Decisions** | Blast design: D1 = C (ruled), D2 (the `setHardness` floor, included), D3 (TNT pending: 1.20.1's TNT until BTW's primed TNT is ported), O1 (the positional resistance hook is here; the strata it reads are #050's), D5 (Modern-Common `doExplosionB`'s order), D6 (blocks with a 1.20.1 block entity keep 1.20.1's blast), D7 (FC's drops merged, 16 per stack) |
| **Alt rejected** | `ExplosionEvent.Detonate` (listener order against protection mods; before entity damage). Redirects of `canDropFromExplosion` / `getDrops` / `onBlockExploded` (exclusive). A loot modifier on `EXPLOSION_RADIUS` (misses non-decaying blasts, cannot run FC's reactions) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Routing every FC-identity block (Modern-Common placeholders and FC classes with vanilla drops would lose 1.20.1 behaviour 4.B shares, and TNT would stop chaining). Forge's positional `IForgeBlock.getExplosionResistance` added to `Block` (re-dispatches every block, collides with other mods). 4.B's 1 in size for every explosion whatever the gamerules (D1 A: 1.20.1-only blocks in the same blast would follow other odds) |
| **Known limitation** | TNT (46) chains through 1.20.1's TNT until BTW's primed TNT is ported. Blocks with a 1.20.1 block entity, beds, doors and collapsed variants keep 1.20.1's blast. Blasted infested stone still spawns silverfish, and blasted nether quartz ore still gives XP to a player's blast (their Modern-Common classes are drop-parity follow-ups). An attachment 1.20.1 owns (torch, rail, redstone, carpet, flower, crop) on a support BTW owns always pops at 100% instead of rolling the blast's decay. Other mods' loot modifiers do not see blasts of BTW-ruled blocks. A mining charge that reaches a powder keg stops (FC's keg primes a stub). Existing worlds keep `tntExplosionDropDecay` false |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W5; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bn)): the blast plan P-BLAST 79/79 in a fresh world (34/79 on 4558220), rerun at W6, W7 and W8 79/79; P-FLOWERS 107/107. `[SELF-AUDIT/blast] 418 FC-identity blocks ... pending [46]; positional resistance on 8 blocks of ids [1, 14, 15, 16, 21, 56, 73, 129] and 0 BTW blocks; 0 warnings (0 expected)` (16 positional blocks once #050's deepslate forms read as those ids); the hook audit's FC-owned pairs 5 -> 13 (RESIST on those blocks); a new world reads `tntExplosionDropDecay = true`; refmap `ExplosionMixin` `m_46075_`, `ExplosionDamageCalculatorMixin` `m_6617_`. The must-watch regression plans the same (P-BEDDOOR, P-HOOK, P-LEAF-V, P-LEAF-DT, mlog_p1, wood_p1, P-RM, P-USE, P-PLACE, the crash set) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Explosions and mining charges feed BTW's piles, loose dirt and sawdust economy at 4.B's rates; the strata protect deep mines from 1.20.1 blasts as in 4.B |

### #050 — Stone strata, deepslate and raw ore follow 4.B

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.18's world: stone and ores from y=320 down to y=-64, the deepslate layer below y=0 (deepslate, cobbled deepslate, eight deepslate ores), raw iron and raw gold drops, raw-ore blocks in ore veins, and their smelting and blasting to ingots; nether gold ore's smelting and blasting |
| **Added** | MC 1.16 (nether gold ore), 1.17 (deepslate, raw ores), 1.18 (world height, ore veins) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-21: 4.B strata at 4.B heights (stone and stone ores: y <= 48 mid, y <= 24 deep, with 4.B's rand(2) edge); deepslate, cobbled deepslate and the deepslate ores become BTW deep-strata stone and BTW ores; raw iron and raw gold are BTW's iron and gold ore chunks, their ingot smelting and blasting removed, and raw-ore blocks unpack into 9 chunks. 2026-10-22: mined deepslate gives loose cobblestone, and the cobbled deepslate -> deepslate smelting goes; depth decides for every stone below y=48, placed or generated; nether gold ore loses its furnace and blast-furnace recipes, its drops stay 1.20.1's; darker deep-strata textures are a follow-up; layered granite, diorite, andesite, tuff and calcite declined |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-PR-DEEP and CF2-PR-ORE: 4.B wrote strata only at world generation, which the port does not run, so every stone and ore read strata 0 and dug like surface stone, and 1.20.1 explosions used the surface resistance at every depth. Deepslate had no FC identity: it dug, dropped and resisted blasts as in 1.20.1. Raw iron and gold had no FC form, so a furnace or blast furnace turned them into ingots, skipping BTW's chunk-to-nugget step. Modern-Common BlockOre lacked 1.5.2's drop rules: Fortune never applied to BTW's ores, and nether quartz ore dropped itself |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend, 4.B parity): FC's own strata code runs, fed from the position; the deepslate forms and raw ore take FC identities. Rebalance: none beyond 4.B |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): strata` and `raw_ore_chunks`. [`StrataRule`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/StrataRule.java) (`strataAt` StrataRule.java:52, `isStrataBlock` :90): strata blocks are id 1 and the FC blocks whose strata conversion is the level; the strata is deep for a deepslate form, top outside the Overworld, else 4.B's height rule with a position-hashed rand(2) edge (per column for stone, per block for ores), on both sides, so the client's dig prediction reads what the server reads. [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java): the derive branch (WorldBridge.java:379); the position owns the strata bits in `getBlockMetadata`, `putMetaOverride` and `setBlockMetadata` (:252, :275, :1385), and a strata block's overlay entry survives a strata change (also `FcMetaOverlay.pruneLoaded`); `applyFcMetadataToVanillaBlock` leaves a strata block's state alone (:725). [`ProxyRegistry`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyRegistry.java): the nine deepslate forms map to 1, 4, 14, 15, 16, 21, 56, 73 and 129, after the stone forms (ProxyRegistry.java:983); `getBlockFormItemId` (:513) keeps their items 1.20.1's (ProxyItem, ModernCookingImport). [`LegacyVariants`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/LegacyVariants.java): raw iron and raw gold count as `fcItemChunkIronOre` / `fcItemChunkGoldOre`, resolved by field name (LegacyVariants.java:281), never as aliases (RecipeBridge.java:326). [`FcRecipeRemovals`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcRecipeRemovals.java) pass 3 (FcRecipeRemovals.java:420): a `minecraft:` cooking recipe also goes when FC's furnace cooks its input into something else. RecipeBridge: FC's chunk smelting also takes raw iron and gold (RecipeBridge.java:417). Data `btw_recipe_replacements/raw_ores/` (a raw-ore block unpacks into 9 chunks) and `btw_recipe_replacements/nether_gold_ore/` (its two cooking recipes removed, #025's format). Modern-Common [`BlockOre`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/BlockOre.java): 1.5.2's drop rules. 1.20.1 explosions read a strata block's resistance at its position through #049's hook. Network protocol "6" (the dig and walking-speed prediction). `[SELF-AUDIT/strata]`. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Strata and deepslate (StrataRule) |
| **Effect** | Overworld stone at or below y=48 (y=49 by the edge) is mid strata (hardness 3; an iron pick mines it, a stone pick only cracks it), at or below y=24 (25) deep strata (hardness 4.5; a diamond pick harvests it, an iron pick cracks it), placed stone included; ores in those layers are harder, and a pick good enough for the ore but not for the rock knocks the ore loose and leaves rough stone. Explosions take less rock at depth (#049). Deepslate is deep strata stone at any height and drops BTW's loose cobblestone, stones and gravel; cobbled deepslate is BTW cobblestone, and its smelting into deepslate is gone; deepslate ores are BTW ores (iron and gold give chunks), deep strata, with no XP; all of them give BTW's hard-surface walking bonus. Raw iron and raw gold are BTW chunks in every BTW container and machine, cook into nuggets and place as chunk blocks; raw-ore blocks unpack into nine chunks; no Minecraft recipe turns raw ore, a deepslate ore or nether gold ore into an ingot. Fortune multiplies BTW ore drops as in 4.B; nether quartz ore drops quartz |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's depth progression on 1.20.1's taller, deeper world; BTW's chunk economy for 1.20.1's raw ores |
| **Dual-sourced?** | No: the strata and the deepslate forms are BTW's; decorative deepslate, copper and the raw-ore blocks stay 1.20.1's, and the deepslate forms' items keep their 1.20.1 identity |
| **Data or code?** | Code (bridge and Modern-Common) plus four recipe replacement files |
| **Alt rejected** | A strata property on 1.20.1's stone and ore states written by a generation feature: it changes the registry state ids, needs a chunk upgrade for existing worlds and leaves player-placed stone at 0. A per-chunk SavedData noise for the edge: server-only, so the client could not predict digs. Explicit "remove" entries for the four raw-ore cooking recipes: case by case, and blind to the next "counts as" item. Position-aware resistance through BlockMixin (overriding the Forge 4-argument default on Block): a Forge-added name with no SRG, and any block class overriding it would bypass the rule |
| **Known limitation** | Stone shows no strata texture (darker deep-strata textures are a follow-up). Player-placed stone and stone FC makes (cement, lava on loose cobble) take their position's strata (4.B: strata 0), by ruling. Granite, diorite, andesite, tuff and calcite stay 1.20.1 stone, soft pockets inside the strata layers (layered strata for them were declined). Other mods' recipes still turn raw ore and deepslate ores into ingots (only `minecraft:` recipes are checked). FC mobs' footsteps on deepslate sound like stone. Silk Touch gives BTW's normal drops. Infested stone has no strata. Copper stays 1.20.1's. Deepslate items keep their 1.20.1 identity, and mining deepslate never gives cobbled deepslate |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W6; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bo)): the strata plan P-STRATA 152 of 153 (55 of 153 on 4558220; its one FAIL, E16, is the nether gold ore row written for the option not ruled, by design), the same at W7; the client plan P-STRATA-C, the client's dig progress equal to the server's in all six cells; P-BLAST 79/79. `[SELF-AUDIT/strata] mapped strata ids [1, 14, 15, 16, 21, 56, 73, 129]; 9 deepslate forms (8 deep, cobbled deepslate -> 4); raw_iron -> 22571, raw_gold -> 22574; blast resistance by position on; 0 warnings (0 expected)`; `Recipe replacements: 2 replaced, 2 removed`; `112 crafting + 2 special + 39 cooking recipes removed`; no deepslate stand-in line. P-RM lists no deepslate or raw-ore cooking recipe, and the recipe census (#025) counts raw iron and raw gold smelting as FC's own chunk recipes (fc 18 -> 20). Every regression plan the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Depth matters again: better picks open deeper rock, and raw ore enters BTW's chunk -> nugget chain |

### #051 — The newer woods have BTW's sidings, mouldings, corners and decorative blocks

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Acacia, dark oak, mangrove and cherry planks, stairs and slabs (FC wood types 5-8 since #034) |
| **Added** | MC 1.7 (acacia, dark oak), 1.19 (mangrove), 1.20 (cherry) |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-20 (FU-W1): the four woods get BTW's decorative wood blocks through runtime patches. 2026-10-22: no 6-plank BTW fence recipe for them; their 1.20.1 trapdoors stay craftable |
| **Conflict** | Their planks had FC identity at item level only, so FC's planks rule and saw never reached them, and every FC site that turns a wood type into a block, name or texture answered blood wood or oak for 5-8: the five stub items' placement and names, the two sub-block classes' wood type, three item renderers, and FC's sub-block recipe loop (0..4). Their 1.20.1 stairs and slabs have no legacy id, so 4.B's stair, slab and saw rules missed them. FC's planks -> fence row would take the 1.20.1 trapdoors' only grid. In the 2x2 grid FC's recipes matched by item only, so two spruce sidings could make another wood's planks |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's own sub-block classes at four new pairs of ids, FC's recipe loop re-run for 5-8, load-time patches keyed on those ids; 4.B's stair and slab rules as 1.20.1 recipes and an FC machine hook. Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): modern_wood_subblocks`. [`ModernWoodSubBlocks`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernWoodSubBlocks.java), built at BTWLifecycle step 5f, after log2 (#034): FC's `FCBlockWoodSidingAndCornerAndDecorative` / `FCBlockWoodMouldingAndDecorative` with FC's own items at 1093/1094 acacia, 1095/1096 dark oak, 1097/1098 mangrove and 1099/1100 cherry; at common setup, after the modern-dye twins, FC's 32 sub-block recipes for wood types 5-8, appended all or nothing; the 1.20.1 stair and slab recipe step in RecipeBridge; its `[SELF-AUDIT/wood]` line. `ProxyRegistry.getBlockId`: the four planks answer 5 while the sub-blocks exist (ProxyRegistry.java:300). Six `@Pseudo` load-time patches in `btw.forge.mixin.fcext`: `FCItemBlockWoodStubWoodMixin` (the five stubs' placement and names), `FCBlockWoodSidingWoodMixin`, `FCBlockWoodMouldingWoodMixin`, and the stub item renderers `FCBlockSidingStubRenderMixin`, `FCBlockSidingDecorativeStubRenderMixin` and `FCBlockWoodMouldingStubRenderMixin`. [`ModernWoodSawHooks`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernWoodSawHooks.java): FC's saw on the four woods' stairs and slabs (and #052's bamboo), registered after the log rule. [`modern-woods.gradle`](Forge/gradle/modern-woods.gradle) derives the four column sides; 52 names in `lang/BTW_modern_en_US.lang`. The exact 2x2 match is #025's (FCCraftingRecipes.java:320-321). Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Woods and logs (ModernWoods, FCLogRules) |
| **Effect** | The four planks follow FC's planks rule (an iron axe or better keeps them, anything else leaves sawdust) and saw into sidings; sidings saw into mouldings, mouldings into corners, corners into gears, each keeping its wood; benches, fences (from three mouldings), columns, pedestals and tables of each wood; placement, drops, names and item textures keep the wood. 6 planks make 6 stairs, 3 mouldings 1 stairs, 2 slabs 1 planks; the saw cuts their stairs (a siding and a moulding) and slabs (2 mouldings; a double slab 2 slabs). Six planks of these woods make their 1.20.1 trapdoors, not BTW fences (ruling 2026-10-22). Their planks are FC planks to BTW's fire too (#014) |
| **Lore mapping** | A wood is a wood: the same FC blocks and recipes as oak to blood wood, drawn from the wood's own planks |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's decorative wood set and its saw chain for the four newer woods |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — the 1.20.1 stairs, slabs, fences, doors, trapdoors and signs of these woods stay; their stair recipe makes 6, as 4.B's do |
| **Data or code?** | Code (one class, a hook, six load-time patches, a recipe step), 4 derived textures, 52 lang keys |
| **Alt rejected** | FC stairs blocks at new ids for the four woods (duplicate stairs blocks). Species slabs as subtypes of FC's wood slab 126 (3 species bits; cherry is 8). Planks identity without the sub-blocks (the 5-8 stubs would place blood wood blocks). `@ModifyVariable` on FC's inline wood-type chain. Adding the 32 recipes at FC init (it renumbered the modern-dye twins' shipped ids) |
| **Known limitation** | Ids 1093-1100 must never change; if one is taken at start-up none of this turns on (one ERROR). Spruce, birch and jungle trapdoors stay uncraftable at BTW's crafting grids, because FC's fence recipe answers that grid. The column sides are derived textures that do not follow resource packs. As in 4.B, no wood sub-block is in a creative tab. Pick-block on a newer wood's siding or moulding gives the stub in that block's own wood, and a moulding block's own item (by command only) draws as oak, as FC's blood wood moulding does |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W7; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bp)): the sub-block plan P-W1 85/85 (24/85 on 4558220) and the client plan P-W1-C, its 10 probes equal to the plan's after column. Logs: `[WOOD] sub-blocks of wood types 5-8 at 1093-1100 ...`, `[WOOD] FC recipes of wood types 5-8: fc_recipe_678..fc_recipe_709 (32), after the modern-dye twins`, `[SELF-AUDIT/wood] ... 0 warnings (0 expected)`, the saw hook's registration line and `newer woods 4 replaced, 8 added`; ProxyBlocks 174 -> 182. Build: `deriveModernWoodTextures: 8 textures checked (4 written)`, then `(0 written)`. The recipe census: `sweep fc total=707 skipped=3 fail=0` (675 before: FC's 32 new recipes craft). P-PLACE's placement sweep gains blocks 1093-1100 with its refusals unchanged; P-FLOWERS, P-BLAST, P-STRATA and the other regression plans the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | The newer woods enter BTW's decorative wood economy at BTW's costs |

### #052 — Bamboo planks come from the saw and the axe

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | Bamboo blocks (and stripped bamboo blocks) and bamboo planks |
| **Added** | MC 1.20 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-21: BTW's plank rule; no bamboo block -> planks recipe; planks through the saw and axe chopping like a log, with no bark and no work stump. 2026-10-22: the saw gives 2 planks and 1 sawdust; an iron-or-better BTW axe 1 plank and 1 sawdust; a weaker axe 1 stick and 2 sawdust; bamboo blocks break by hand and drop themselves. Crimson, warped and bamboo planks as BTW woods declined |
| **Conflict** | The 1.20.1 recipe made 2 planks from a bamboo block in the 2x2 grid with no tool, a plank source outside BTW's saw-or-axe gate; the saw did nothing to a bamboo block; FC's chopping refuses a log FC has no item for |
| **Pattern** | **C** (rebalance through BTW's own routes) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): bamboo_planks`. `btw_recipe_replacements/bamboo_planks.json` removes `minecraft:bamboo_planks`, with its reason (#025's format). [`ModernLogChoppingRecipe`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/recipes/ModernLogChoppingRecipe.java): the recipe type `betterthanwolves:modern_log_chopping`, with the data file `recipes/bamboo_chopping.json`: an FC axe and a log of `#minecraft:bamboo_blocks` give planks, or sticks with a weak axe, by FC's tool rules, and the sawdust is ejected at the player. FC's saw reads the same recipe through `ModernWoodSawHooks` (#051); RecipeBridge rebuilds the saw's index at each injection, so it follows `/reload`. JEI shows the chopping recipes. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Recipe bridge (RecipeBridge, WorkbenchFallback) and Woods and logs (ModernWoods, FCLogRules) |
| **Effect** | Saw: 2 bamboo planks and 1 sawdust. An FC axe of iron or better at the work stump or in the 2x2 grid: 1 plank and 1 sawdust; a wooden, stone or gold axe: 1 stick and 2 sawdust. No bark, no work stump, no chewing: a bamboo block still breaks by hand and drops itself, as in 1.20.1 |
| **Lore mapping** | A bamboo block is a bundle of cane, half a log of wood (its 1.20.1 yield is half a log's), cut by BTW's tools at a log's saw-to-axe ratio |
| **BTW adds** | Bamboo joins BTW's saw-or-axe plank economy |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — bamboo, its blocks, planks and other recipes stay; only the tool-free block -> planks recipe goes |
| **Data or code?** | Data (the numbers, the removal) on a small code recipe type |
| **Alt rejected** | A log's numbers (saw 4 + 2, chop 2 + 2: doubles bamboo's plank yield over 1.20.1's). BTW's log rule for the harvest (a bare hand would chew a bamboo block into BTW's chewed log, as stems; not ruled) |
| **Known limitation** | Bamboo planks are not FC planks (no iron-axe rule, no sidings), like crimson and warped planks (declined as BTW woods). Sticks from bamboo (2 -> 1) and the bamboo mosaic stay. Bamboo wood still reads as a flammable stand-in to BTW's fire (#014) |
| **Verified by** | P-W1 rows K1-K10, M1 and M2 (round 3, 2026-10-28, W7; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bp)): the bamboo block -> planks recipe is gone and a lone bamboo block crafts nothing; the saw gives 2 planks, 1 sawdust and no bark, also from a stripped bamboo block; an iron axe 1 plank, a stone axe 1 stick, the axe stays in the grid and 1 sawdust lands at the player; bamboo -> stick, the bamboo mosaic and a hand break that drops the block stay. The recipe census: 709 -> 708 recipes (#025) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Bamboo planks cost a saw or an axe, as BTW's wood does |

### #053 — The BTW beacon: the pyramid's block decides its power

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | The 1.20.1 beacon: `BeaconBlockEntity` (a pyramid of `#beacon_base_blocks`, five powers chosen in `BeaconMenu`, effects to players, the beam through stained glass, the activate / ambient / deactivate sounds, the "Bring Home the Beacon" and "Beaconator" advancements) and the compass that points at the world spawn or a lodestone |
| **Added** | Beacon 1.4 (menu 1.4.2), stained-glass beam 1.8, beacon sounds 1.9, lodestones 1.16 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-21: FC's beacon runs; the pyramid's block decides the power; no 1.20.1 menu; a soulforged steel beacon binds the respawn on right-click, overriding the random respawn (#054). 2026-10-22: ender chests get 4.B's antenna tiers in full, as their own unit after this round |
| **Conflict** | 1.20.1 opened its menu on a click (`BeaconBlock.use` overrides `use` without super) and applied its own powers. FC's beacon never ran: the cell holds a 1.20.1 block entity, FC was handed a `TileEntityBridge` it would cast, HookPolicy's block-entity guard kept FC's USE and REMOVE off (#036), nothing ticked `FCTileEntityBeacon`, its `btw.modern.TileEntityBeacon` superclass was a stateless stub, FC's pyramid rule stopped at y=0, FC's Fortune, Looting and True Sight potions were no MobEffects and FC's ambient flag was dropped, the magnetic-field and looting lists were neither saved nor read, and the compass ignored them. In the End (and above the Nether roof) FC's sky test was always false: 1.20.1 has no sky light there. FC's beacon effects reached no player while FC's player list was empty (#047) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's beacon owns the behaviour; 1.20.1 keeps the block entity, the beam, its hum, the advancements and the lodestone |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_beacon`, `magnetic_compass` and `sky_column`. [`FcBackedBlockEntities`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcBackedBlockEntities.java) and `FcBackedBlockEntity`: an FC tile entity attached to a 1.20.1 block entity (its `fcData` sub-tag; an FC world on both sides; a copied `fcData` restarts as a new block). [`BeaconBlockEntityMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BeaconBlockEntityMixin.java): the attachment (load, save, setLevel), FC's `updateEntity` at `tick` HEAD, `updateBase` answers FC's level, `applyEffects` cancelled, the activate and deactivate sounds dropped, `createMenu` null. [`FcBeacons`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcBeacons.java): the tick, the mirrored level, the sounds, Forge's `LootingLevelEvent`. Load-time `fcext.FCTileEntityBeaconPyramidFloorMixin`: the pyramid reaches below y=0. `HookPolicy.isTeGuarded(Block, fc)` (HookPolicy.java:292): the block-entity guard lifts for FC-backed blocks, so beacon USE is FC's by the general rule, and FC also runs on an empty-handed sneak-click there. WorldBridge hands FC its own tile entity and prunes the point lists; [`FcWorldSavedData`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcWorldSavedData.java) saves `FCMagneticPoints` and `FCLootingBeacons`. Modern-Common `TileEntityBeacon`: 1.5.2's state. [`BTWMobEffects`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/BTWMobEffects.java) and `PotionMapping`: `betterthanwolves:fortune`, `looting` and `true_sight`; `PlayerBridge.addPotionEffect` keeps FC's ambient flag. The magnetic compass: [`MagneticField`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/MagneticField.java) (4.B's strongest point per player every 40 ticks), a target packet (BTWNetwork id 7), `MagneticFieldClient` (the compass target). The sky column: `WorldBridge.fcCanSeeSky` (WorldBridge.java:1766), used by both FC worlds: where a dimension has no sky light, FC's sky test reads the chunk's sky-source column. `[SELF-AUDIT/fcbe]`. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, FC-backed block entities and the beacon (FcBackedBlockEntities, FcBeacons) |
| **Effect** | Gold: haste; diamond: Fortune (BTW's fortune for BTW-harvested blocks); emerald: Looting, also ambient for every mob dying in range, 1.20.1 mobs included; lapis: True Sight; glowstone: night vision; hellfire: fire resistance and a ring of fire; iron: a strong magnetic field; soulforged steel: a right-click binds the respawn point (also an empty-handed sneak-click), and a bound, powered, in-range beacon decides the next respawn over 4.B's random respawn (#054); glass: decorative; dung block: nausea and poison unless in full soulforged armour; ender block: an ender-chest antenna (endermen; silverfish in the End). Range 20/40/80/160 by level, a column with no height limit, ambient effects, creative players exempt. One block kind per pyramid, at any y (also below y=0) and in any dimension (End beacons see the sky as in 4.B). The beam and its hum are 1.20.1's and run while FC's beacon is on; FC's wither sounds mark power-up and power-down. A beacon moved with /clone or a structure block powers up at its new place. Compasses point at the strongest magnetic point (the spawn, or a powered beacon; iron and soulforged steel pull twice as hard) and spin in the Nether and the End unless a beacon is near; a lodestone compass keeps its lodestone. FC's ambient effects (beacon, mill stone, starvation, near-death, gloom) show as ambient. In the End and above the Nether's ceiling FC sees the open sky as 4.B did, also for flowers and fishing |
| **BTW adds** | The whole 4.B beacon table; FC's Looting and Fortune as visible effects; ambient looting on 1.20.1 mobs; 4.B's magnetic field on the 1.20.1 compass |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Partly: the beam, its stained-glass colours, its hum and the beacon advancements stay 1.20.1's; lodestone compasses keep their lodestone |
| **Disables (justification)** | The 1.20.1 beacon menu and its five powers, by ruling (4.B decides the power by the pyramid's block). 1.20.1's activate and deactivate sounds, because FC's own power-up and power-down sounds mark the same edges (no edge goes unsounded) |
| **Data or code?** | Code (mixins, bridge, one packet); one atlas JSON; three lang keys |
| **Decisions** | Beacon design: D1 (the ender chest's antenna tiers: ruled 2026-10-22, 4.B's tiers in full, their own unit), D2 (a) (the hum stays, the activate and deactivate sounds go), D3 (a) (Fortune on 1.20.1-loot blocks deferred), D4 (a) (True Sight's client visuals a follow-up), D5 (a), D6 (a) (FC runs on an empty-handed sneak-click on every FC-backed block), D7 (a) (game time prunes the point lists), D8 (a) (the ender antenna loads chunks as 4.B did) |
| **Alt rejected** | A `ProxyBlockEntity` on the beacon cell (would orphan every saved `minecraft:beacon` block entity and drop the 1.20.1 renderer). A Forge capability (attached before the level exists; the tick, menu and sound changes need the mixin anyway). Writing FC's level at the tick's HEAD (hides 1.20.1's activation edge and the advancement) |
| **Alt rejected (2)** | Copying vanilla effect icons (atlas sprite aliases instead). A block-by-block sky scan for skylight-less dimensions (1.20.1's sky-source column is the same rule, kept on both sides, one array read) |
| **Known limitation** | True Sight shows no mob-spawn or spawn-chunk particles (4.B's client visuals are not ported, nor are the Ender Spectacles'). Diamond-beacon Fortune does not reach blocks 1.20.1's loot tables drop (copper ore, nether gold ore, amethyst). The soulforged steel beacon's blight never reaches grass below y=0, and antenna spawns start at y=0 (FC's 1.5.2 ranges). An ender antenna can load chunks around it when it probes for a spawn spot, as in 4.B. Other players' compasses and compasses in item frames point at the world spawn. Ender chests still open the player's 1.20.1 ender inventory: 4.B's antenna tiers are a follow-up (ruling 2026-10-22). A beacon bind saved before this version counts as an ordinary 1.20.1 respawn point until the player rebinds (#054) |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W8; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bq)): the beacon plan P-BEACON 66/66 (25/66 on 4558220), the save pair P-BEACON-PERSIST A 3/3 (0/3) and B 4/4 (1/4), and the client plan P-BEACON-C 14/14 (2/14), each the same at W9; P-MOBS 65/65 and P-BLAST 79/79. `[SELF-AUDIT/fcbe] 1 rows, beacon table ok, potions 3/3, 0 warnings (0 expected)`; the hook audit: FC-owned pairs +1, VANILLA -2, 4 known block-entity gaps (#036); no FC beacon tick or beacon looting WARN, no beacon ClassCastException; `BeaconBlockEntityMixin`'s refmap carries all nine names. Every regression plan the same |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | The beacon is 4.B's late-game goal again: what it is built from decides what it does, and soulforged steel anchors the respawn |

### #054 — 4.B's hardcore respawn

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 respawn: `PlayerList.respawn` resolves a respawn anchor, a bed or a forced `/spawnpoint` (Player.java:1326-1345), else builds the player at the world spawn within the `spawnRadius` gamerule (ServerPlayer.java:259-298); `PlayerRespawnEvent`; the respawn anchor; the world border; the integrated server's LAN state; the `doDaylightCycle` gamerule and sleeping, which move or stop the day clock; the world's default game type (set when the world is created, changed by `/defaultgamemode` and by a dedicated server's `gamemode` property at every start); Forge's `PortalSpawnEvent` |
| **Added** | Respawn anchors 1.16; `doDaylightCycle` 1.6; the rest predates 1.5.2 or is 1.20.1 structure |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-21: port 4.B's hardcore respawn (a random point 1000-2000 blocks from the original spawn, x1.5 after the Nether, x2 after the Wither, x4 after the End; the soft respawn; the early-game shared respawn; the End exit to the original spawn); a charged respawn anchor in the Nether still works; a bound soulforged-steel beacon overrides the random respawn (#053); beds never set the spawn (#037). 2026-10-22 (RESPAWN 7(a), option 3): the random respawn applies in every game mode, except in worlds created as creative, where players respawn as in 1.20.1 |
| **Conflict** | Triage CF2-U1-HS. 4.B's respawn (`FCUtilsHardcoreSpawn.HandleHardcoreSpawn`, Server FCUtilsHardcoreSpawn.java:107-188, called from vanilla/server ServerConfigurationManager.java:1184) never ran: every death respawned at the world spawn.
