The port also stored FC's beacon bind in the hardcore home (`m_HardcoreSpawnChunk`), answered 1.20.1's respawn getters from it as a forced point (ServerPlayerMixin), and copied the bind cooldown across deaths. FC's own code cannot run through the bridges: the bridge's top-block query counts water and leaves and never loads a chunk, the ground level is 64 everywhere, and `IsSinglePlayerNonLan` dereferences a null `Minecraft`. 1.20.1 fire never reached FC's lighting-time progress setter (`FCBlockPortal.tryToCreatePortal`), and FC's creative-world tests read the stand-in's SURVIVAL in every world |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's numbers, flags and spawn list, 1.20.1's world. Rebalance: none (4.B parity) |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): hardcore_respawn` and `creative_world` (shared with #048). [`PlayerListRespawnMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/PlayerListRespawnMixin.java): HEAD and RETURN of `PlayerList.respawn` (`m_11236_`), a STORE `@ModifyVariable` on its respawn point (null when 4.B ignores the point) and on its resolved Optional (the validated beacon position), and an inject before `firePlayerRespawnEvent` that keeps the point and sends 4.B's line. [`HardcoreRespawn`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/HardcoreRespawn.java): 4.B's `GetValidatedRespawnCoordinates` for FC's bind (vanilla/server EntityPlayer.java:2806-2969); a non-forced bed point is ignored; `HandleHardcoreSpawn` / `AssignNewHardcoreSpawnLocation` / `AssignPlayerToOldSpawnPos*` against 1.20.1 terrain (1.5.2's top solid non-leaves block, generating the chunk; the generator's sea level + 1 as ground; feet not in a fluid or powder snow; inside the world border; a noise pre-filter that skips oceans without generating them; a per-respawn terrain-generation budget), placed at `ServerPlayer.restoreFrom` TAIL (`onRestore`, HardcoreRespawn.java:244) before the respawn packet; every respawn starts with 4.B's fresh-player home, time, bind cooldown and dimension-switch time. Radii, multipliers, the soft radius, the day window and the Nether gate come from FC's statics. The singleplayer morning skip and thunder clear. `appliesTo` (:578) is `!WorldInfoBridge.isCreativeWorld(server)` (WorldInfoBridge.java:107: the world's default game type is creative), read once per respawn; `WorldInfoBridge.getGameType` (:128) answers FC's world game type from the same value. The spawn list is saved (`FCSpawnLocations`, FcWorldSavedData) and pruned in `WorldBridge.ModSpecificTick` (`validateSpawnLocationList`, WorldBridge.java:3848). `WorldInfoBridge.getTerrainType` (:86) answers large biomes from the overworld's noise settings (shared with #048). `PlayerBridge.setSpawnChunk` writes 1.20.1's respawn point and remembers FC's bind (`fcBindPos`). [`NetherPortalProgress`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/NetherPortalProgress.java): a LOWEST-priority listener of Forge's `PortalSpawnEvent` calls FC's `FCUtilsWorld.GameProgressSetNetherBeenAccessedServerOnly` on server levels. `[SELF-AUDIT/hcspawn]`. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Hardcore respawn (HardcoreRespawn) |
| **Effect** | A death with no usable respawn point puts the player at a random dry spot 1000-2000 blocks from the original spawn (x1.5, x2 and x4 with progress, x4 again on Large Biomes). A death within a day of that assignment puts them within 100 blocks of it with 10 health and 6 less food (at least 12). Before the Nether, a death within a day of anyone's latest assignment puts them with that player. Leaving the End takes the player to the original spawn and keeps the respawn point. A charged anchor, a valid `/spawnpoint` and a bound, powered, in-range soulforged-steel beacon (#053) still decide the respawn and clear the hardcore home; a bed point saved by an earlier version is ignored. In singleplayer a new point starts the next morning without thunder. Lighting a Nether portal sets the Nether progress flag at once, as in 4.B; the portal's random tick still sets it too, and FC's campfire lighting posts the same event (#058). In a world created as Creative (its default game mode is Creative) the player respawns as in 1.20.1 (the respawn anchor, `/spawnpoint` or bound beacon, else the world spawn), and leaving the End goes to the respawn point; Survival, Adventure and Hardcore worlds use the hardcore respawn for every player, whatever their own game mode. In creative worlds BTW's own creative rules apply again, as in 4.B: no possession, no arcane scrolls from BTW mobs, no mutant calves, no villager level-ups from trades, no wolf infection or dire wolves |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's death cost: distance from the base, the soft respawn and the early-game shared respawn, scaled by BTW's progress flags; anchors, beacons and the recovery compass gain their 4.B worth |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1 resolves anchors, `/spawnpoint` and modded respawn blocks; 4.B resolves FC's beacon bind (in every world) and every respawn without a point; in a creative world 1.20.1 decides every respawn except FC's beacon bind |
| **Disables (justification)** | The ServerPlayerMixin respawn getters, which made FC's hardcore home a forced 1.20.1 respawn point; a bed point saved before #037 (beds ruling) |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one mixin (5 injectors), two classes, edits to PlayerBridge, ServerPlayerMixin, FcWorldSavedData, WorldBridge, WorldInfoBridge, BedSpawnPolicy, FCGameplayBridge, VillageInhabitants and BTWLifecycle |
| **Alt rejected** | Calling FC's `HandleHardcoreSpawn` through the bridges (the bridge queries above would spawn players on lakes, fail on every unloaded column and throw in singleplayer). Moving the player in `PlayerRespawnEvent` (after the respawn packet: a second teleport and chunk load). `@Redirect`s of `getRespawnPosition` / `findRespawnPositionAndUseSpawnBlock` (exclusive). Setting FC's world type at server start (after the spawn area generates) |
| **Known limitation** | The point search loads and generates terrain on the server thread, as 4.B did, bounded by a 3-second budget per respawn (`-Dbtw.hardcoreRespawn.budgetMs`); when it is spent the respawn falls back to the original spawn. The noise pre-filter also rejects spots 1.20.1 adds after the noise stage (icebergs, mangrove roots, eroded-badlands pillars, trees on low ground), which 4.B's literal test would accept. FC's day is 1.20.1's day clock: a slept night counts, and with `doDaylightCycle false` the one-day windows never end. Whether a world is creative follows its default game mode, as in 4.B: `/defaultgamemode`, or a dedicated server's `gamemode` property (applied at every start), turns the hardcore respawn off or on from the next death. On an existing world the first death after updating already uses the new respawn. Mystery meat on a player's death is not ported |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W9; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (br)): the respawn plan P-HCR 53/53 (23/53 on 4558220; its F2b, the Nether flag set at the lighting, passes through NetherPortalProgress), the creative-world plan P-HCR-CW 26/26 (20/26), Large Biomes P-HCR-LB 8/8 (2/8; terrain generation 235, 243 and 590 ms), the save pair P-HCR-PERSIST 1/1 and 2/2, the beacon-bind plan P-HCR-BEACON 9/9 (void before #053) and the client plan P-HCR-C 6/6 (3/6); P-BEACON 66/66, P-VLB, P-VCURE and P-VPLACE unchanged. `[SELF-AUDIT/hcspawn]` with the hook on, hardcore respawn on, FC radius 1000-2000 (x1 x1), soft radius 100, 24000 ticks, spawn effect 22223, budget 3000 ms and 0 warnings; P-HCR-CW logs `creative world true ... -> hardcore respawn off`; P-HCR logs exactly one terrain-generation budget WARN (its budget row). Refmap `PlayerListRespawnMixin` `m_11236_`; ServerPlayerMixin keeps `m_9015_` and loses `m_8961_`, `m_8963_` and `m_8964_`. Every regression plan the same, after three plan corrections, each proven against FC's source (a Nether beacon lights only under bedrock, a peaceful world heals the soft respawn's health, deep strata keep a lesser pickaxe from sticking in stone) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Death costs distance again, as in 4.B; anchors, beacons and the recovery compass are worth their price |

### #055 — Torches, soul torches, ladders, snow, pressure plates and rails follow 4.B's support rule; the redstone torch places as in 4.B

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's torch, wall torch, soul torch, soul wall torch, redstone torch, redstone wall torch and ladder, which 1.20.1 places and keeps by `canSurvive` (`TorchBlock`, `WallTorchBlock`, `LadderBlock`; the redstone torches inherit or delegate it): any sturdy face, glowstone, redstone lamps, TNT, pistons, beacons and the tops of glass panes and iron bars included. Its floor attachments, by the same test: the snow layer (`SnowLayerBlock.canSurvive`: a full collision face below, or its two block tags), which 1.20.1's weather also asks before it lays snow (`Biome.shouldSnow`), the pressure plates (`BasePressurePlateBlock.canSurvive`: rigid or centre support below) and the rails (`BaseRailBlock.canSurvive`: rigid support below, asked at placement only; a rail's survival is `BaseRailBlock.shouldBeRemoved`) |
| **Added** | Soul torches: MC 1.16; the pressure plates of the other woods and of polished blackstone: MC 1.13 to 1.20; the attachment rules are 1.20.1's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-23: torches and ladders are refused by placement wherever 4.B refused them, with no pop-off window (Q-R3 (a)); soul torches take the torch's 4.B support rule (their nearest relative). 2026-10-25: a soul torch also drops at any neighbour change on a support 4.B refuses, as the torch does; the redstone torch's side placement is fixed this round (FOUND-R3). 2026-10-31: with 4.B's solid top (#061), the 1.20.1 paths that never ask FC (weather snow, a sneaking player's snow layer, pressure plate and rail) follow 4.B through the same veto, and their survival ends where 4.B's ends (a veto only; the client gated as before); the modern wooden pressure plates take the oak plate's 4.B rule and the polished blackstone plate the stone plate's, while the weighted plates stay FC's stubs (the supports' side of that ruling is #062) |
| **Conflict** | With #040's normal-cube answers, FC's own attachment rule came back for FC's neighbour check and FC's item route, but 1.20.1's placement (sneaking, 1.20.1's own route, other mods' `useOn`) still asked 1.20.1's `canSurvive`: a torch or ladder could be placed on glowstone, a redstone lamp, TNT, a piston, a beacon, the top of a glass pane or iron bars, or the side of glass or ice, then fell off at the next block change beside it (neither 4.B nor the earlier port). Soul torches have no FC identity, so they kept 1.20.1's rule everywhere. FC's item route stood a redstone torch up in mid-air beside a wall on a side click (a standing 76:0 wherever the clicked face's neighbour cell was free), and the port's redstone torch kept a full-cube collision box, so FC refused it wherever a living entity overlapped the cell (4.B's torch has none). The floor attachments: 1.20.1's weather laid snow on glass, TNT, pistons, unlit redstone lamps, a closed upper oak trapdoor and FC machines without a 4.B solid top (the BTW hopper, mill stone, cauldron, crucible, light bulbs), and a sneaking player placed snow layers, plates and rails there and on glowstone, beacons and an eight-layer snow (plates and rails also on ice, hoppers and cauldrons; plates on the oak and nether brick fences, cobblestone walls, glass panes, iron bars and anvils), where 4.B lays and places none of them (4.B's weather asks FC's snow cover rule: `World.canSnowAt`, vanilla/server World.java:2970-3002). A rail stayed when its support turned into one of these: FC's rail neighbour check never runs (BaseRailBlock overrides `neighborChanged` without the base body). 1.20.1's BlockItem asked FC's own blocks of these families only FC's `canBlockStay` (true everywhere), so a sneaking player put the soulforged plate on glass or in mid-air beside a wall and hung a BTW ladder with no side to hang from, and a BTW torch with no support was placed and dropped at once. The modern pressure plates had no FC rule at all |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's own attachment predicates answer 1.20.1's placement and survival through one veto (and the rails' own neighbour check). Rebalance: none (4.B parity) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): attach_support_rules`. [`AttachableSupportMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/AttachableSupportMixin.java) (on TorchBlock, WallTorchBlock, LadderBlock, SnowLayerBlock, BasePressurePlateBlock and BaseRailBlock; RETURN of `canSurvive`, `m_7898_`, cancellable): 1.20.1's true becomes false when [`FcAttachSupport`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcAttachSupport.java)`.refuses` (FcAttachSupport.java:309). `refuses` asks the state's rule block, its support twin's FC block where `SUPPORT_TWIN` (:145, 14 entries filled at :200-209) has one (the soul torch and soul wall torch: the torch and wall torch, FC 50; the powered rail: the rail, 66, as the port's FC block 27 is a plain block; the ten other wooden pressure plates: the oak plate, 72; the polished blackstone plate: the stone plate, 70), else the state's own FC block, with FC's metadata of the state asked: the torch family (FCBlockTorchBase: standing on `canPlaceTorchOn`, hanging from a centre hardpoint), the ladder family (`FCBlockLadderBase.CanAttachToFacing`), the redstone torch family (Modern-Common [`BlockTorch`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/BlockTorch.java), 1.5.2's BlockTorch for FC's 75 and 76: standing on `canPlaceTorchOn`, hanging from a normal cube) and three floor families, which ask FC's `canPlaceBlockAt` of the cell, a test of the block below only: ground cover (FCBlockGroundCover: the snow layer 78 and ash 1046), pressure plate (FCBlockPressurePlate: 70, 72 and the soulforged plate 230) and rail (Modern-Common `BlockRailBase`: 66, 28, 235 and 236). A floor family vetoes only over a support both views read alike (`sameOnBothViews`, FcAttachSupport.java:442: air, an FC identity, or a support relative, #062), on the server too, so any other modern support keeps 1.20.1's answer. FC's own blocks of these families (BTW's torches 1025, 1026, 1033 and 1057, ladders 1051 and 1052, ash, the soulforged plate) take FC's `canPlaceBlockAt`, the test 4.B's ItemBlock asks, which reads no orientation: [`ProxyBlock`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ProxyBlock.java)`.canSurvive` (ProxyBlock.java:933) asks `refuses` before FC's `canBlockStay`. [`RailSupportMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/RailSupportMixin.java) (RETURN of the private static `BaseRailBlock.shouldBeRemoved`, `m_49398_`, cancellable): a rail 1.20.1 keeps drops when `FcAttachSupport.railDropped` (FcAttachSupport.java:408) drops it, because FC's rule refuses the block below or the block an ascending rail climbs onto has no solid top in FC's World (4.B's rule, vanilla/server BlockRailBase.java:169-208). FC's classes are resolved by name once (one INFO line); a miss or a throw vetoes nothing. On the client [`ClientAttachSupport`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/ClientAttachSupport.java) answers from ClientFcWorld's support view (#062) where both views read the support alike (for FC's own torches and ladders, the four sides and, for a torch, the block below). [`BlockBehaviorMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/BlockBehaviorMixin.java)`.btw$neighborChanged` (BlockBehaviorMixin.java:142) drops a support twin (a soul torch, a modern plate) whose support FC's rule refuses at any neighbour change. Modern-Common `BlockTorch` (new; the redstone torches' `ConcreteTorchBlock` extends it): 1.5.2's placement (the clicked face first, then a search), no collision box, and `onPostBlockPlaced` tells a wall torch's six neighbours. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Attachment support (FcAttachSupport) and Placement validity (WorldBridge) |
| **Effect** | Torches, soul torches and ladders can no longer be placed on glowstone, redstone lamps, TNT, pistons, beacons, the tops of glass panes and iron bars, or the sides of glass and ice, as in 4.B, by any placement path; a soul torch already standing on such a support drops at the next block change beside it, as a torch does. A redstone torch clicked onto a block's side hangs on that wall (never on glowstone's side), it no longer stands in mid-air beside the clicked face, and it no longer has a collision box, so a mob or player in the cell no longer blocks it; the blocks beside a wall torch BTW hangs hear of it at once; a standing redstone torch is refused on top of glowstone and ice, as in 4.B, since FC's World answers 4.B's solid top (#061). Stone, deepslate, fence and wall tops, spawners and the tops of glass and ice stay supports. 1.20.1's weather lays no snow, and a sneaking player places no snow layer, pressure plate or rail, where 4.B refuses them: on glass, glowstone, redstone lamps, TNT, pistons, beacons, a closed upper oak trapdoor, an eight-layer snow and FC machines without a 4.B solid top; plates and rails also not on ice, hoppers or cauldrons, and plates not on fences, walls, panes, iron bars or anvils. A snow layer or plate already there goes at its next neighbour or shape change and a rail at its next neighbour change, an ascending rail also when the block it climbs onto loses its solid top. The modern wooden plates and the polished blackstone plate take the oak and stone plate's rule, at placement and at any neighbour change. A sneaking player can no longer hang a BTW ladder with no side to hang from, place a BTW torch with no support, or put the soulforged plate or ash on glass or in mid-air; the client predicts these refusals where it reads the support as the server does |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's attachment rules on every torch, ladder, snow layer, pressure plate and rail placement and on 1.20.1's weather snow, the soul torch and the modern plates included, and 4.B's redstone torch placement |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's placement, weather and survival run as before and only take FC's refusals; supports FC's rule accepts keep 1.20.1's answer, and a snow layer, plate or rail over a modern support with neither an FC identity nor a support relative keeps 1.20.1's answer; other mods' torches, plates and rails are not support twins |
| **Data or code?** | Code: two mixins, two new bridge classes, a branch in an existing mixin, a line in ProxyBlock's `canSurvive`, one Modern-Common class |
| **Decisions** | Q-R3 (a) (refused by placement, ruled 2026-10-23); the redstone torch's 4.B placement rule (FOUND-R3, Q-P1 (a)); the floor families (ruled 2026-10-31): a veto only, survival ends too, the client gated as before; the powered rail asks the rail's rule through a support twin; the activator rail and the weighted plates, FC's stubs of blocks 4.B removed, take no rule |
| **Alt rejected** | Keeping 1.20.1's supports for the torches and ladders 1.20.1 places (Q-R3 (b)): they would pop at the next block change beside them, neither 4.B nor 1.20.1. Holding #040's normal-cube answers until this rule was ready (Q-R3 (c)). An FC identity for the soul torches: it would give them FC callbacks, drops and an item route they never had. A mixin on `Biome.shouldSnow` (the player's snow layer and its pop-off would be missed). FC's rail neighbour check as a NOTIFY hook (Modern-Common's `BlockRailBase` tests only below, and FC's rail bodies would run beside 1.20.1's). The rails' survival in AttachableSupportMixin (`shouldBeRemoved` exists in BaseRailBlock alone, so the inject would need `require = 0` on five targets) |
| **Known limitation** | 1.20.1 still lets its torches attach to a BTW pane (support faces cached at `BlockPos.ZERO` in an empty world, where a pane is the full cell); 4.B allowed no torch on a grate. World generation and readers off the server and render threads get no veto: snow the 1.20.1 world generator lays on a support 4.B refuses (glass, TNT or lamps open to the sky in a snowy biome; rare) goes at its first neighbour change (follow-up FU-WGSNOW). FC's own route still accepts a powered rail anywhere, because the port's FC block 27 is a plain block: the rail is placed and drops at once as an item, where 4.B refuses the click (follow-up FU-RAIL27). The veto never grants, so the supports FC accepts and 1.20.1 refuses (leaves tops, the stub weighted plates, activator rails, trapped chests; snow on bottom slabs, fences and walls) stay refused on 1.20.1's paths. FC's own neighbour check of the snow layer and of plates 70 and 72 (BlockBehaviorMixin's NOTIFY) still asks the stand-in of a modern support at a neighbour change, so a snow layer on a honey block goes then. The client does not predict over a support only the server reads (a modern block without a support relative, a double slab): if the server then refuses a sneaking placement, the client keeps its predicted stack count until the next slot sync. No client plan row covers the snow layer and rail prediction. A redstone wall torch a turntable moves still does not tell its new neighbours |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-25/26, 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-31 to 2026-11-03; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W1 and W10 with its solid-top fix, SUPPORT-VETO, SUPPORT-REL and guard batch; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bs)): the placement plan P-RANB-PLACE 114 of 117 at W1 (59/117 on 4558220; its three redstone torch rows waited for W10 by design), 115 of 117 at W10 and 117/117 since #061; the client plan P-RANB-PLACE-C 13/13 (7/13) with its probe gate (a torch, a ladder and a soul torch kept in hand: no client desync); refmap `AttachableSupportMixin` `canSurvive` -> `m_7898_`; `Mixin config OK (55 entries ...)` at that build; every regression plan the same at W1 apart from P-PLACE's row H4 (#040). The redstone torch at W10: P-FOUND-RT 20 of 24 (12/24 on 4558220): the side clicks, the wall torch on glowstone's side, the missing collision box and the clicked face passed, and the rows of a standing redstone torch on glowstone's or ice's top (R4, R7, R8, R15, and P-RANB-PLACE's C11 and V25) failed until FC's World answered 4.B's solid top (#061, 2026-10-31): P-FOUND-RT 24/24. The floor families (2026-10-31): P-SUPPORT-VETO 106/106 (48 GAP, 41 CONTROL, 17 SANITY; 58/106 before, every GAP failing), over 1200 ticks of rain on glass, glowstone, lamp and stone roofs in a snowy biome, sneaking placements (snow layers, plates, rails, the soulforged plate, a BTW ladder and a BTW torch), `canSurvive` asked directly, rails, plates and snow whose support changes, and FC's route for the powered rail; `Mixin config OK (78 entries ...)`; the refmap maps `RailSupportMixin` `shouldBeRemoved` to `m_49398_`; the INFO line `FC attachment rules resolved: FCBlockTorchBase, FCBlockLadderBase, FCBlockGroundCover and FCBlockPressurePlate ...`. With the modern plate twins (2026-11-02, #062): P-SUPPORT-VETO amended (C12, V16 and V18 flip as designed) 106/106 and P-SUPPORT-REL 100/100. W10's guard batch (2026-11-03, after #061, #062 and the floor families; `Mixin config OK (79 entries ...)`, LinkAudit 5): P-FOUND-RT 24/24, P-RANB-PLACE 117/117, P-FOUND-RS 80/80 and P-FOUND-CARTS 80/80 (`--wave W10`, GATE after OK), P-USE 39/39, P-PLACE 28/29 and P-RM the same as their last records, SET-CRASH the same; the clients P-RANB-PLACE-C 13/13 with its probe gate, placeconsume 30/30 and P-FOUND-RS-C 9/9 the same. W10's gate passed |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Torches, ladders, snow, plates and rails go only where 4.B lets them, whoever or whatever places them |

### #056 — BTW's pressure plates, buttons and detector rails follow 4.B

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's pressure plates, buttons and detector rails: plates and buttons press from their own entity checks (a wooden button for an `AbstractArrow`, re-checked each `ticksToStayPressed`), `DetectorRailBlock.checkPressed` presses for any minecart and gives a comparator reading of its carts, and minecarts ride only `BaseRailBlock`s in `#minecraft:rails`; the comparator, which reads a container's `getAnalogOutputSignal` |
