| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-23 (FOUND-R3): the soulforged steel pressure plate, BTW detector rails, comparators on the BTW chest and brick oven, mobs on pressure plates and BTW arrows on wooden buttons are fixed this round; BTW containers give 1.5.2's comparator readings, and the comparator itself stays removed (#019); the vanilla detector rail 28 follows 4.B (an empty cart does not trigger it); BTW's detector rails are 1.20.1 detector rails (Q-RS1 (a), Q-RS2 (a)) |
| **Conflict** | The soulforged steel pressure plate (230) never pressed: Modern-Common's plate bodies were stubs. No mob pressed the vanilla plates (70, 72): FC mobs are puppets that never move through `Entity.move`, and BTW's own collision call hit the same stub. FC's arrows (BTW puppets, not `AbstractArrow`s) never held a wooden button, which 1.20.1's re-check released at once. The BTW chest (1035) and the brick oven (1023/1024) gave no comparator reading, and an inventory change told no comparator. BTW's detector rails (wood 235, soulforged steel 236) were ProxyBlocks: carts derailed on them, FC's cart check never saw a 1.20.1 cart, and Modern-Common's BlockDetectorRail had no power answers, so they never powered anything. The vanilla detector rail 28 pressed for an empty cart, which 4.B's does not |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's 1.5.2 contact and power bodies on the Modern-Common bases; 1.20.1 runs the rails and the buttons' re-check with FC's rule as their filter. Rebalance: none (4.B parity) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_redstone`. Modern-Common: [`BlockBasePressurePlate`](Modern-Common/src/main/java/btw/modern/BlockBasePressurePlate.java) gets 1.5.2's plate mechanism (tick rate 20, the update tick, entity contact, the mob-type test, the sensitive box, the power answers), and `BlockPressurePlate` keeps its mob type (230 players, 70 mobs, 72 everything); `BlockButton` 1.5.2's arrow contact (wooden buttons); `BlockChest` 1.5.2's `getInventory` and comparator answers, `BlockFurnace` the comparator answers (the brick oven), and `TileEntity.onInventoryChanged` tells the comparators (Forge's `updateNeighbourForOutputSignal`); `BlockDetectorRail` 1.5.2's power and comparator answers. [`ButtonBlockMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/ButtonBlockMixin.java) (HEAD of `checkPressed`, `m_51120_`): an arrow-pressable button that holds no `AbstractArrow` but an FC arrow runs 1.20.1's own pressed branch. [`FcDetectorRailBlock`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcDetectorRailBlock.java): 235 and 236 are registered as this 1.20.1 detector rail under their ProxyBlock names, in `#minecraft:rails`, with FC's identity (WorldBridge's rail codec: the shape, plus 8 when pressed) and BTW's textures. [`DetectorRailBlockMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/DetectorRailBlockMixin.java) (`@ModifyArg` of the cart predicate of the one `getInteractingMinecartOfType` call in `checkPressed`, `m_52432_` / `m_52436_`): every DetectorRailBlock (28, 235, 236) counts only the carts FC's `ShouldPlateActivateBasedOnMinecart` accepts, with each cart's 1.5.2 type and rider from its FC view (#057). WorldBridge's plate, button and detector rail codecs round-trip every 1.5.2 bit; TickPolicy and HookPolicy rows for the plates and rails (the rails' INSIDE hook is 1.20.1's equivalent body); `[SELF-AUDIT/rails]`. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Redstone and entity views (FcDetectorRailBlock, VanillaEntityViews) |
| **Effect** | The soulforged steel plate presses for players only, the stone plate for any mob or player and the wooden plate for every entity, BTW's mobs included, as in 4.B. A BTW arrow presses and holds a wooden button. A comparator (removed from survival, #019) reads BTW chests and the brick oven as 1.5.2 did, and hears their inventory change. BTW's wooden and soulforged steel detector rails are rails minecarts run over, and they power by BTW's rule: the wooden rail for any cart, the soulforged steel rail for a cart carrying a player; the vanilla detector rail presses only for a cart that is not empty (a storage or furnace cart, or a ridden one), as in 4.B. The detector rails' cart filter and the block dispenser's carts and boats go through the same entity views (#057) |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's three plate kinds and its detector rail rules on 1.20.1's rails and redstone; BTW's arrows on wooden buttons; comparator readings for BTW's containers |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's plates, buttons, rails, power and comparator code run unchanged for blocks without an FC rule; another mod's detector rail keeps 1.20.1's cart rule |
| **Data or code?** | Code (two mixins, one block class, Modern-Common ports, codecs) and data (blockstates, models and the rails tag for 235 and 236) |
| **Alt rejected** | Making ProxyBlock a rail (it cannot extend BaseRailBlock). FC's contact body as an FC-owned INSIDE hook on the rails (a second press path beside 1.20.1's own, with its own tick and comparator timing). A redirect of the rail's whole cart query (it would rebuild the private search box), or of the button's `AbstractArrow` query (typed and cast, so a puppet cannot join it) |
| **Known limitation** | BTW detector rails saved before this version reload as north-south, unpowered rails (their saved ProxyBlock metadata has no 1.20.1 property; they never worked before). Rerunning the manual `ModelGenerator` would point block_235/236's item models back at the ProxyBlock-era models |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W10; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bt)): the redstone plan P-FOUND-RS 80/80 (61/80 on 4558220) and its client plan P-FOUND-RS-C 9/9 (6/9). `[SELF-AUDIT/rails] ... FC's cart rule on 3/3 ids, 0 warnings`; the tick audit's block_235 and block_236 (`random=NONE gated=0/24 sched=VANILLA`); the hook audit's `minecraft:detector_rail` `INSIDE=NOTIFY[equivalent:detector-rail]`; ProxyBlocks 182 -> 180 (235 and 236 are detector rails now). Refmap `ButtonBlockMixin` `m_51120_`, `DetectorRailBlockMixin` `m_52432_` / `m_52436_`; build `Mixin config OK (77 entries ...)`. W10's guard batch (2026-11-03, after #055's floor families, #061 and #062): P-FOUND-RS 80/80 `--wave W10`, GATE after OK, and P-FOUND-RS-C 9/9, the same as before; W10's gate passed |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | BTW's redstone with plates, buttons and detector rails works as in 4.B |

### #057 — BTW sees 1.20.1 minecarts, boats, paintings, XP orbs and mobs

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | The entities 1.20.1 owns outright: minecarts of every type, boats and chest boats, paintings, experience orbs, armor stands and other 1.20.1-only mobs, item frames, primed TNT, arrows, falling blocks; 1.20.1's boat items per wood type |
| **Added** | Most predate 1.5.2 as entities; boats per wood type 1.9, chest boats 1.19 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-23 (FOUND-R3): BTW's hopper and block dispenser work with 1.20.1 minecarts. 2026-10-25: the block dispenser picks up a 1.20.1 boat as that boat's own item, wood and chest kept (Q-CARTS-3 (a)). Q-CARTS-1 (a) and Q-CARTS-2 (a): 1.20.1-only living entities are EntityLiving to FC, and FC's every-entity queries see them |
| **Conflict** | FC finds entities through its World (`getEntitiesWithinAABB`, the untyped `getEntitiesWithinAABBExcludingEntity`, `getEntityByID`) and is handed them in its block contact callbacks (`onEntityCollidedWithBlock`, `onEntityWalking`, `onFallenUpon`). The port answered those with BTW's own entities, players and items only, and handed a generic bridge to the callbacks, so FC never saw a 1.20.1 minecart, boat, painting, orb or 1.20.1-only mob as the 1.5.2 class 4.B gave it: the BTW hopper ejected beside a chest minecart instead of filling it, the block dispenser took the rail and not the cart or boat, the detector block and BTW's detector rails ignored them, the hopper never swallowed an orb, and the mining charge, bellows, platforms, falling blocks and FC mobs' pushes passed through them. Minecarts and boats FC placed became generic BTW puppets that nothing drew and nothing could ride |
| **Pattern** | **B** (bridge): every entity FC finds is the entity in the world, seen as the 1.5.2 class 4.B gave it; **D** for the block dispenser's boat item. Rebalance: none (4.B parity) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): fc_entity_views`. [`VanillaEntityViews`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/VanillaEntityViews.java)`.of`, the one function every entry point asks, in this order: a BTW puppet's own FC object; a player's PlayerBridge; an item's ItemEntityBridge; a living entity's LivingEntityBridge; a class view of a minecart (`btw.modern.EntityMinecart`, `getMinecartType` 0-6, a container cart also an `IInventory` over its 1.20.1 container), a boat (`EntityBoat`), a painting (`EntityPainting`) or an orb (`EntityXPOrb`, `xpValue` = value x count); else a generic view. Each view is cached per entity (weak keys) and re-synced each time FC is handed it (id, position with 1.5.2's `posY = minY + yOffset`, motion, rotation, liveness, size, box, rider); FC's writes go back: setDead discards it (a container cart spills, as 1.5.2's did), `attackEntityFrom` is its own hurt, `addVelocity` / `setVelocity` / `setPosition` move it, a cart's or boat's collision pushes both by 1.5.2's paired formula, and field writes are flushed as deltas at the next sync and at the end of the level tick. Entry points: WorldBridge's three queries and the three contact-callback wrappers (ProxyBlock, BlockMixin, BlockBehaviorMixin); the entity bridges sync their box and map FC's `mountEntity` on a view to 1.20.1 riding. [`FcVehicles`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcVehicles.java): an FC object of a frozen 1.5.2 minecart or boat class is spawned as the 1.20.1 entity, and a saved generic puppet of such a class converts at the end of the tick its chunk loads in. Load-time [`FCBlockBlockDispenserBoatItemMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCBlockBlockDispenserBoatItemMixin.java) (`@Pseudo`, `remap = false`): the block dispenser files a boat it took under the item its boat view recorded. `[SELF-AUDIT/views]`. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Redstone and entity views (FcDetectorRailBlock, VanillaEntityViews) |
| **Effect** | BTW's hopper fills a chest or hopper minecart and swallows 1.20.1 XP orbs; the block dispenser takes a minecart or boat in front of it, files a boat as that boat's own item (wood and chest kept; the oak boat is FC's boat item) and places 1.20.1 minecarts and boats; the detector block sees 1.20.1 entities; a mining charge, bellows, platforms and falling blocks reach them; FC mobs see and push 1.20.1 entities, as 4.B's mobs did every entity. The detector rails' cart filter (#056) reads each cart's type and rider through the same views |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's machines and mobs act on every entity of the 1.20.1 world, as 4.B's acted on every entity of its own |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — the 1.20.1 entities, their AI, physics, rendering and items are unchanged; FC's writes reach them through their own methods; FC projectiles keep 1.20.1's projectile filter for entities with no FC object of their own |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one bridge class, one spawn helper, one load-time patch, edits to the entity bridges and the contact wrappers |
| **Alt rejected** | A view per caller (the hopper, the dispenser, the detector rail): one type map each, every other caller left blind. A projectile-only rule. FC subclasses of the frozen cart and boat classes spawned as BTW puppets (drawn by nothing and ridden by nothing, as the old generic puppet was). 4.B's literal boat item for every boat (Q-CARTS-3 (b): a spruce boat would come back oak, a chest boat without its chest) |
| **Known limitation** | FC's every-entity effects (the detector block, platforms and bellows, the mining charge, FC mobs' pushes) also reach 1.20.1's technical entities, which 4.B never had: markers, display and interaction entities, item frames, leash knots; the detector block turns on for them, and a platform can move them. FC's wither may target an armor stand (1.20.1's own skips them). FC field writes on a 1.20.1 mob outside the synced set (a cow kick's motion) are not written back. A 1.20.1 mob gets FC's share of a push and its own, where a BTW puppet keeps FC's only. A BTW minecart or boat puppet saved by an earlier build with no FC state is removed by its first tick |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W10; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bu)): the entity plan P-FOUND-CARTS 80/80 (46/80 on 4558220), over the typed, untyped and by-id queries, the hopper and a chest or hopper minecart, the block dispenser and carts and boats (a boat back as its own item), dispenser-placed carts and boats, the detector block, an XP orb and the mining charge. `[SELF-AUDIT/views] 75 view overrides ... 0 warnings`; no mixin apply error for the boat-item patch; build `Mixin config OK (77 entries ...)`. W10's guard batch (2026-11-03): P-FOUND-CARTS 80/80 `--wave W10`, GATE after OK; W10's gate passed. P-MOBS, which reads FC's entity queries, has no W10 run recorded |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | BTW's hoppers, block dispensers and detectors work with 1.20.1's minecarts, boats and orbs, as in 4.B |

### #058 — Nether portals lit by a campfire follow 1.20.1's frames

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1 Nether portals: `PortalShape` (finds a frame on either axis, any interior from 2x3 to 21x21; `isEmpty` decides what counts as open interior), `createPortalBlocks` (the cells' `axis`), Forge's cancellable `PortalSpawnEvent`, and `NetherPortalBlock.updateShape`, which keeps a portal only while its frame is valid along its axis |
| **Added** | Portals predate 1.5.2; frames larger than 2x3 since MC 1.7 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-23 (FOUND-R3): the 1.20.1 Nether portal bug found this round is fixed this round; campfire-lit portals go through 1.20.1's PortalShape. Defaults kept: a campfire opens any frame fire opens (2x3 to 21x21), and the campfire path has no dimension gate, as in 4.B |
| **Conflict** | FC's campfire lighting (`FCBlockCampfire.SetOnFireDirectly` -> `FCBlockPortal.tryToCreatePortal`) ran FC's own 2x3 test and wrote every cell as `setBlock(90, 0)`, which WorldBridge resolves to `nether_portal[axis=x]`: a north-south frame got east-west cells, drew turned, lost a column at its first east or west neighbour update and built its exit portal along x, and frames larger than 2x3 never opened. 1.20.1's `PortalShape.isEmpty` counted FC's campfires as solid, so fire never lit a frame with a campfire inside, which 4.B lit. (The earlier suspicion that FC's 1.5.2 neighbour check breaks larger 1.20.1 portals was refuted: FC's hook for id 90 is an empty body, and 1.20.1's `updateShape` decides validity at every size.) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): 1.20.1's PortalShape creates, sizes and orients every portal; FC keeps its lighting entry, its interior rule (the four campfires) and its random tick (possession, the Nether flag). Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): nether_portal`. [`NetherPortalShapes`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/NetherPortalShapes.java): `tryToCreatePortal`, on a server WorldBridge only: `PortalShape.findEmptyPortalShape` (axis X, then Z, as BaseFireBlock), Forge's `PortalSpawnEvent` (`ForgeEventFactory.onTrySpawnPortal`), then `createPortalBlocks`; a throw is logged once and answers false. `isFcPortalInterior`: a ProxyBlock whose legacy id is one of FC's four campfires (unlit, small, medium, large; resolved once FC is ready, one INFO line). Load-time [`FCBlockPortalShapeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCBlockPortalShapeMixin.java) (`@Pseudo`, `remap = false`; HEAD of `FCBlockPortal.tryToCreatePortal`, `require = 1`) returns the bridge's answer, so FC's 2x3 test, its `setBlock(90, 0, 2)` writes and its own flag call no longer run. [`PortalShapeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/PortalShapeMixin.java) (RETURN of `PortalShape.isEmpty`, `m_77717_`, common side): a false answer becomes true for an FC campfire. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Nether portals (NetherPortalShapes) |
| **Effect** | A campfire lit inside an obsidian frame opens a portal along the frame's own axis in any frame a fire would fill (2x3 to 21x21), through Forge's PortalSpawnEvent; north-south portals lit by a campfire no longer lose a column when a block is placed beside them. A fire lights a frame with a campfire inside, lit or not, as in 4.B (the campfire's cell becomes portal, as 4.B's `setBlock(90)` did). FC's portal random tick (possession, the Nether flag) is unchanged, and the one PortalSpawnEvent sets the Nether flag for fire and campfire lighting alike (#054) |
| **BTW adds** | BTW's campfire lighting and its campfire-in-the-frame rule on 1.20.1's portal frames of every size |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's frame search, portal blocks, travel and PortalSpawnEvent are unchanged; fire keeps its own Overworld and Nether gate |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one bridge class and two mixins |
| **Alt rejected** | An axis branch in WorldBridge's id-90 write: dead code after this route, and two different frame rules. Porting 1.5.2's `onNeighborBlockChange` for portals: it would create exactly the suspected large-portal bug. Capping portals at 2x3. Routing fire through FC's lighting: two portal attempts would run |
| **Known limitation** | A campfire opens every frame fire opens, so a large portal possesses as often as that many cells of 2x3 portals (FC's possession tick runs per cell). A campfire in a frame in the End opens a portal, which 1.20.1 sends to the Nether, as 1.5.2's travel did (no dimension gate on the campfire path). A mod that cancels PortalSpawnEvent also stops a campfire-lit portal; the campfire then stays lit and FC's netherrack fallback runs as for a failed frame. North-south portals a campfire lit before this version keep their east-west cells until relit. No client plan proves the `isEmpty` mixin (the client applies the same class to the same PortalShape bytes) |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W10; noted in `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (br)): the portal plan P-FPORTAL 38/38 (33/38 on 4558220), over FC-lit 2x3 frames on both axes, a 3x4 frame, a fire-lit frame with a campfire inside and the campfire path itself; its server log has exactly one `[nether_portal] FC's campfire blocks [1013, 1014, 1015, 1016] count as open portal interior` and no failure line. Refmap `PortalShapeMixin` `m_77717_`; build `Mixin config OK (77 entries ...)`; no mixin apply error. W10's gate passed (2026-11-03). P-HCR (fire lights a 2x3, the Nether flag through NetherPortalProgress) has no W10 run recorded |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Campfire-lit portals work at any frame size and in the frame's own orientation, and a campfire in a frame no longer blocks fire |

### #059 — BTW's base classes draw 1.5.2's icons again; the build composes the lifted wire's cross

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's block and item rendering by baked models and atlas sprites, which never asks a block or item for a 1.5.2 icon; the places where FC's own client code still asks one: an item a mob holds or wears, the block an enderman carries, a rail or redstone wire a platform lifts, the potion a witch drinks, the BTW hopper's filter icon, and every block FC draws itself (wicker panes, plates, the workbench, mushroom caps) |
| **Added** | Baked models: MC 1.8; 1.5.2's icon registration is gone from 1.20.1 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-23 (FOUND-R3): the wicker pane's missing texture and the lost texture code on vanilla-only block and item bases are fixed this round. 2026-10-25: 1.5.2's icon code is restored in every Modern-Common base class, dead ones too; a platform-lifted redstone wire uses a cross texture composed at build time from 1.20.1's dust textures (no 1.5.2 art shipped) |
| **Conflict** | The port's Modern-Common stand-ins of 1.5.2's block and item base classes had dropped 1.5.2's icon members (the texture names the constructor takes, `registerIcons` and the icon getters), so an FC class on such a base registered its unlocalized name and answered that one icon for every side, meta and damage; most of those names map to no 1.20.1 texture, so FCBakedModel drew `minecraft:missingno`. The wicker pane (1084) drew the missing texture in all 70 of its quads; the soulforged steel plate (230), the workbench (1034) and the mushroom caps (1078, 1079) drew wrong faces, and the hopper's filter icon was lost. Mobs holding or wearing wool, logs, slabs, fence gates, walls, jukeboxes, dispensers, furnaces, pistons, lamps, enchanting tables or dyes, endermen carrying logs, pumpkins or mycelium, and platforms lifting redstone wire drew the missing texture; a lifted curved rail drew straight, spruce leaves drew as oak, chiseled stone bricks and sandstone as plain, a fern as grass, a birch sapling as oak and a splash potion as drinkable |
| **Pattern** | **D** (closest; 4.B parity restored in port code): each Modern-Common base keeps its 1.5.2 icon members with 1.5.2's bodies, and every name they register resolves to a 1.20.1 sprite. Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tags `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): legacy_icons` and `legacy_icon_textures`. Modern-Common: the block and item bases carry 1.5.2's icon members (texture names, `registerIcons`, `getIcon` / `getIconFromDamage` / `getIconFromDamageForRenderPass` and the named getters), the wicker part included (`BlockPane` keeps its texture names, `BlockBasePressurePlate` and `BlockPressurePlate` take a name, `BlockMushroomCap`, `BlockWorkbench`, `BlockChest`, `BlockDetectorRail`); `BlockGlass`, `BlockIce` and `BlockPortal` pass their names to a new `BlockBreakable` name constructor; `isRepeaterPowered` moves up to `BlockRedstoneLogic`; `Block` and `Item` answer `GetHopperFilterIcon` null and `ItemBlock` forwards it to its block; `ItemBlock.getIconFromDamage` answers 1.5.2's flat item icon at the ids where the port registers a plain ItemBlock. [`FCBakedModel`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/FCBakedModel.java): 55 `VANILLA_TEXTURE_MAP` keys for the 1.5.2 names that named no 1.20.1 texture (FCBakedModel.java:1797). [`legacy-icon-textures.gradle`](Forge/gradle/legacy-icon-textures.gradle) (`composeLegacyIconTextures`, applied from `Forge/build.gradle`): composes `textures/block/redstonedust_cross.png` from 1.20.1's `redstone_dust_dot`, `_line0` and `_line1` as 1.20.1 lays out a four-way wire (1.5.2's `redstoneDust_cross` in 255 of 256 pixels), checked in and rewritten only when its pixels or bytes differ; a missing client-extra jar WARNs and skips. `BlockDoor`, `BlockFire.getIcon` and `BlockGrass.getBlockTexture` keep their port bodies by design. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Legacy icons (Modern-Common icon members, FCBakedModel) |
| **Effect** | The wicker pane shows its wicker texture, the soulforged steel plate looks like steel, the BTW workbench and mushroom blocks show their proper faces, and a hopper's filter shows the right texture (and none for items that are not filters). Blocks and items held or worn by mobs, carried by endermen or lifted by platforms show their BTW textures instead of the missing texture (logs, wool, pumpkins, mycelium, slabs, leaves, walls, gates, jukeboxes, dispensers, furnaces, pistons, enchanting tables, lamps, dyes, potions); a lifted curved rail stays curved and a lifted redstone wire shows 1.5.2's cross. Other draws change only toward 4.B's (a mob-held glass pane draws glass, a held stone plate stone) |
| **BTW adds** | Nothing new: 4.B's textures wherever FC draws a block or item |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's own models and sprites are unchanged, and resource packs keep retexturing the mapped sprites |
| **Data or code?** | Code (Modern-Common bases, one table) and one texture composed at build time |
| **Alt rejected** | Shipping 1.5.2's own `redstoneDust_cross` art (ruled out 2026-10-25: no 1.5.2 art). Restoring only the members a reachable draw uses (ruled out: dead ones too, so no later FC path meets a missing member) |
| **Known limitation** | The composed cross is written by the first build that finds 1.20.1's client-extra jar; without it a lifted wire draws the missing texture. Like the derived wood textures (#034, #051), it does not follow resource packs. Three reachable draws have no plan row yet (a mob holding iron bars, a platform lifting a vanilla detector rail, a mob holding an anvil). BlockBed's six `bed_*` faces stay unmapped: 1.20.1 beds are entity-textured, and nothing FC draws asks for them |
| **Verified by** | Proofs (round 3, 2026-10-28; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W10; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bv)): the client plans P-WICKER-C 64/64 (28 server rows and 36 client probes; 46/64 on 4558220), P-WICKER-H 26/26 (19 and 7; 21/26) and P-TEXTURES-C 56/56 (4 and 52; 19/56), with both quad-dump gates OK (the wicker blocks, the two detector rails' new rail models, every other quad identical or an atlas relocation only, no `missingno`); the name gates before landing (the design's 45 map keys, 0 problems; 161 restored names, 0 unresolved; `post_names` OK after landing). Build: `composeLegacyIconTextures: 1 texture checked (1 written)`, then `(0 written)`, the composed cross equal in pixels to its check; LinkAudit's missing members 6 -> 5 (SlotArmor's `ItemArmor.func_94602_b` now resolves) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | None (visual): BTW's blocks and items look as in 4.B wherever FC draws them |

### #060 — Entities rest where 4.B's collision boxes put them when FC swaps a block

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's push-out of entities embedded in a block (`ItemEntity.tick`'s `moveTowardsClosestSpace`) and `Block.pushEntitiesUp`, which lifts entities when a block swap fills the space they rest in (farmland to dirt) |
| **Added** | These rules are 1.20.1's |
| **Maintainer ruling** | None needed: 4.B parity (an entity rests where 4.B's collision boxes put it) |
| **Conflict** | FC reads some 1.20.1 blocks as 4.B blocks whose collision box is taller than the 1.20.1 shape: minecraft:farmland (15/16 high) is FC's farmland 60, a full box (Server FCBlockFarmlandBase.java:60-65). An item or mob lying on the 1.20.1 shape sits in that gap. When FC swapped in a block that fills it (bone meal fertilizing farmland into 204), the entity was left 1/16 inside a full block and 1.20.1 pushed it out sideways a random distance: an item 0.04-1.58 blocks away, beyond 1.5 in about 12% of runs |
| **Pattern** | **D** (closest; 4.B parity restored in port code, as #040). Rebalance: none |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): block_swap_lift`. [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java)`.setBlock` (WorldBridge.java:1199-1245): before the write, `fcCollisionGapUnderEntities` (:1272) reads the gap, only on the server thread and only for a cell that is not air and not a ProxyBlock, whose 1.20.1 collision shape is neither empty nor full and holds an entity's box: FC's own collision boxes for the cell (`addCollisionBoxesToList`, the call FC's `getCollidingBoundingBoxes` makes) minus the 1.20.1 shape; a throwing FC class is logged once per site (`SWAP-LIFT`) and lifts nothing. After a successful write, in the ProxyBlock and the vanilla branch, `liftOutOfFcCollisionGap` (:1323) runs the body of 1.20.1's `Block.pushEntitiesUp` on the part of the gap the new block's shape fills: every non-spectator entity there moves up. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Block-swap lift (WorldBridge.setBlock) |
| **Effect** | An item or mob resting on a block whose 1.20.1 shape is lower than FC's box is lifted onto the new block when FC fills that gap (an item on farmland that bone meal fertilizes stays where it lay, on top), instead of being pushed sideways. A swap into air, into a cell with no 1.20.1 collision, or into a full or ProxyBlock cell reads nothing and lifts nothing, so every other swap leaves its entities where 4.B leaves them |
| **BTW adds** | Nothing new: 4.B's resting positions on 1.20.1's shapes |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's push-out and `pushEntitiesUp` are unchanged; the lift runs only where FC's box and 1.20.1's shape differ |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one rule in WorldBridge |
| **Alt rejected** | Lifting any entity standing in the part of a cell an FC swap raises, whenever the old 1.20.1 shape was partial: it covers FC's own ProxyBlock-to-ProxyBlock swaps, where the old shape already is the 4.B box, so a mob on a loose slab that a falling slab completes would rise onto it, where 4.B leaves it inside the full block |
| **Known limitation** | Metadata-only writes (`setBlockMetadata`) are not covered. Plant twins and stand-ins, which FC reads with their live shape, have no gap. Off the server thread the rule does nothing |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, W2 fix, 2026-10-26/27; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, Round 3, W2 fix; noted in `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bi)): the bone meal plan P-BM's row P15a passes in three runs out of three, the item resting at (-27.5, -60.0, -21.5), distance 0.0000 (the W2 run read 1.5848 and failed; before the fix the push-out moved it a random 0.04-1.58, past the row's 1.5 radius in about 12% of runs since the row was written); every other P-BM row and the W2 regression set the same. Build: `Mixin config OK (59 entries ...)` |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | Items and mobs stay where 4.B leaves them when BTW's soil changes under them |

### #061 — 4.B's solid top

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's sturdy top face (`BlockStateBase.isFaceSturdy(level, pos, Direction.UP)`: the block's support shape covers the whole top face), the answer the bridge gave FC's `World.doesBlockHaveSolidTopSurface` |
| **Added** | The sturdy-face test is 1.20.1's; 1.5.2 and 4.B ask the block |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-31: with this fix, FC's World answers 4.B's hard point, and 1.20.1's paths that never ask FC (weather snow, a sneaking player's plate or rail) follow 4.B through #055's veto. The fix itself is 4.B parity; FOUND-R3's redstone torch rows found the gap at W10 |
| **Conflict** | 4.B's World answers `doesBlockHaveSolidTopSurface` with the FC block's own large centre hard point on top, `block.HasLargeCenterHardPointToFacing(world, i, j, k, 1)` (vanilla/server World.java:4786-4791, vanilla/client World.java:5190-5195), which FC's classes override; WorldBridge answered 1.20.1's sturdy top face, and ClientFcWorld a normal-cube test. FC asks this wherever 4.B asks whether something can stand on a block: the redstone torch (1.5.2's `BlockTorch.canPlaceTorchOn`, #055), pressure plates, rails, snow and ash resting on a block, fire, mushrooms (their spread, and their survival through PlantSoilMixin), stem fruit, nether growth, the mining charge, the beacon's enderman and silverfish spawns, where endermen place end stone, FC mobs' dismount and teleport cells, and 4.B's beacon respawn search (#054). With 1.20.1's answer they all stood where 4.B refuses: on glass, glowstone, ice, TNT, pistons, the redstone lamp, the beacon, a closed upper oak trapdoor, an eight-layer snow and FC blocks whose 1.20.1 shape is a full cube although FC gives them no hard point on top (light bulbs, the BTW hopper, mill stone, cauldron, crucible and more). W10's redstone torch proof found it: a standing redstone torch was placed and stayed on glowstone and ice (P-FOUND-RT R4, R7, R8 and R15; P-RANB-PLACE C11 and V25) |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend): FC's own rule on 1.20.1's world. Rebalance: none (4.B's answers) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): solid_top_surface`. [`WorldBridge`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/WorldBridge.java)`.doesBlockHaveSolidTopSurface` (WorldBridge.java:2834): the FC block at the cell's id (`getBlockId`) answers `HasLargeCenterHardPointToFacing(this, x, y, z, 1)`, so a modern block FC reads through a stand-in answers as its stand-in (the solid cube is a normal cube, a collider asks 1.20.1's sturdy face, a support relative answers as its BTW relative, #062) and a double slab as FC's double slab; only where FC has no block for the cell (a 1.20.1 block FC reads as air, such as powder snow, or any modern block if the stand-ins could not be allocated) does 1.20.1's `isFaceSturdy(UP)` answer. [`ClientFcWorld`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/ClientFcWorld.java)`.doesBlockHaveSolidTopSurface` (ClientFcWorld.java:163) answers the same rule on the client, so #055's prediction agrees with the server. [`HardcoreRespawn`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/HardcoreRespawn.java)`.aroundBeacon` (HardcoreRespawn.java:325), 4.B's search for a respawn spot around a bound beacon (#053, #054), asks the level's WorldBridge, as 4.B's vanilla/server EntityPlayer.java:2962 asks its World. No FC, Modern-Common, mixin or build change. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Solid top surface (WorldBridge, ClientFcWorld) |
| **Effect** | What may stand on a block is 4.B's answer for every FC rule that asks: a standing redstone torch, a pressure plate or a rail is refused on glass (the redstone torch keeps glass through 1.5.2's id list), glowstone, ice, TNT, pistons, the redstone lamp, the beacon, a closed upper oak trapdoor, an eight-layer snow and FC blocks with no solid top, and one already there drops at its next neighbour change; snow and ash no longer rest there; a fire with nothing to burn beside it goes out there on its next tick; mushrooms neither spread to, nor are placed on, nor survive on the ones with an FC identity; no pumpkin or melon grows there and nether growth does not spread onto them; a mining charge facing down on a cell that is not a normal cube stays attached only on a 4.B solid top (an upper slab yes, glass no); the beacon's enderman and silverfish spawns, FC mobs' dismount and teleport cells and the beacon respawn need a 4.B solid top below. On the client a top slab or an upside-down stair now holds a standing redstone torch in the prediction, as on the server |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's solid-top answer for every BTW rule that asks it: torches, plates, rails, snow, fire, mushrooms, stems, nether growth, mining charges, spawns and the beacon respawn |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — 1.20.1's own code never asks this method; a modern block FC reads through a stand-in keeps 1.20.1's answer (a support relative answers as its BTW relative, #062), and a 1.20.1 block FC reads as air keeps 1.20.1's sturdy face |
| **Data or code?** | Code: one method in WorldBridge and one in ClientFcWorld, one call in HardcoreRespawn |
| **Alt rejected** | A veto in FcAttachSupport alone: plates, ground cover, fire, rails and the rest would keep 1.20.1's supports. A table of the 1.20.1 blocks whose answer differs: every new mapping would need a row, and FC's metadata-dependent answers (slabs, stairs, trapdoors, the saw, the planter) would be lost |
| **Known limitation** | The removed blocks FC keeps as stubs (the trapped chest 146, the weighted plates 147 and 148, the activator rail 157: FCBlockStub, a rock cube) answer true where 1.20.1 answers false, so FC's route can stand a redstone torch, plate or rail on one and snow and fire can rest on one; only `/setblock` or `/give` puts them in a world (#019). An eight-layer snow is no solid top, as 4.B has no layered snow. Within a 1.20.1 family only the members with an FC identity answer as FC: the oak trapdoor 96 against the other trapdoors, glass 20 against stained and tinted glass, ice 79 against packed and blue ice, whose stand-ins answer 1.20.1's face (follow-up FU-REL-FAMILIES, #062). ProxyBlock's display and prediction worlds, the FC entities' client world and the bonus basket's world-generation test keep their own answers. Each answer reads the cell's id twice where one face test ran before; every caller is event- or tick-driven |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, W10 solid-top fix, 2026-10-31; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, W10 solid-top fix and W10 guard batch; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bw)): P-FOUND-RT 24/24 (20/24 before: R4, R7, R8 and R15, a standing redstone torch on glowstone's or ice's top, FAIL -> PASS) and P-RANB-PLACE 117/117 `--wave W10`, GATE after OK (C11 and V25 FAIL -> PASS). Build: `Mixin config OK (77 entries ...)`, LinkAudit missing members 5 and foreign 0, BridgeAudit 0 high. Checked offline before landing: an audit of every caller of `doesBlockHaveSolidTopSurface` in the live classes, a probe of every 1.20.1 state against FC's answer, and a scan of the round-3 and regression plans (no other row changes). W10's guard batch (2026-11-03, #055): P-FOUND-RT 24/24 and P-RANB-PLACE 117/117 again, P-FOUND-RS 80/80 and P-FOUND-CARTS 80/80, the clients P-RANB-PLACE-C 13/13 and P-FOUND-RS-C 9/9; W10's gate passed |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | What can stand on what is 4.B's answer again, for every BTW rule that asks |

### #062 — Modern blocks take their BTW relative's support rules

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Modern feature** | 1.20.1's other fences (spruce, birch, jungle, acacia, dark oak, mangrove, cherry, bamboo, crimson, warped), its twenty walls besides the cobblestone and mossy cobblestone wall (brick, prismarine, red sandstone, mossy stone brick, granite, stone brick, mud brick, nether brick, andesite, red nether brick, sandstone, end stone brick, diorite, blackstone, polished blackstone brick, polished blackstone, cobbled deepslate, polished deepslate, deepslate tile, deepslate brick) and its sixteen stained glass panes: blocks FC has no identity for, which it reads through a shaped-collider stand-in (ModernBlockStandIn; the eight flammable wooden fences through a flammable one) |
| **Added** | Fences of the other woods: MC 1.8 to 1.20; the other walls: MC 1.14 to 1.19; stained glass panes: MC 1.7 |
| **Maintainer ruling** | 2026-10-31 (2): modern fences, walls and panes count as their BTW relatives for every support question, not for drops, hooks, ticks, connections, collision, rendering or identity; (1) the modern pressure plates take the oak or stone plate's rule (#055). 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-02: all ten wooden fences answer as the oak fence 85, not as BTW's fence of the same wood, the walls as the cobblestone wall 139 and the stained panes as the glass pane 102. 2026-11-02: whether a placed tool may stick into a block is a support question too, and how ground cover above them is drawn is a follow-up |
| **Conflict** | 4.B answers FC's support questions for its fence (85; the nether brick fence 113 is the same class), its cobblestone wall (139) and its glass pane (102). The stand-in answers 1.20.1's own faces instead, so the 46 modern blocks answered unlike their relatives (FC asks through six per-block methods, two world reads and one id list): an oak fence held a stake, a placed tool or a lightning rod, rested snow and ash on the ground under it and held up falling blocks, where a spruce fence did none of these, and an iron spike hung on a spruce fence's arms but not on the oak fence's; a stained pane held torches, soul torches, BTW torches, candles, chunk ore and ash, which a glass pane refuses; #055's floor families left every modern fence, wall and pane to 1.20.1's plate rule; and the server refused a standing redstone torch on a modern fence or wall (its id is not in 1.5.2's list of 85, 113, 20 and 139) while the client, which reads them as air, predicted it and shrank its stack. Once the centre hard point was the relative's, the placed tool's test (`FCItemTool.CanToolStickInBlock`, the tool's efficiency against the block's material) still read the stand-in: no tool stuck into the eight flammable wooden fences (FLAMMABLE_ROCK), while a crimson or warped fence, or any waterlogged wooden fence, took every pickaxe and the iron chisel and no axe: the reverse of 4.B's oak fence, which takes every axe |
| **Pattern** | **D** (FC-extend), a read only: a related support answers FC's support methods with its relative's FC block, on the same world and cell. Rebalance: none (4.B's answers for the relative) |
| **Implementation** | Tag `FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): support_relatives` (the table also carries #055's `attach_support_rules`). [`FcAttachSupport`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/FcAttachSupport.java)`.SUPPORT_RELATIVE` (FcAttachSupport.java:197, filled at :211-231): the ten wooden fences to the oak fence (85, FCBlockFenceWood), the twenty walls to the cobblestone wall (139, FCBlockWall), the sixteen stained panes to the glass pane (102, FCBlockPane); exact blocks, not tags. `supportRelative` (:260) answers the relative's FC block when the block is in the table, has no FC identity and FC reads the cell through a stand-in (`ModernBlockStandIn.isStandIn` of `ProxyRegistry.getWorldBlockId`, the same reading on both sides), else null; `supportRelativeId` (:280). The seams, each a read: [`ModernBlockStandIn`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/ModernBlockStandIn.java) hands its large, small and centre hard points, the ground-cover rest and the resting offset to the relative on WorldBridge reads (ModernBlockStandIn.java:437-485, `supportRelative` :494; `CanSupportFallingBlocks` follows the centre hard point; `IsNormalCube`, which also decides suffocation, push-out and path finding, stays the stand-in's and answers alike); [`ClientFcWorld`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/client/ClientFcWorld.java)`.forSupport` (ClientFcWorld.java:101), the view `ClientAttachSupport.worldFor` hands #055's prediction, reads a related block as its relative's id (:120), while `forLevel` keeps identity reads for every other client reader (FC's client beacon matches glass 20); Modern-Common `World.getSupportBlockId` (World.java:833, `getBlockId` by default) and its WorldBridge override (WorldBridge.java:227), read by 1.5.2's `BlockTorch.canPlaceTorchOn` id list (BlockTorch.java:68); #055's floor-family gate (`sameOnBothViews`, FcAttachSupport.java:442) counts a related support as read alike. Load-time [`FCItemToolStickRelativeMixin`](Forge/src/main/java/btw/forge/mixin/fcext/FCItemToolStickRelativeMixin.java) (`@Pseudo`, `remap = false`; `@ModifyArg` index 1 of the one `CanToolStickInBlock` call in `FCItemTool.onItemUse`, `require = 1`, `allow = 1`): the tool test is asked of `FcAttachSupport.supportBlock` (FcAttachSupport.java:293), the relative's FC block for a stand-in that stands for a related support on a WorldBridge; the stuck-in block, its `CanToolsStickInBlock` and the placement sound stay the stand-in's. `BridgeSelfAudit.runServerStart` calls `FcAttachSupport.audit` (BridgeSelfAudit.java:85): one INFO line with the relatives and rule twins by FC target, a WARN for an entry without an FC target or for a `minecraft:` fence, wall or stained pane with neither FC identity nor relative. Hooks: `BRIDGE_SPEC.md`, Support relatives (FcAttachSupport, ModernBlockStandIn) and Attachment support (FcAttachSupport) |
| **Effect** | Modern fences and walls answer as the oak fence and the cobblestone wall: a standing redstone torch stands on them (1.5.2's id list); stakes, placed tools and lightning rods hold on their tops and bottoms; FC's falling blocks rest on them; snow and ash that FC places rest above them over a solid ground, and grass under a snow-topped fence counts as covered; an iron spike on a fence's or wall's arm drops; stone, oak, soulforged and modern plates are refused on them, at placement and at any neighbour change. Stained glass panes answer as the glass pane: torches, soul torches, BTW torches, candles, chunk ore, iron spikes, ash and plates no longer stand on top. Every axe, and no other tool, sticks into the top or bottom of a modern wooden fence, waterlogged or not, as into the oak fence; walls take pickaxes and the iron chisel, as the cobblestone wall; panes take no tool. 1.20.1's own snow on fences and walls stays refused. The client predicts the new refusals, and a standing redstone torch on a modern fence, wall or stained pane no longer desyncs the held stack. Identity, drops, the saw, pistons, hooks, ticks, connections, collision, path finding, rendering, mining speed and harvest are unchanged |
| **BTW adds** | 4.B's support answers of the oak fence, the cobblestone wall and the glass pane on every 1.20.1 fence, wall and stained pane, for BTW's stakes, placed tools, lightning rods, spikes, torches, candles, plates, snow, ash and falling blocks |
| **Dual-sourced?** | Yes — the blocks keep their 1.20.1 identity, drops, connections, collision and rendering, and FC keeps reading them as their stand-in for everything but support questions; 1.20.1's own attachments are never granted a support 1.20.1 refuses; modded fences, walls and panes keep their stand-in's answers |
| **Data or code?** | Code: a table and four methods in FcAttachSupport, five delegating methods in ModernBlockStandIn, a support view of ClientFcWorld, a world read in Modern-Common and WorldBridge, one load-time mixin, a server-start audit line |
| **Decisions** | Exact blocks, not the 1.20.1 tags (tags are datapack data that change on `/reload` and would give any pack's fence the oak fence's answers; the server-start audit checks the table against them); a method is handed to the relative only when every caller of it asks what may rest on, hang from or stand against the block, so `IsNormalCube` is not; a support-only client view, so no other client reader sees a relative |
| **Alt rejected** | The relative's id in `ProxyRegistry.getWorldBlockId`: every FC read would see an oak fence, so drops, the saw, pistons, the dispenser, collision, fire odds and hardness would change, which the ruling excludes. `@Pseudo` mixins at each FC dispatch site: more than eight injects, and a new FC caller would be missed. For placed tools: a `@ModifyVariable` of the stuck-in block (the placement sound and `CanToolsStickInBlock` would move too), or the relative's material on the stand-in (material is identity: mining speed, harvest, fire and lava would change) |
| **Known limitation** | Ground cover above a related support is drawn where the stand-in's offset puts it, on top of the post, although FC's rules treat it as resting on the ground under the fence, and ash above an oak fence draws on the ground (ruled a follow-up, FU-REL-DRAW). Unruled families keep their stand-in's answers (follow-up FU-REL-FAMILIES): a closed modern fence gate holds FC torches, soul torches and candles on top and takes plates, where the oak gate 107 refuses both (and a redstone torch on a closed modern gate still desyncs); stained and tinted glass hold plates, snow, rails, ash and stakes on top and wall torches and ladders on their sides, which glass 20 refuses; packed and blue ice answer every hard point, the solid top and a normal cube, where ice 79 answers none, so plates, rails, stakes, the redstone torch and snow stand on them (1.20.1's snow tag already refuses packed ice); the other trapdoors, wooden and iron, hold torches, redstone torches, plates, snow, rails, ash, stakes and candles when closed in the upper half, and wall torches and ladders on their side when open, where the oak trapdoor 96 holds none (and a waterlogged one would read metadata 0); FC torches and candles stand, and FC's snow and ash rest, on oak leaves 18 but not on the modern leaves; a closed modern door holds wall torches and ladders, the oak door 64 none. The 40 wall states with no side and `up=false` (only `/setblock` makes them) have no collision and read as air on both views. BTW's own wood fences (the wood sidings' fence subtype; the port's 1093, 1095, 1097 and 1099 for woods 5-8) keep their 4.B answers, while the 1.20.1 fence of the same wood answers as the oak fence. After an update, attachments the relatives now refuse go at their next neighbour or shape change, and so do the spruce and acacia plates on the fence tables of taiga and savanna village houses, which survive generation. A BTW torch sneaked onto a stained pane beside a modern block with neither FC identity nor relative is refused by the server but not predicted by the client, which keeps its predicted stack count until the next slot sync. Modded fences, walls and panes have no relative |
| **Verified by** | Dev-server proofs (round 3, W10, 2026-11-02 and 2026-11-03; scratchpad fu_runs/BASELINE_RUNS_FU.md, W10 SUPPORT-REL, W10 STICK and W10 guard batch; `STUB_CATALOGUE.md` (bx)): the server plan P-SUPPORT-REL 100/100 (50 GAP, 36 CONTROL, 14 SANITY; 50/100 before, every GAP failing), over the stand-in reads (unchanged), the new `ranb world` support keys (centre and small hard points, ground-cover rest and offset, falling support), sneaking placements, `canSurvive` asked directly, survival at a neighbour or shape change, FC's own route and 1200 ticks of rain; P-SUPPORT-VETO amended (its C12, V16 and V18 flip as designed) 106/106; the client plan P-SUPPORT-REL-C 17/17 (7/17 before) with its probe gate GATE after OK, no player input; the placed-tool plan P-TOOLSTICK-REL 38/38 (20 GAP, 15 CONTROL, 3 SANITY; 18/38 before), its six `btwproof dig` lines (spruce, crimson and oak fence; iron axe and pickaxe) identical before and after, so mining speed and harvest are unchanged; P-USE 39/39. Log: `Support relatives: 46 supports answer as {FCBlockFenceWood 85=10, FCBlockPane 102=16, FCBlockWall 139=20}; rule twins: 14 blocks ...`, no WARN. Build: `Mixin config OK (79 entries ...)`, the refmap unchanged (48 classes: the `@Pseudo` mixin adds none), LinkAudit missing members 5, BridgeAudit 0 high. W10's gate passed (2026-11-03; #055) |
| **Net effect on FC loop** | A modern fence, wall or stained pane is as good, or as poor, a support as the BTW block it resembles, for every BTW attachment and placed tool |

### Follow-up from #005 implementation

The stonecutter pass reinforced that several planned recipe overrides —
lodestone (needs FC soulforged steel), recovery compass (needs FC
arcane scroll), conduit disable (needs FC crucible replacement in the
same sprint to avoid regression) — are **blocked on stable resource
locations for FC items**. FC's legacy ParseID→index mapping renders
each item as `betterthanwolves:item_<runtime_index>`, which shifts if
the FC config changes ParseIDs. Promoted as a blocker to the deferred
`#006` tag-remediation entry.

---

## Integration directions (2026-10-10)

Recorded from a design discussion on 2026-10-10.
Nothing here is implemented. It applies Protocols #1 and #8. Points marked
*verify* come from memory of BTW 4.B, not from the repo: check them
against the FC source before building on them.

### Critique of the current log

- Protocol #2 says gate, don't delete, but most implemented entries
  subtract: #001 Mending halved, #006 Frost Walker capped, #008 Swift
  Sneak capped, #010 Piercing capped, #011 Sweeping Edge suppressed,
  #007 respawn anchor single-use, #005 stonecutter slabs halved, #004
  loot stripped, #002 phantoms, patrols, traders and wardens switched
  off by gamerule.
- The deferred tables read the same way: "disable or nerf", "limit
  count", "goat horn locked", "reduce range".
- They ask "how do we stop X trivialising BTW?" instead of "what does
  BTW make X mean?".
- Pattern D (FC-extend) is barely used. #009 (the totem costs soul flux)
  is the one true FC-infrastructure gate.
- Wave 2 (W2) of the 2026-10-10 fix effort (triage SW5) turned drowned,
  husks and strays into plain FC zombies and skeletons, and glow squid
  into FC squid. Wither skeletons map to FC's own type-1 wither
  skeleton, existing zombie villagers stay villager zombies, and since
  2026-10-28 a village's zombie villagers keep that identity too (#048).
  It closed real gaps (these mobs ran 1.20.1 AI with no FC behaviour, and
  they triggered D2's per-tick scroll drops), but it deleted variety and
  contradicts #003, which naturalizes husk/stray/drowned.

### Principles

1. **Every modern material enters a BTW processing chain**: saw,
   millstone, turntable, kiln, crucible, cauldron, filtered hopper.
2. **Gate by BTW world progression, not by gamerules.** FC already
   tracks progress (`FCUtilsWorld.GameProgressHasNetherBeenAccessedServerOnly`,
   `...HasWitherBeenSummonedServerOnly`,
   `...HasEndDimensionBeenAccessedServerOnly`). Lighting a Nether portal
   sets the first flag (`FCBlockPortal`), and lit overworld portals
   possess nearby animals and villagers. `FCUtilsHardcoreSpawn` widens
   the random-respawn radius at each stage (×1.5 Nether, ×2 Wither, ×4
   End) and stops the shared early-game respawn once the Nether flag is
   set (ported since 2026-10-28, #054). That is the existing precedent
   for progression gating.
3. **Keep modern mob identities** and give them BTW behaviour, instead
   of collapsing them into base FC mobs.
4. **Give purposeless modern blocks BTW jobs.**

### Ideas by theme

**Death and hardcore spawn**
- BTW's random respawn (`FCUtilsHardcoreSpawn`) makes the recovery
  compass (echo shards, ancient cities), lodestones and maps genuinely
  valuable. Integrate them; don't nerf them.
- Respawn anchor as the Nether's fixed spawn, charged with soul
  material instead of glowstone (replaces #007's cap).
- Phantoms as things that hunt in the dark, tied to BTW gloom
  (`FCDamageSourceCustom.m_DamageSourceGloom`), instead of
  `doInsomnia=false` (#002).

**Souls (BTW's core theme)**
- Sculk is Mojang's own soul/death mechanic: catalysts bloom where mobs
  die. BTW captures souls with soul-sand-filtered hoppers
  (`FCTileEntityHopper`) and urns.
- Sculk as a soul source for urns; catalysts competing with soul
  hoppers; the warden guarding the richest soul source instead of
  `doWardenSpawning=false`. Soul-capture rules: *verify*.
- Soul fire as permanently "stoked" heat under the cauldron and
  crucible: a mid-game convenience earned in soul sand valleys
  (*verify* how stoked fire, `FCBlockFireStoked`, is made and decays).

**Mechanical power and redstone**
- BTW machines emit vibration game events: sculk sensors detect running
  mills, noisy industry in the deep dark summons the warden, and wool
  dampens it (a new job for BTW wool).
- Comparator outputs for FC machines: cauldron cook progress, hopper
  fill, urn souls.
- Animal power: a horse, donkey, camel or goat on a lead turns a BTW
  axle, a tier between the hand crank and the windmill. The goat's ram
  fits FC's existing `OnKickedByCow` hook (`FCEntityCow`,
  `FCEntityCreeper`).
- BTW buoyancy (items float or sink by type, `Item.GetBuoyancy`) plus
  bubble columns gives item elevators and buoyancy sorting, instead of
  "rebalance flow". Exact buoyancy rules: *verify*.
- BTW already has a lightning rod (`FCBlockLightningRod`). Copper rods
  feed the same lightning mechanics; oxidation weakens them and
  honeycomb wax (bees) preserves them.

**Materials**
- Deepslate, tuff and calcite as BTW's deeper stone strata. The seed
  exists: since 2026-10-10 `ModernBlockStandIn` reads deepslate as an
  anonymous solid stand-in (a legacy id allocated at runtime from
  159-174; 160 in the 2026-10-10 dev log); real FC stone behaviour grows
  from there. Since 2026-10-28 deepslate, cobbled deepslate and the
  deepslate ores are BTW's deep-strata stone, cobblestone and ores
  (#050); layered strata for granite, diorite, andesite, tuff and
  calcite were declined by the maintainer (2026-10-22).
- Copper melted in the crucible as an early metal.
- Mud bricks sun-dried like BTW's unfired bricks
  (`FCTileEntityUnfiredBrick` cooks in place, with rain decay).
- Decorated pots shaped on the turntable with sherds and kiln-fired,
  joining BTW's vase/urn pottery chain.
- Archaeology loot carrying BTW lore items (arcane scrolls, rare seeds).
- Cherry, mangrove, bamboo, crimson and warped wood through the saw:
  sidings, mouldings, corners, and bark for tanning. Since 2026-10-20
  cherry and mangrove, with acacia and dark oak, are FC woods (#034:
  the saw, bark and tanning), and crimson and warped stems go through
  the saw without bark. Since 2026-10-28 the four woods have their
  sidings, mouldings, corners and decorative blocks (FU-W1, #051), and
  bamboo blocks give planks through the saw and the axe, with no bark
  (#052, ruling 2026-10-21). Crimson, warped and bamboo planks as BTW
  woods were declined (2026-10-22).

**Workstations**
- Fletching table = BTW arrow bench (broadheads, composite bow).
- Stonecutter = mechanically powered stone saw that outputs BTW shapes.
- Blast furnace needs bellows or stoked heat.
- Composter feeds BTW's dung/fertilizer chain (*verify* the 4.B chain).
- Chiseled bookshelf powers the Infernal Enchanter by the books it
  holds (today `FCBlockInfernalEnchanter` counts `Block.bookShelf`
  within 8 blocks).

**Enchanting (the biggest win)**
- BTW's arcane scrolls (mob drops, used in the Infernal Enchanter)
  frame every post-1.5.2 enchantment:
  - Frost Walker from strays;
  - Soul Speed from piglins and wither skeletons;
  - Swift Sneak from wardens;
  - Riptide, Loyalty and Channeling from drowned;
  - Multishot, Piercing and Quick Charge from pillagers.
- The caps in #006, #008, #010 and #011 then become rare, earned
  top-tier enchantments instead of nerfs.

**Mobs**
- Modern identities as FC variants: drowned keep tridents; husks'
  hunger feeds BTW hunger penalties; strays keep slowness arrows; glow
  squid as BTW's night head-crab squid (`FCEntitySquid`).
- Modern animals under BTW husbandry (hunger, loose food, breeding
  harness):
  - bees pollinate hemp;
  - sniffers dig up hemp seeds (BTW drops them from grass,
    `FCBlockGrass`);
  - axolotls hunt FC squid;
  - a turtle helmet blocks squid head-crabbing;
  - cats scare FC creepers (FC creeper AI must learn that cats count
    like ocelots).
- Piglins barter BTW Nether goods; BTW nether growth spreads onto
  nylium.

**Teaching** (the "wiki-dependent" CurseForge complaint, triage CTX-3)
- An advancement tree that mirrors BTW's tech ladder, with recipe-book
  unlocks driven by it.

**Ecosystem** (brief)
- Tags (for example Forge ingot tags for soulforged steel), JSON loot
  and recipes, `IItemHandler` capabilities on BTW baskets and hoppers.
  Likely blocked on the same stable FC resource locations as the
  recipe overrides (see Follow-up from #005).

### Extension points the read-only rule requires

FC code cannot change, so integrations need these hooks in the bridge or
the planned FC-extension subproject:

| # | Extension point | Enables |
|---|-----------------|---------|
| a | Registry mapping modern blocks to FC **behaviour** (generalises `ModernBlockStandIn`) | Deepslate strata, chiseled bookshelves, copper rods, soul fire. First instance: the FC plant twins, `TwinRegistry` (#018); then log2 for the four newer woods, `ModernWoods` (#034); then the support relatives, 1.20.1's fences, walls and stained glass panes answering FC's support questions as their BTW relatives, `FcAttachSupport` (#062) |
| b | AI injection so FC mobs recognise modern entities | Cats vs creepers, axolotls vs squid, goats on `OnKickedByCow` |
| c | Machine game events + comparator outputs | Sculk sensing, warden summoning, redstone readouts |
| d | Progression-flag API over FC's `GameProgress*` flags | Replacing the #002 gamerules with progression gates |

### Fidelity layering

- The faithful core stays 1.5.2-exact. Integrations are a separate,
  labelled layer on top.
- A purist server option (core only) is reasonable, but it must not be
  the default.

### Testing

- Forge's GameTest framework is loaded with no tests: the dev logs show
  `Enabled Gametest Namespaces: []`. Use it for in-world BTW regression
  tests of integrations and the faithful core.

### Suggested first moves

1. Adopt "integrate, don't disable" as a protocol (done: Protocol #8).
2. Turn the #002 gamerule switches into progression gates.
3. Move the modern enchantments onto arcane scrolls.
4. Rework the SW5 variant collapse into FC variants.

---

## Adding a new integration — checklist

When you pilot a new feature:

- [ ] Never edit FC code (Protocol #1). Put the change in the Forge
      bridge, Modern-Common or the FC-extension subproject.
- [ ] Say what BTW adds to the feature (Protocol #8). If the entry
      disables, caps or removes anything, justify why integration was
      not possible.
- [ ] Pick an integration pattern (A–E) and justify it in one sentence.
- [ ] Write an alternative you considered and rejected (forces you to
      explore the design space).
- [ ] Prefer Forge event → loot-table override → recipe override →
      mixin, in that order.
- [ ] If mixin, include a full Javadoc block on the class with:
      Feature / Conflict / Pattern / Rebalance / Injection strategy /
      Alt rejected.
- [ ] Tag the new port code (bridge or subproject, never FC code) with
      `// FCMOD-INTEGRATION(1.20.1): <feature>`.
- [ ] Add a new row to the Integration log above.
- [ ] Rebuild and verify refmap (`grep <MixinName>
      Forge/build/tmp/compileJava/betterthanwolves.refmap.json`).
- [ ] Smoke-test in dev run.
- [ ] Smoke-test in built jar (reobfuscation catches things dev misses).

## Tracking deferred integrations

Catalogue of post-1.5.2 MC features that touch FC's design surface and are
not yet integrated. Grouped by domain. Each row carries a tentative
pattern (A–E). Items already implemented live in the integration log
above and do **not** appear here. Pattern **E** ("naturalize") rows are
listed for completeness but are batch-approvable with no code at all.
Many rows still read "nerf" or "limit". A **Direction:** note (added
2026-10-10) gives the integrate-don't-disable direction (Protocol #8),
which supersedes the row's tentative note; see
[Integration directions](#integration-directions-2026-10-10). A row with
no Direction note that disables, caps or nerfs a feature is open: it
needs an integrate-don't-disable proposal before promotion.

Promote a row out of this section by writing it up in the integration
log above with the next sequential `#NNN`.

### Combat & weapons (1.9 onward)

| # | Feature | Since | Pattern | Notes |
|---|---------|-------|---------|-------|
| — | Off-hand slot | 1.9 | D | FC inventory has no slot 40; pair with FC torch/shield use |
| — | Attack cooldown bar | 1.9 | C | FC combat assumes instant repeat; cooldown halves DPS vs FC penalty model |
| — | Shields | 1.9 | D | FC variant: requires hide + iron, blocks % based on stamina |
| — | Tipped arrows / lingering potions | 1.9 | C | Lingering only craftable if FC potion stand |
| — | Trident + Riptide/Loyalty/Channeling | 1.13 | B | Gate behind drowned drop in FC nether/wild loot. **Direction:** drowned keep tridents as an FC variant; Riptide/Loyalty/Channeling come from drowned arcane scrolls |
| — | Smithing-template netherite upgrade | 1.20 | A or B | Re-tier netherite under Soulforged, or gate behind soulforge |
| — | Mace + Wind Charge | 1.21 | — | Future port only |
